## Strategy E: Market-neutral narrative-divergence pairs

### Thesis

Within a single GICS industry group, AI's cross-document synthesis (1.1, 1.4, 1.10) can identify two companies L (laggard) and S (leader) where the narrative divergence in public disclosures and analyst reaction patterns has gotten ahead of the fundamental divergence. Long L / short S captures mean reversion without net market exposure, compensating 2.7 (regime maladaptation) and 2.20 (textbook-rational penalty) — market direction doesn't matter for the pair's returns.

### Explicit confrontation of disadvantage 2.6 (no access to private information)

Per `Experiment_Parameters.md` pre-mortem requirement: this is the load-bearing constraint for E and is confronted explicitly here (not only in the pre-mortem section).

**E's universe excludes any name where the narrative divergence thesis depends on:**

- Expert network calls or consultations
- In-person management access or private investor meetings
- Conference attendance that reveals non-public context (sell-side conferences included)
- Buy-side intelligence or fund-manager relationships
- Industry contacts, supplier or customer channel checks, or private competitive intelligence
- Any information whose provenance cannot be traced to a specific public document or public news release

**During thesis construction, if reasoning requires any phrase resembling "per industry contacts," "sources familiar with," "per channel checks," "management has indicated privately," or any equivalent — the thesis is not tradeable in E. The session must abandon the thesis and move to the next candidate.**

All thesis construction must be fully auditable against: 10-K and 10-Q filings, 8-K announcements, earnings call transcripts, public analyst reports (full text), press releases, publicly available news, and publicly available industry data (including subscription-available sector data if the subscription is broadly accessible at retail tier).

### Instrument eligibility rule

A pair consists of:

- Long position in company L
- Short position in company S
- Both in the same GICS **industry group** (6-digit GICS level, tighter than sector — GICS sectors would allow pairs like "two software companies" which may have minimal correlation)
- Both legs executable within IBKR minimum-share-size constraints at the pair's AI-chosen risk budget (**Rev 43, owner directive, 2026-07-28** — formerly "2% of strategy portfolio per leg")

Per `Experiment_Parameters.md` rev 19 §Position size, a pair is sized as **one thesis**: the AI sets one combined CaR budget with no numeric ceiling and sizes the legs to the hedge ratio. The budget is seven-factor justified and adversarially attacked on size. Each leg is one position for the 30-trade gate count; a pair equals two trades.

**ETF pair substitution:** If individual-stock shorting is not feasible at the current strategy portfolio size (typical issue when S's share price × minimum share count × ~~2% cap~~ **[Rev 43, owner directive, 2026-07-28 — the pair's AI-chosen Capital-at-Risk budget; a pair is sized as ONE thesis, not per leg. ~~bounded by the ≤ 10% per-name / ≤ 75% per-strategy envelopes~~ — owner directive 2026-08-05 retired both envelopes, so the pair budget has no numeric ceiling]** interact unfavorably), the pair may use same-industry-group ETFs as substitute legs. ETF-pair execution dilutes the idiosyncratic thesis (ETFs carry many constituents) but preserves the market-neutral structure. This substitution is a documented accepted cost when portfolio size does not support individual-stock pair execution. As portfolio size grows past that threshold, individual-stock pairs become the primary construction without any rule change needed.

### Entry criteria

1. Narrative divergence thesis identified: specific public-information basis for why L is under-narrated relative to S, with explicit predictions about what public events would cause reconvergence
2. Adversarial counter-argument doesn't identify a decisive flaw
3. Classical-method delegation produces: L–S correlation over trailing 252 trading days ≥ 0.5 (pairs with lower correlation are not pairs — they are two independent bets); beta-adjusted leg sizing if L and S have materially different volatilities
4. Expected holding period to thesis resolution is 1–6 months
5. Short-financing cost for the S leg (borrow rate × position size × expected holding period) is computed by code and is ≤ 15% of thesis expected return — otherwise financing eats the alpha

### Exit rules and thesis invalidation

**Exit if any of:**

- Convergence target reached: the specific public indicators defined at entry as "thesis playing out" are met (e.g., S misses earnings consensus by > X% while L meets/beats)
- Thesis invalidation: new public information changes the fundamental situation on either leg (management change, material operational event, M&A announcement, regulatory action)
- Time-based exit at 6 months from entry: thesis is stale; short-financing costs accumulating; exit regardless of P&L
- Correlation breakdown: rolling 60-day correlation between L and S falls below 0.3 (pair relationship has broken; thesis is no longer market-neutral)
- **Short-leg stop — MANDATORY, distance AI-determined per pair at entry (Rev 45, owner directive, 2026-08-18).** Close the pair if S rises from its short-entry price by at least the **stop distance fixed for this pair at entry**. `Experiment_Parameters.md` §Position size makes a stop mandatory on any short leg because it is the only thing that bounds short Capital at Risk — *"without it CaR is unbounded and the phrase 'risk is capped by sizing' would be false"* — and this rule is what makes E's short-leg CaR an enforced number rather than an assumption. **Before this revision E had no stop of any kind anywhere in its machinery while every document that sized an E pair assumed a mandatory +25% one; that gap was found by D2 on 2026-08-18 (`events.decision_log` ops-note `5f65959b-d259-47e7-966e-e0b61f9ccbde`) at E's first thesis to reach the sizing step, and closed here.**

  **The distance is NOT a fixed constant, and deliberately so (owner directive: "no fixed numbers, it should be determined by the AI so it can leverage context of current market conditions").** It is judgment-native machinery in the sense of `Claude_Task_Plan.md` SL2 (A) 1b: chosen fresh for each pair during thesis construction from that pair's own measured conditions — S's realized and implied volatility, the pair's trailing-252-day spread distribution and where the entry sits in it, the 252-day correlation and the beta-derived hedge ratio, borrow cost and expected holding period, and the prevailing SPY-trend / VIX / breadth regime — so that a low-volatility utilities pair and a high-beta semiconductor pair do not inherit the same bound from a constant that was never derived for either. Strategy B's `SHORT_STOP_LOSS_PCT = 0.25` is B's own machinery and is **not** imported here: B's 25% was derived against B's post-event-drift horizon, and importing it would license an E pair to carry a bound nothing about E produced.

  **Three rails, none waivable:**
  1. **Set at entry, BEFORE sizing.** The distance is chosen and written down before the CaR arithmetic that consumes it, because short-leg CaR *is* `short notional × that distance` and E's pair budget is the sum of both legs' CaR. A pair whose distance has not been set cannot be sized, and therefore cannot be entered. This ordering is the whole point: a distance chosen after the size is a rationalisation of the size.
  2. **Frozen for the life of the position — it may tighten, never widen.** Re-deriving the distance after an adverse move is exactly the criteria-drift-under-loss-pressure failure that judgment-native machinery requirement (d) names, and it would retroactively unbound a CaR figure already recorded and already justified. A tightening is permitted only as part of a full pair exit.
  3. **Recorded with its justification.** Write the chosen distance and the reasoning into the thesis's `events.decision_log` entry (`fields.short_stop_pct`, `fields.short_stop_rationale`), under the same discipline as the seven-factor CaR justification and subject to the same mandatory adversarial attack on size. An unrecorded distance is treated as unset — see rail 1.

  **The stop closes the PAIR, not the short leg alone.** When it fires, cover S and sell L in the same session, for the reason the Partial exits paragraph below already gives: a lone surviving leg is a naked directional position and defeats the market-neutral structure that is this strategy's entire thesis.

**Not exit-triggering:**

- Adverse mark-to-market on the pair with no thesis-invalidating news
- General market moves (pair should be approximately neutral to these by construction)

### Partial exits (trim / scale-out)

A partial trim is allowed only by reducing both pair legs together, proportionally, in the same session — a market SELL on part of the long L leg paired with a market partial-COVER on part of the short S leg, sized to preserve the pair's hedge ratio (Rev 41, owner directive, 2026-07-22). Trimming one leg alone is never permitted: it would leave a naked, unhedged leg and defeat the market-neutral pair structure. This directive does not change the fact that E's fills-derived campaign/lot accounting is long-only-correct only — a pre-existing limitation, unrelated to and unchanged by this directive.

### Declared expected frequency

6–12 pair theses per year over active periods, equaling 12–24 trades counted toward the 30-trade gate (two positions per pair).

### Classical-method delegation

- Correlation computation (L vs. S, trailing 252 trading days, rolling 60 days)
- Beta adjustment for leg sizing (regression L returns on S returns, use hedge ratio)
- P&L attribution per leg (verify thesis is playing out in the expected direction on each side)
- Short financing cost estimation using broker's current rate
- GICS industry group classification lookup

### Router activation rule

**Technical:** SPY Trend State ≠ DOWN AND VIX Regime ≠ HIGH AND Breadth State = HEALTHY

Rationale: In DOWN regimes and HIGH VIX regimes, within-sector correlations collapse toward 1 (everything moves together in fear) or toward 0 (everything unmoored). Either extreme breaks pair mechanics. In UP or NEUTRAL regimes with LOW or NORMAL VIX, sector-level correlations are in the 0.5–0.8 band where pair trades have room to express divergence.

The additional HEALTHY breadth requirement (tightening from the prior B-identical rule) reflects that E runs market-neutral within-industry pairs, which require cross-sectional dispersion inside each sector to generate convergence edge. Narrow markets (breadth < 50%) correspond to concentrated participation where leadership clusters in a small number of names; within-industry pair spreads compress because the long-leaders outrun the laggards in the same factor regime. B, which exploits 2–8 week post-event overshoots in specific names regardless of breadth, does not need this additional constraint.

**Fundamental question:** Is the current sector-rotation environment conducive to within-industry-group mean reversion, or are macro forces dominating intra-sector dynamics?

## Pre-mortems

Per `Experiment_Parameters.md`, a completed pre-mortem is required before any strategy's first trade: one per strategy plus one one-time pre-mortem for the router. *(rev 2026-07-10 — Strategy Arsenal autonomy conversion, owner directive.)* N is no longer a fixed count — it is the current roster size (`state.strategy_roster`). Adding a strategy through the Strategy Arsenal Lifecycle increments N and requires its own fully-cycled pre-mortem (authored by SL2, reviewed by the Attacker + Orchestrator routine pair) before that strategy may leave UNDER_REVIEW for SHADOW; each pre-mortem is scoped to its own strategy at its own entry rather than to a founding N+1 cohort completed before a single shared first trade. Each is reviewed adversarially under the routine architecture (Adversarial Review Attacker + Orchestrator routines per `Claude_Task_Plan.md`), and revised if material weakness is found. The founding-cohort pre-mortems below are historical artifacts of the pre-launch reviews completed under the prior incognito-session + paste-back workflow; newcomer pre-mortems are authored and reviewed autonomously under the same protocol.

**SUPERSESSION BANNER — the 2% position-size cap, all five pre-mortems (Rev 43, owner directive, 2026-07-28).** Every pre-mortem below was accepted while the fixed 2%-of-strategy-portfolio position size was in force, and each one **load-bears on it**: each cites the 2% cap in its cross-constraint flowing limitation as *magnitude-only* mitigation for a confluence of disadvantages (A: 2.4/2.13/2.14/2.19 · B: 2.4/2.13/2.14/2.15/2.20 · C: 2.18 plus 2.1/2.4/2.13/2.15 · D: 2.4/2.8/2.13/2.15/2.17/2.19 · E: seven of its fourteen cited disadvantages). **Rev 43 retired that cap** in favour of thesis-scaled risk budgeting (`Experiment_Parameters.md` rev 18). Consequences, stated explicitly so nothing below is read as operative-but-false:

1. **Every "2% cap" and 10%/75% hard-envelope reference in the pre-mortems below is SUPERSEDED.** Rev 19 retired all numeric sizing ceilings. The current discipline is the AI-chosen per-thesis risk budget, with a recorded seven-factor justification and mandatory adversarial attack on size. Where historical text is retained, it must be explicitly struck or annotated; it is not an operative bound.
2. **The confluence-list mitigation claims are NOT re-derived here, and are stale until re-opened.** Whether a variable, judgment-set budget still delivers the same magnitude-only mitigation for those disadvantages is exactly the question an adversarial cycle exists to answer — it is not something to assert by editing the accepted text. **Annotation is not re-opening.** Per `AI_Trading_Foundation.md` §5.2 step 3 all five pre-mortems are **re-opened**; A3 enqueues them.
3. **Read the pre-mortems below as historical accepted artifacts on this point**, not as a current description of the sizing control. The canonical current specification is `Experiment_Parameters.md` §Position size.

**Terminology bridge — routine architecture migration.** Pre-mortem text below predates the routine architecture migration documented in `Decision_Log.md`. Historical references map to current architecture as follows:
- "sub-step A" / "Sub-step A" → M1a routine (strategy-blind regime scoring)
- "sub-step B" / "Sub-step B" → M1b routine (strategy-mapping)
- "Session 1" (in divergence-review context) → M1b output file (already produced; non-adversarial input)
- "Session 2" / "Attacker (incognito)" → Attacker routine (BigQuery transcript keyed by review id, cycle number, and role)
- "Session 3" / "Judge (incognito)" → Orchestrator routine (reads that exact current attacker row)
- "Session 4" / "Theater auditor (incognito)" → theater-check flag emitted by the Orchestrator routine; the standalone theater-auditor stage was not preserved in migration scope (orchestrator self-certifies). See migration entry for accepted-risk rationale and the option to add a separate Theater Auditor routine in a future revision if self-certification proves inadequate.
- "incognito session" (generally) → routine with fresh context and prompt-discipline file-read scoping

The pre-mortem text below is preserved as historical record. Operational behavior follows current routine architecture per `Claude_Task_Plan.md` and the relevant section of this document.

### Pre-mortem: Regime router

**Revision:** 5 (2026-04-23), following four successive adversarial review cycles. Rev 1: MATERIAL WEAKNESS. Rev 2: MATERIAL WEAKNESS (CONVERGENT theater-check). Rev 3: TIER 1 DEFECT — REVISION REQUIRED (MIXED theater-check). Rev 4: TIER 1 DEFECT — REVISION REQUIRED (DIVERGENT theater-check; 2 items surfaced, both attributable to rev 4's own edits). Rev 5 applies a spot-edit fix to those 2 items under the revision-churn cap in Experiment_Parameters.md rev 13 — no cycle 5 is run because every cycle-4 Tier 1 item was a propagation error or scope-lock gap introduced by rev 4's edits rather than a residual original-architecture defect. Tier 2/3 items from all prior cycles remain in "Known limitations" (Section 12).

**Self-containment statement:** This pre-mortem enumerates within its own text every item on the requirements list (regime states, technical indicators with thresholds, fundamental template with inputs, misclassification scenarios, failure modes, failure indicators). A reader with no access to other documents should be able to verify requirement satisfaction directly from this document. Cross-references to `Experiment_Parameters.md` for experiment-level triggers (foundation-change) are reproduced in Section 8 to satisfy this rule for incognito-review readers.

#### 1. Regime states the router attempts to classify

The router does not attempt to classify a single global regime label, but it does place the market into one of an enumerated set of composite regime archetypes via the five technical indicators below. Each strategy's activation rule implicitly bets that some archetypes favor its mechanism and others do not.

The archetypes (combinations of the 5 indicators that produce materially different activation patterns — each archetype is fully expressible by the 5 mechanical indicators, no trending / no subjective subdimensions):

1. **Clean bull.** SPY Trend = UP, Breadth = HEALTHY, VIX Regime = NORMAL or LOW, Yield Curve NORMAL, Sustained Inversion Flag = NOT-SUSTAINED. All five strategies technically activate.
2. **Narrow bull.** SPY Trend = UP, Breadth = WEAK (< 50% above 200dma), VIX NORMAL. Strategy A deactivates (needs HEALTHY breadth); B, C, D activate; E deactivates (tightened rule also needs HEALTHY breadth). Note: this archetype subsumes the prior rev 3 archetype 8 ("slow-burn deterioration with VIX trending up but still NORMAL"). VIX-trending is not among the five mechanical indicators, so the mechanism cannot distinguish the two within the indicator set; the slow-burn-deterioration scenario is instead handled in Section 6 as a misclassification risk.
3. **Sideways / transitional.** SPY Trend = NEUTRAL (SPY > 50dma OR 50dma > 200dma, not both), VIX NORMAL. A deactivates; B, C, D activate subject to other conditions; E conditional on breadth.
4. **Late-cycle.** SPY Trend = UP or NEUTRAL, Yield Curve SUSTAINED Inversion = SUSTAINED (≥18 consecutive months inverted). D deactivates; others may activate.
5. **Volatility shock on otherwise benign trend.** SPY Trend ≠ DOWN, VIX Regime = HIGH (> 25). B and E deactivate; C activates (only needs SPY ≠ DOWN); D evaluates Sustained Inversion separately; A evaluates breadth separately.
6. **Confirmed bear.** SPY Trend = DOWN (SPY < 50dma AND 50dma < 200dma). All five strategies technically deactivate.
7. **Volatile bear.** SPY Trend = DOWN, VIX HIGH. All deactivate; the regime the router exists most obviously to avoid.
8. **Liquidity event / flash crash.** SPY Trend crosses to DOWN briefly, VIX spikes to HIGH, then reverses within days. Router flips twice; strategies deactivate and reactivate. Transient misclassification cost, not a regime.

The router's job is to produce different activation vectors across these archetypes. The structural bet is that the mechanical technical indicators are reasonable proxies for the archetypes and that the fundamental template adds meaningful disagreement value in the boundary cases.

**Sustained Inversion Flag reversion rule.** Once SUSTAINED, the flag reverts to NOT-SUSTAINED when the Yield Curve State has been NORMAL (10Y > 2Y) continuously for ≥ 30 consecutive trading days. This prevents a single-day un-inversion from flipping the flag and re-activating D during a still-late-cycle regime.

#### 2. Technical indicators, thresholds, and justifications

All five indicators are defined in "Shared regime vocabulary" above and are mechanical — no AI classification; computed from public end-of-day price/volume data.

**Threshold immutability (per-strategy — rev 2026-07-10, Strategy Arsenal autonomy conversion, owner directive).** Every shared regime-vocabulary threshold and every existing strategy's committed thresholds are immutable for that strategy's life and stay frozen; re-tuning one is prohibited and constitutes that strategy's termination (terminate-and-restart-as-a-materially-different-new-strategy), per `Experiment_Parameters.md`. A newcomer adopted through the Strategy Arsenal Lifecycle may ADD its own single-strategy mechanical measurement (precedent: D's Sustained-Inversion Flag) without touching any existing threshold — that additive append is not a revision. What remains prohibited is quiet re-tuning of an in-force threshold: the parameter-fishing guard is unchanged.

**Threshold justifications:**

- *SPY Trend (50-day / 200-day SMA pairing):* Standard industry practice for trend identification, documented in Faber's tactical asset allocation literature. The specific 50/200 choice over alternatives (20/100, 21-EMA/55-EMA) is a free parameter; stress-testing at monthly review compares realized strategy performance under the counterfactual 20/100 split. If the counterfactual materially outperforms over 24+ months, log as threshold-sensitivity risk — does not trigger mid-experiment revision.
- *VIX 15/25 bands:* VIX < 15 corresponds approximately to the bottom tercile of its 1990–2025 distribution; VIX > 25 to the top quintile. Defensible as "comfortably out-of-range" markers. Also a free parameter; stress-tested analogously.
- *Yield Curve (10Y > 2Y):* Standard recession-signaling curve measure per NY Fed research. Sign-based; non-arbitrary.
- *Sustained Inversion 18-month window:* Historical median lead time from inversion onset to NBER-dated recession is approximately 17–26 months depending on cycle (1989: 17; 2000: 15; 2007: 23; 2020: 9; 2022–24: 26). 18 months is approximately the median. Free parameter; stress-test counterfactuals at 12 and 24 months.
- *Breadth 50% cutoff:* Standard "narrow market" threshold in institutional practice. Free parameter; stress-test at 40% and 60%.

**Authoritative data sources.** SPY price: consolidated tape close. Moving averages: computed by the participant from daily closes; never recalled from memory. VIX: CBOE close. Treasury yields: FRED constant-maturity series. Breadth: participant-computed from S&P 500 constituent list and daily closes, OR from a single pre-declared third-party source (e.g., MacroMicro) with the specific source URL recorded; if that source becomes unavailable, the indicator falls back to participant computation with documentation of the transition.

#### 3. Per-strategy technical activation rules

Specified in each strategy's section; restated here for self-containment:

- Strategy A: ACTIVATE if SPY Trend = UP AND Breadth = HEALTHY.
- Strategy B: ACTIVATE if SPY Trend ≠ DOWN AND VIX Regime ≠ HIGH.
- Strategy C: ACTIVATE if SPY Trend ≠ DOWN.
- Strategy D: ACTIVATE if (SPY Trend = UP OR NEUTRAL) AND Sustained Inversion Flag = NOT-SUSTAINED.
- Strategy E: ACTIVATE if SPY Trend ≠ DOWN AND VIX Regime ≠ HIGH AND Breadth = HEALTHY.

B and E are differentiated on the Breadth requirement (tightening from a prior-revision duplication). Rationale in Strategy E's section.

#### 4. Fundamental template (summary)

Full specification is in "Fundamental analysis template (monthly)" above; restated here for self-containment:

- **Session architecture (rev 3 fix):** two architecturally-separate incognito sessions. Sub-step A (strategy-blind regime scoring) runs in its own incognito session on the 6 input categories; its output is the structured regime scoring. Sub-step B (strategy-mapping) runs in a fresh incognito session that receives ONLY sub-step A's regime scoring output (not the underlying inputs), and maps to each strategy's fundamental question. Separation is architectural rather than instructional; resolves rev 2's "single-session blinding does not blind" defect.
- **Reconciliation rules (rev 3 addition):** mechanical checks applied after sub-step B. If sub-step A scores `shock_overlay = acute` OR sub-step A scores `risk_sentiment = stressed` (for A/D) OR sub-step A scores `growth_momentum = decelerating` AND `policy_stance = hawkish` (for A) OR sub-step A scores `inflation_trend = reaccelerating` AND `policy_stance = hawkish` (for D), sub-step B ACTIVATE calls for the affected strategies are overridden to DO-NOT-ACTIVATE. Contradictions logged for monthly review.
- **Inputs:** 6 categories — macro releases (BLS/BEA/Census, initial prints with 3-month AND 12-month windows); Fed/FOMC (Fed primary sources, dot-plot drift over 12 months); earnings aggregate (FactSet or named equivalent); geopolitical (structured criteria: trade flows / oil > $5/bbl / sovereign spreads / major currency > 2%); policy (primary-source citations); cross-cycle comparison (contextual only, not a 2.14 mitigation per rev 3 honest-scope correction).
- **Fallback protocol:** no silent source substitution. If ≥2 of the 5 primary input categories are absent at session time, fundamental call defaults to DO-NOT-ACTIVATE across all strategies and sub-step B is not run that month.

#### 5. Four-session router divergence review

Full specification is in "Router divergence review" above; restated for self-containment:

- **Session 1** (fundamental author, non-adversarial): the sub-step B output that produced the disagreement.
- **Session 2** (attacker, incognito): argues for the technical call, attacks the fundamental call.
- **Session 3** (judge, incognito): provisional activation state; does NOT produce a theater-check flag.
- **Session 4** (theater auditor, incognito — rev 3 addition): given only the outputs of Sessions 1–3, assesses whether the three prior sessions produced genuinely divergent reasoning. Produces CONVERGENT / DIVERGENT / MIXED flag. Architecturally separated from Session 3 to eliminate judge self-certification (rev 2 defect).
- **Decision rule:** Session 3's verdict binds IF Session 4 flags DIVERGENT or MIXED. If Session 4 flags CONVERGENT, default DO-NOT-ACTIVATE regardless of Session 3. Additional ambiguity in Session 3's output (non-committal, conditional on future evidence) also defaults to DO-NOT-ACTIVATE.
- **Logging:** all four session outputs and the Session 4 flag recorded in `Decision_Log.md`.

#### 6. Misclassification scenarios (with real-time checkability)

1. **Regime transitions with lagging technicals (including slow-burn deterioration).** Technical indicators compute from trailing windows; genuine transitions take weeks to show. The specific sub-case of "slow-burn deterioration" — SPY Trend = UP mechanically, Breadth = WEAK, VIX elevated within NORMAL but trending upward — is a market-top-in-formation pattern the five mechanical indicators cannot distinguish from archetype 2 (narrow bull). This is the highest-risk misclassification scenario. *Checkable in real time?* Partially — Breadth typically leads the 50/200 cross by weeks, and a VIX drift within the NORMAL band (e.g., 15 → 23 over 30 days) is observable even if not mechanically classified. *Operational response:* monthly review explicitly compares Breadth trajectory to SPY Trend and tracks VIX drift inside the NORMAL band; any breadth deterioration > 10 points month-over-month OR VIX drift > 5 points within NORMAL over 30 days is flagged as transition candidate. Flag triggers an out-of-cycle fundamental refresh per scenario 3.
2. **Novel regimes without historical analogue.** Cross-cycle input (fundamental template input #6) asks for an analogue and accepts "no close analogue exists" as an answer. *Checkable:* Yes. *Response:* any month where #6 returns "no analogue" is flagged.
3. **Information shocks between monthly updates.** Mid-month shocks move technical signals but do not refresh fundamental. *Checkable:* Yes. *Response:* out-of-cycle fundamental refresh is permitted and required when any one of: VIX crosses NORMAL→HIGH, SPY Trend flips to DOWN, or a geopolitical event meeting the structured criteria occurs. Out-of-cycle session flagged in logs. **Re-risking limb (added 2026-08-24).** Every trigger above — here and in scenario 1 — is a deterioration trigger; until this revision there was no symmetric path back once a strategy gated OFF mid-month, only the next scheduled monthly M1a/M1b cycle. D2's 2026-08-13 router review recorded this asymmetry explicitly: every trigger on the list is a deterioration trigger, five-for-five unmet, three unmet specifically in the risk-REDUCING direction. An out-of-cycle fundamental refresh is now ALSO permitted and required when ALL THREE of the following hold together: (a) a strategy has stood at DO-NOT-ACTIVATE for at least 15 consecutive trading days — counted in `state.market_calendar` trading days from the START of that strategy's current unbroken DO-NOT-ACTIVATE run, where the run start is the earliest `as_of_date` such that every `events.regime_events` row (scope `STRATEGY_ACTIVATION`, one row per `as_of_date`, latest row per day) from that date through the latest carries DO-NOT-ACTIVATE. A re-affirmation — a later row restating the state the strategy was already in — EXTENDS the run; it does NOT reset the clock. **Rev 48 (2026-09-05) corrects this clause**: Rev 46 measured the dwell as trading days elapsed since the LATEST row's `as_of_date`, which let any no-change re-affirmation zero the counter — measured 2026-09-05, that literal reading returned 1 trading day for every strategy on the roster, against true unbroken-run lengths of 22 trading days for two of them (and a left-censored lower bound of 94 for a third), so the limb was effectively unreachable and the asymmetry Rev 46 set out to close was still open. The run is computed for every active strategy by `state.rerisking_limb_status`, so the dwell condition is measurable rather than asserted — and leg (b) below now has a dated, measured anchor to test against, since `## Regime scoring (strategy-blind, monthly)`'s `### Shock / overlay grading rubric` (added the same revision) requires every scoring of that axis to record the date of the most recent qualifying event and the baseline / peak / current Brent triplet; (b) the specific reconciliation-override axis producing that DO-NOT-ACTIVATE call — one of the reconciliation rules governing acute shock overlay, stressed risk sentiment, growth+policy, or inflation+policy — has a dated, MEASURED reversal in its own primary inputs, not a narrative re-reading; and (c) the technical plane is simultaneously benign: VIX Regime ≠ HIGH AND SPY Trend ≠ DOWN AND Breadth = HEALTHY. This is **deliberately conjunctive where the de-risking triggers above are disjunctive** — any ONE deterioration trigger fires a refresh, but ALL THREE re-risking conditions must hold together — so the router stays fast to protect and slow to re-engage. This limb triggers a **REFRESH, never a flip**: it re-runs the M1a/M1b machinery, whose output is then reconciled and, on technical-vs-fundamental disagreement, adjudicated by the normal divergence review (Section 5 above). It does NOT authorise D2 or any daily routine to score a fundamental axis itself; the M1a/M1b strategy-blind/strategy-aware split is unchanged.
4. **Synchronized AI-consensus regime calls (per 2.8).** Many participants running similar AI models converge on the same call, creating crowded positioning. *Checkable?* Partially. *Response:* monthly crowding diagnostic — sub-step A explicitly prompted to identify 2–3 widely-held positions the current AI consensus supports, and to flag divergence opportunities rather than endorse consensus. Residual risk remains.
5. **Narrative over-fit on the fundamental side (per 2.4).** Fundamental produces plausible but predictively-weak regime stories. *Checkable?* Imperfectly, via Session 4 theater-check flags on divergence reviews and the realized-return-vs-router-classification indicator in Section 9.

#### 7. Router performance measurement (rev 3 — counterfactual metric dropped)

The prior revision specified a router-contribution metric = actual portfolio TWR − all-active counterfactual TWR. Rev 2 adversarial review identified that this metric is path-dependent on the router's own prior decisions: "positions in deactivated strategies treated as held at most recent actual state" is a book conditional on past router choices, not a neutral baseline for measuring router contribution. The metric was mathematically incoherent for its stated purpose.

Rev 3 fix: the router-contribution metric is removed. There is no tractable way to construct an all-active counterfactual that is not path-dependent on past router decisions; any simulation of "what positions strategies would have held during deactivation periods" would itself be conditional on a chosen policy for taking trades during those periods, and that policy is not specified anywhere in the experiment.

**Retained measurement:**
- **All-SGOV benchmark.** What the capital would have returned if held entirely in SGOV. This is the legitimate external benchmark and already exists per `Experiment_Parameters.md`'s success threshold. The router's value is measured indirectly through each strategy's excess-of-SGOV TWR, computed per-strategy in Indicator 9.1.

**Router's value is not separately measured.** The experiment measures each strategy against SGOV; sustained strategy underperformance-vs-SGOV during ACTIVATE periods (Indicator 9.1) indicates the router is not adding value for that strategy. This is the only defensible router-performance signal available.

#### 8. Failure modes (detection / mitigation / residual risk)

**Foundation-change trigger reproduced here for self-containment.** Per `Experiment_Parameters.md`: each month, `AI_Trading_Foundation.md` Part 4 questions are re-answered. If a documented edge is materially reduced, a documented disadvantage is materially added or increased, or the monthly verification produces findings that change the edge map, each strategy is assessed independently. Strategies whose exploited edges or compensated disadvantages are materially affected terminate. The default on "materially affected?" is bias-toward-termination.

**2.7 (regime maladaptation).** Central risk; the router exists to compensate.
- *Detection:* Section 9's realized-return-vs-router-classification indicator (9.1).
- *Mitigation:* Technical half is mechanical, immune to training-distribution drift at the classification level. Fundamental half runs two-session architecturally-blinded scoring (sub-step A session isolated from strategy-specific questions).
- *Residual:* The fundamental half remains a single-model artifact. If the underlying model has systemic maladaptation bias, the router cannot self-detect. The foundation-change trigger (reproduced above) is the external check.

**2.4 (narrative over-fit).**
- *Detection:* Session 4 theater-check flags on divergence reviews; aggregated monthly.
- *Mitigation:* Closed input list; structured geopolitical criteria replace open-ended language; two-session architectural blinding prevents strategy-question priming of regime scoring.
- *Residual:* A structured template does not prevent plausible-but-wrong synthesis within it.

**2.14 (recency bias).**
- *Detection:* Monthly review checks each template input for 12-month-window compliance and cross-cycle-input presence.
- *Mitigation (honest scope correction from rev 2):* 12-month trends alongside 3-month in the input specification. The cross-cycle analogue input was previously cited as a mitigation; rev 3 removes that claim because the analogue is generated by the same model it is meant to de-bias (circular). The cross-cycle input is now contextual only.
- *Residual:* AI weight-level recency bias persists regardless of prompt structure and is not meaningfully mitigated at the router level. Acknowledged.

**2.24 (cross-session inconsistency).**
- *Detection:* Session 4 theater-check flag on every divergence review.
- *Mitigation:* Deliberately exploited as edge via the four-session architecture (rev 3) with architectural separation of theater-audit from judge.
- *Residual:* All four sessions run on the same model weights; weight-level biases are correlated. CONVERGENT theater-check rate above base rate signals dominance. The stopping rule in `Experiment_Parameters.md` rev 12 explicitly acknowledges this saturation and bounds the number of review cycles.

**2.8 (homogenization and correlated-execution risk).**
- *Detection:* Monthly crowding diagnostic (Section 6.4); anti-predictive router indicator in Section 9.
- *Mitigation:* Fundamental session explicitly prompted to flag (not endorse) AI-consensus positions; primary-source requirements replace aggregator content.
- *Residual:* 2.8 affects the technical layer too, not only the fundamental side. If a large fraction of AUM is driven by AI operators using similar technical signals, SPY/breadth/VIX measurements themselves are partially formed by AI-consensus behavior. Not fixable at router level; flagged as systemic experiment-level risk.

#### 9. Dated failure indicators for monthly review

Each indicator has an explicit threshold, measurement window, and minimum denominator where applicable.

**9.1 Realized-return-vs-router-classification.**
- Window: trailing 12 calendar months.
- Measurement: per strategy, total TWR during ACTIVATE months minus total TWR during DEACTIVATE months, computed on excess-of-SGOV basis.
- Fires if: excess-of-SGOV return during ACTIVATE months ≤ −5 pp cumulative OR lower than excess-of-SGOV return during DEACTIVATE months, for any strategy, over the window.
- Minimum denominator: ≥ 3 months of each state. Below denominator, report raw values without firing; log "un-fireable due to insufficient regime variety."

**9.2 Theater-check flag rate.**
- Window: trailing 3 calendar months.
- Measurement: proportion of divergence-review Session 4 outputs flagged CONVERGENT.
- Fires if: CONVERGENT rate ≥ 50% AND total divergence reviews in window ≥ 6. Below denominator, report raw counts only. Known limitation: denominator may be unreachable in benign regimes (see Known Limitations #2).

**9.3 Signal-redundancy (rev 3 — calibration language removed).**
- Ex ante disagreement expectation (committed at inception, not calibrated): in regimes with meaningful variety, at least one of the five strategies is expected to show technical/fundamental disagreement in any given month.
- Window: trailing 6 calendar months.
- Fires if: zero divergence reviews triggered across all 5 strategies in the window AND ≥ 2 of the 8 regime archetypes from Section 1 occurred during the window. The second clause prevents firing during uniformly-benign periods where agreement is expected.

**9.4 Chronic default-deny bias.**
- Window: trailing 12 calendar months.
- Measurement: proportion of divergence reviews resolved as DO-NOT-ACTIVATE via default (Session 4 CONVERGENT tiebreaker, non-committal Session 3 output, or conditional Session 3 output) vs affirmative DO-NOT-ACTIVATE ruling from Session 3 with DIVERGENT or MIXED Session 4.
- Denominator carve-out (rev 4): fallback-suppressed months per the fundamental template's Fallback interaction rule are excluded from both numerator and denominator. Those months produce DO-NOT-ACTIVATE for all strategies without a divergence review occurring, and counting them in 9.4 would conflate data-availability failures with judge-ambiguity bias. Fallback months are tracked separately as a monthly review data-availability diagnostic.
- Fires if: ≥ 40% of resolutions fell to ambiguity default, with minimum denominator ≥ 5 reviews (non-fallback). Threshold acknowledged as uncalibrated per Known Limitations #4.

**9.5 Regime-transition-count (adversarial-testing diagnostic).**
- Window: trailing 12 calendar months.
- Measurement: count of days in the window on which any of the 5 technical indicators changed state from the prior trading day.
- Fires (non-kill diagnostic): if total transition-days < 10 in the window, router has operated in narrow regime space. Any claimed router validation from such a period is logged as "not adversarially tested"; compounding out-performance qualified accordingly. Threshold acknowledged as unjustified per Known Limitations #5.

**9.6 Fundamental template compliance.**
- Every monthly fundamental output (sub-step A output) checked: was each of the 6 input categories addressed via explicit tool-call retrieval, or documented as absent per the fallback protocol?
- Compliance checker: the monthly review session explicitly compares sub-step A's output against the template input list; any silent skip (no output AND no absence flag) fires this indicator. Known limitation: this is a format check, not a content check (see Known Limitations #6).

#### 10. Scenarios under which the router underperforms SGOV

Per Section 7's framing (router-value measured indirectly via per-strategy excess-of-SGOV), the router underperforms SGOV when the strategies' ACTIVATE-period returns fail to beat SGOV over the relevant window. Specifically:
- Router activates B, C, or D into DOWN-in-formation regime (Section 6 misclassification scenario 1, slow-burn deterioration sub-case — SPY=UP, Breadth=WEAK, so A and E are already deactivated by Section 3 activation rules, but B, C, and D are technically ACTIVATE per archetype 2) and those strategies take losses that would have been avoided by SGOV.
- Chronic default-deny (indicator 9.4) keeps strategies deactivated during normal markets where SGOV is the floor, so the system matches SGOV instead of beating it.
- Signal-redundancy (indicator 9.3) indicates the router is not doing work; fees erode against SGOV.

#### 11. Change-control lock

All thresholds in Section 2, all activation rules in Section 3, all template input categories in Section 4, the operational thresholds for misclassification-scenario flags in Section 6 (specifically the breadth >10pp month-over-month flag and the VIX drift >5pp within NORMAL over 30 days flag for scenario 1), and all indicator thresholds in Section 9 are immutable from experiment inception per `Experiment_Parameters.md`. *(rev 2026-07-10 — Strategy Arsenal autonomy conversion, owner directive.)* Revising any of these *live* items — a shared-vocabulary threshold or an existing strategy's own router rule — constitutes that strategy's termination and restart-as-a-materially-different-new-strategy via the Strategy Arsenal Lifecycle, not a quiet edit. A newcomer only APPENDS its own activation rule (and optionally its own single-strategy measurement); appending never touches an existing threshold and is not a revision. Roster-membership changes (adding or retiring a strategy) are versioned policy in `strategy/roster.yaml`, not experiment termination. A structural change to the router itself that re-tunes the shared vocabulary or an existing rule still requires a full router pre-mortem (now routine-run). "New-experiment initiation" is reserved for a deliberate owner-directed change to the measurement-and-selection MACHINERY, not for growing or shrinking the roster. Stress-tests (Section 2 justifications) are analytical exercises at monthly review, not operational revisions.

#### 12. Known limitations

Per `Experiment_Parameters.md` rev 12 stopping rule, items surfaced during adversarial review that are Tier 2 (calibration/measurability) or Tier 3 (completeness/scope) are carried as documented limitations rather than resolved. Each is checked at every monthly pre-mortem-indicator review.

1. **Slow-burn deterioration subjectively identifiable but mechanically absorbed into archetype 2 (rev 4 update).** The five mechanical indicators cannot distinguish a "slow-burn deterioration" (SPY=UP, Breadth=WEAK, VIX trending up within NORMAL) from a benign narrow-bull. Rev 4 resolved the prior rev 3 enumeration contradiction by removing the separate archetype and treating slow-burn as a misclassification sub-case in Section 6 scenario 1, with an explicit VIX-drift flag (VIX drift > 5 points within NORMAL over 30 days). *Status:* Tier 2 accepted; the flag is a partial not a full detection. *Review trigger:* if monthly review subjectively identifies slow-burn conditions that the flag did not catch and the router activated strategies that then lose capital, escalate to foundation-change review.

2. **9.2 denominator may be unreachable in benign regimes.** Minimum of 6 divergence reviews in 3 months exceeds the baseline expectation (~3–4 reviews in 3 months for a system with 5 strategies at ~20% monthly disagreement rate). In regimes with low disagreement volume, 9.2 is structurally unfireable. *Status:* Tier 2 accepted. *Review trigger:* if the denominator has been unreachable for 12+ consecutive months, revisit at monthly review.

3. **9.1 may be unfireable for strategies stuck in one state across the 12-month window.** Strategy D in a sustained-inversion regime can be DEACTIVATE for 18+ consecutive months, precluding the ≥3-months-each-state denominator. *Status:* Tier 2 accepted. *Fallback:* for any strategy with < 3 months of one state in the window, Indicator 9.1 reports raw cumulative TWR without the activate-minus-deactivate comparison, and that raw value is compared against the strategy's SGOV benchmark separately.

4. **9.4 threshold (40%) uncalibrated.** No ex ante basis for the 40% default-deny threshold. *Status:* Tier 2 accepted. *Review trigger:* recalibrate if early operational data shows baseline default-deny rate is materially different from 40%; recalibration is NOT a mid-experiment threshold change (forbidden by Section 11) but a diagnostic-interpretation adjustment logged for the next experiment.

5. **9.5 threshold (10 transition-days) unjustified.** No distributional basis. *Status:* Tier 2 accepted. *Treatment:* 9.5 remains a non-kill diagnostic. If empirical transition rate over first 6 months is materially different, the interpretation is adjusted but the threshold is not revised mid-experiment.

6. **9.6 is a format check, not a content check.** Using revised macro data instead of initial prints, aggregator summaries instead of primary sources, or stale data — none of these fire 9.6 as long as something is retrieved and logged. *Status:* Tier 2 accepted. *Partial mitigation:* the template input specifications explicitly name initial-print and primary-source requirements; a separate monthly spot-check (≥ 1 input randomly selected per month and verified against primary source) is added to the monthly review template.

7. **Monthly rhythm timing.** Fundamental session runs on the first trading day of each calendar month. *Status:* Tier 3 resolved in rev 3; recorded here as the operational convention.

8. **Model-version change mid-experiment.** If Anthropic releases a new Claude model during the experiment, the artifact the pre-mortem was written about materially changes. *Status:* Tier 3 accepted. *Treatment:* a model-version change is handled by `AI_Trading_Foundation.md` Part 4's Version-change protocol, not the router pre-mortem — no early refresh fires; Tier 1 items are unaffected and Tier 2 numerical claims are flagged version-pending-replication. The deployment fact itself (the in-use-model field and its accompanying Tier 2 flip) is synced under Part 4's "Write authority — two tiers" Tier M mechanism by whichever of D3 (daily), Q4 (quarterly), or A3 (annual) fires first; version-specific capability research is then picked up by the quarterly Q3 delta and the annual A1 full re-derivation. (There is no monthly AI-capabilities review — that task folded into quarterly Q3 at the 2026-06-06 routine renumbering, and rev 3 dropped the prior Part 4 Question 8 — "model deprecation or version update" — from triggering refresh; see Part 4's Version-change protocol instead.)

9. **Strategy activation/deactivation execution mechanics.** When a strategy transitions from DEACTIVATE to ACTIVATE, the deployment mechanics (immediate, scale-in, wait-for-next-signal) are specified in each strategy's section, not the router pre-mortem. *Status:* Tier 3 scope clarification — not a router pre-mortem concern.

10. **Flash-crash recurrence threshold (Section 1 archetype 8, liquidity event).** At what frequency does "transient misclassification cost" become "structurally fragile regime"? *Status:* Tier 3 accepted. *Review trigger:* if ≥ 3 archetype-8 events occur within any 6-month window, escalate to monthly review for regime reclassification discussion.

11. **Self-containment scope boundary for non-Experiment_Parameters.md references (rev 4 addition from cycle 3 judge).** The pre-mortem's self-containment statement requires inline enumeration of the six requirements. Scaffolding cross-references to Experiment_Parameters.md (foundation-change trigger) are reproduced inline in Section 8. References to AI_Trading_Foundation.md Part 4 (the Version-change protocol and Write-authority-two-tiers sections, model-version change) in item 8 above and references to other parts of Strategy.md are not reproduced inline because they are scope-boundary references, not requirement-content references. *Status:* Tier 3 accepted as scope clarification. *Review trigger:* if a future adversarial cycle identifies a requirement-content dependency on a non-EP.md document that is not reproduced inline, escalate.

12. **Session 4 architectural saturation despite separate-session design (rev 4 addition from cycle 3 judge).** Session 4 runs on the same model weights as Sessions 1–3, so even with context isolation, weight-level biases are correlated. The four-session architecture is deliberately binding (Session 4 CONVERGENT → default DO-NOT-ACTIVATE) on a channel that the 2.24 residual in Section 8 acknowledges as saturated. *Status:* Tier 2 accepted; the architecture chooses the conservative direction (default DO-NOT-ACTIVATE) on a known-saturated channel, which is defensible. *Feedback loop:* indicator 9.4 catches chronic default-deny at the aggregate level and would eventually fire if Session 4's CONVERGENT rate is producing under-activation bias. *Review trigger:* if 9.2 fires (CONVERGENT rate ≥ 50% in 3 months with ≥ 6 reviews) AND 9.4 fires (≥ 40% default-deny in 12 months) simultaneously, escalate to foundation-change review of the four-session architecture.
