**Acceptance under combined revision-churn + saturation stop (Experiment_Parameters.md rev 14 stopping rule):** Cycles 4, 5, and 6 all surfaced exactly one defect class — asymmetric reasoning between binding constraints (Constraint 2 and Constraint 3) — with the locus shifting across revisions. Cycle 4 attacked rev 4's coarse asymmetry (Constraint 3 had a "flowing exclusion" while Constraint 2 had no exclusion despite same hard-wired status). Cycle 5 attacked rev 5's grounding asymmetry (Constraint 2's at-entry-identifiability argument was near-circular vs. Constraint 3's mechanism-based; 2% cap asymmetrically attributed). Cycle 6 attacked rev 6's over-correction (Constraint 2's strengthened self-reference argument now contaminates Constraint 3's entry criterion 6, which had not been updated to graded framing). Each fix resolves one direction of asymmetry and exposes another at the next resolution level — the binding constraints are different in their actual at-entry identifiability properties (2.4 uniformly closed under self-reference; 2.19 split between transparent-citation and buried-framing forms), and any attempt to make their textual treatments fully parallel produces a new mismatch at the next zoom level. This is the saturation pattern (EP rev 14 stop condition ii) combined with revision-induced defects (EP rev 14 stop condition iii). Cycle 7 not run; pre-mortem accepted with rev 7's fix applied, residual asymmetric-reasoning structure documented in Known Limitations as a property of the underlying constraints rather than a fixable document defect.

Cycle outcomes by revision: rev 1 → cycle 1 TIER 1 (2 items); rev 2 → cycle 2 TIER 1 (2 items); rev 3 → cycle 3 TIER 1 (3 items); rev 4 → cycle 4 TIER 1 (1 item); rev 5 → cycle 5 TIER 1 (2 items); rev 6 → cycle 6 TIER 1 (1 item); rev 7 → ACCEPTED under combined revision-churn + saturation stop; rev 8 → cycle 8 TIER 1 DEFECT — REVISION REQUIRED (2 confirmed items; 4 of the attacker's 7 findings downgraded or reframed on independent grounds; AR_orc 2026-08-05). Cycles 1-7 CONVERGENT theater-check; cycle 8 MIXED (orchestrator independently downgraded three attacker Tier-1 claims and independently found a new Tier-1 item not in the attacker's output). Cycles 1-4 ran under three-session architecture (8/8 CONVERGENT, 0 adjudicator invocations). Cycles 5-6 ran under single-session architecture per EP rev 14.

**Systematic money-loss scenarios:**

1. **Beta-dominated loss during mid-hold regime pivot.** Router deactivates A on regime change, but deactivation only gates NEW entries — open A positions run through the pivot with no router-driven stop. Because A's router cadence is monthly (mechanical technical signals update daily but router state change involves the fundamental template) and A's holding period is 1–12 months, any DOWN transition that occurs while A has open positions exposes those positions for the remaining holding period. The 12-month hard time stop is the binding backstop, not the router.
2. **AI catalyst identification systematically late (per 2.14):** by the time Claude's synthesis produces the thesis, consensus has already priced it; entry is late-cycle within the thesis arc.
3. **Homogenization (per 2.8):** AI catalyst calls are consensus trades; synchronized positioning creates shared exit dynamics.
4. **Late-cycle bull market where catalyst beats no longer move stocks:** the edge decays with market maturity; historical base rates stop applying.
5. **12-month hard time stop forces exits on slow-developing theses:** correct theses that take 14 months to play out are force-exited at 12.
6. **Counter-argument-passing but predictively-weak narratives (per 2.4, added rev 3).** Entry criterion 4 requires adversarial counter-argument without decisive flaw. Both the bull thesis and the counter-argument are produced by the same model on the same weights; 2.4 reduces by ~30% with explicit counter-argument, leaving ~70% of the bias in place. Systematic entry into theses that sounded sound at entry (passed the counter-argument) but turn out predictively weak is the 2.4 failure mode. Unlike scenarios 2 (2.14), 3 (2.8), and 4 (structural late-cycle), the 2.4 scenario is largely undetectable at entry — the filter designed to catch it is itself subject to it. Detection happens after the fact, via the edge-decay indicators below (hit rate decline, beta-dominance pattern).

**SGOV-underperformance scenarios (market conditions under which SGOV wins, rev 4 rewrite):**

1. **High short-rate regime (SGOV's hurdle is high).** When the Fed funds rate is elevated (≥ 4%), SGOV yields 4%+ annualized at negligible risk. A must clear that hurdle net of drag (commissions, bid-ask, STCG tax on a 1–12 month hold) plus variance. In an elevated-rate regime, A's breakeven thesis move after drag is ~6–8% on a typical position — higher than the median catalyst move, so even a directionally-correct A book can fail to beat SGOV. Consequence of the market condition: typical catalyst produces 3–5% moves; tax and commission drag consume a meaningful fraction; net excess vs. SGOV is negligible or negative.
2. **Low-dispersion regime (individual-stock catalysts don't differentiate).** When cross-sectional return dispersion is compressed — historically associated with late-cycle uniformity, indiscriminate risk-on, or indiscriminate risk-off — individual-stock catalyst reactions are muted relative to market beta. Even when A identifies the correct directional call, the thesis magnitude is small and drag-dominated. Consequence: mixed hit rate (50/50) combined with symmetric payoffs and drag → SGOV wins.
3. **Macro-dominance regime (idiosyncratic narratives drowned by macro).** When macro shocks (rate surprises, geopolitical events, policy actions) dominate daily price action, single-name catalyst narratives get overwhelmed. The router's fundamental question ("Is the current environment one in which individual-stock catalysts are being rewarded, or is macro dominating?") explicitly addresses this regime; router deactivation prevents new entries. But open A positions remain exposed (see Section 2 scenario 1). Consequence: catalyst theses correctly identified produce realized returns that look like market beta rather than idiosyncratic alpha, and the beta-dominance indicator (Section 4) fires.
4. **Late-cycle bull market where catalyst beats no longer move stocks.** This is the Section 2 scenario 4 condition restated for its SGOV-underperformance implication: forward expectations are fully priced, so actual-catalyst-matches-expectation produces zero-or-negative reaction. A's mechanism still identifies theses but the thesis-to-return conversion is broken. Consequence: low hit rate on direction, small magnitude on the wins → SGOV wins.

**Edge-decay indicators:**

- Hit rate on catalyst-direction calls declining across consecutive 10-trade windows
- Average winning position size not materially greater than average losing position size
- Catalyst-event-driven returns approximately equal to market returns over same periods (A is just long beta wearing a narrative costume). Test (rev 2 formula correction): a position is beta-dominated if `|A_position_return − SPY_return_over_same_holding_period| < |SPY_return_over_same_holding_period|` (β=1 decomposition: residual alpha is smaller in magnitude than the market's contribution). The prior formula `|excess return| < |absolute return|` was incorrect — it systematically over-flagged clean alpha as beta-dominated in cases where position return and SPY return were same-signed.

**Specific disadvantages posing greatest risk:**

- **2.4 (narrative over-fit):** Central risk, added in rev 2. A's core mechanism is AI narrative synthesis; the 2.4 bias ("plausible but predictively-weak narratives; same model can produce coherent narratives for opposite conclusions") is the on-point failure mode. *Mitigation:* entry criterion 4 (adversarial counter-argument at entry) is the textbook 2.4 control. *Residual:* explicit counter-argument reduces bias by ~30%, leaving ~70% of the bias in place. This is a weight-level property, not removable by prompting. Counter-argument is useful, not sufficient. **[rev 11 provenance flag, 2026-08-24 — the ~30%/~70% split above is UNSOURCED. It originates in `AI_Trading_Foundation.md` §1.3's unattributed sentence ("the bias is only reduced by about 30% — it is hard-wired in the model's weights"), uncited since the repository's first commit (`e7be4c0`, 2026-05-08); A1's 2026-07-28 annual re-derivation searched for a source at all four evidence levels with two independent agents and recorded it ABSENT (`Annual_AI_Foundation_Sweep.md` §C.3), remedy NEW RESEARCH — not a replication on the current model line — and `AI_Trading_Foundation.md` §§1.3 and 2.4 both carry that flag verbatim. 2.4's EXISTENCE claim and the narrative-over-fit MECHANISM are unaffected; only the MAGNITUDE is unsupported. Read ~30%/~70% here as an unsourced working assumption, not a measurement. This section's OWN size-attack paragraph (Section 1 eligibility rule) already carries a consistent honest framing for its instance of this same borrowed figure (rev 9/rev 10: "an analogically-grounded approximation, not a freshly-derived figure") — this flag extends that same honesty to this locus, which the rev 9/10 fix did not reach. Swept identically across all five strategy pre-mortems' primary 2.4 loci in one pass rather than only the two AR_orc-flagged loci (Strategy C `premortem-C-2026-a3` cycle 15 T1-4; Strategy E `premortem-E-2026-a3` cycle 11 T1-1).]**
- **2.8 (homogenization):** A's core thesis construction is consensus-adjacent.
- **2.13 (miscalibration):** optimism on catalyst magnitude affects both conviction-to-enter and conviction-to-size. Current controls are ordinal-only conviction, the seven-factor budget record, and mandatory adversarial attack on size. Rev 19 removed every numeric magnitude backstop. *Residual:* structural and unbounded within the strategy.
- **2.14 (recency bias):** narrative weighted heavily toward most recent quarter.
- **2.18 (instruction adherence over capital preservation):** A's thesis-scaled sizing hands the loss magnitude to the same judgment 2.18 says may not privilege capital preservation. Both mechanical envelopes are retired. The seven-factor record and mandatory adversarial size attack are judgment-dependent. *Residual:* one thesis may consume the full strategy portfolio; the −50% deployed-TWR kill detects loss after the fact rather than capping it.
- **2.19 (look-ahead bias):** during thesis construction, Claude may be pattern-matching to historical analogues whose outcomes are encoded in the weights. *Partial strategy-level control (rev 3, scope walked back in rev 4):* entry criterion 6 (2.19 universe exclusion) catches theses that explicitly cite a named historical setup as primary support. *Scope (rev 4 honest characterization):* this catches the transparent-citation minority of 2.19 operation. The dominant operating form of 2.19 — where outcome memory shapes framing without being cited as the support — is not caught by the exclusion, because Claude evaluating whether its own thesis rests on buried analogical framing is itself subject to 2.19-adjacent biases. The Scaling Paradox (larger models show this worse) means even the exclusion's enforcement is weakening-over-time at the model level. *Residual:* the buried-framing form of 2.19 remains materially unmitigated at the strategy level. See Known Limitations for this residual; the experiment-level 36-month mark-to-market kill trigger (per Experiment_Parameters.md) is the ultimate external check.
- **2.20 (textbook-rational penalty):** A assumes the market will respond to fundamentals; in behaviorally-irrational periods, fundamental catalysts get ignored. *Router protection is entry-only:* the router deactivates A in DOWN regimes, preventing new entries during behaviorally-irrational phases that the router has identified. Open positions carrying into such a phase have no router-driven stop; the 12-month hard time stop is the binding open-position backstop. Rev 2 correction: the prior claim that "the router partially mitigates 2.20" overstated the router's scope. The router gates entries; it does not mitigate open-position exposure to 2.20 during mid-hold regime pivots.
- **2.26 (RL-post-training-induced decision-token overconfidence):** ordinal-only conviction prevents probability arithmetic but does not cap the size it produces. With both envelopes retired, overconfidence can inflate the budget without a numeric backstop. *Residual:* structural; measured post hoc through size-vs-outcome calibration.

**Binding core-edge constraints (req 7 confrontation — req 7 gloss added rev 10: `Experiment_Parameters.md`'s "Strategy pre-mortems" list, seventh bullet — "if the strategy's core edge is materially constrained by a specific disadvantage in `AI_Trading_Foundation.md` Part 2, the pre-mortem must explicitly confront that constraint and specify any exclusions or limitations on the strategy's universe that flow from it"):**

Strategy A's core edge is AI narrative synthesis. Three distinct constraints materially affect this edge and are confronted explicitly. The principled distinction between which constraints admit flowing exclusions vs. flowing limitations is at-entry identifiability of the bias signature (rev 5 reframing, retained in rev 6).

*Cross-constraint flowing limitation (rev 6 addition; superseded rev 8, 2026-08-03).* ~~The 2% position size cap mechanically bounds per-thesis exposure independent of which AI_Edges disadvantage produced the bad signal. It separates sizing from the narrative output's apparent strength and applies uniformly to all theses regardless of bias mechanism. Listed once here at the cross-constraint level rather than under each individual constraint, to avoid asymmetric per-constraint attribution. The cap flows from the confluence of multiple disadvantages affecting conviction-to-size: 2.4 (over-fit confidence), 2.13 (asymmetric optimism), 2.14 (recency-driven conviction), 2.19 (buried-framing-driven conviction). It does not eliminate any of these biases; it bounds the per-thesis loss magnitude when a biased thesis fails.~~

*(Rev 43, 2026-07-28 — superseded: `Experiment_Parameters.md` rev 18 / `Strategy.md` rev 43, owner directive, retired the fixed 2%-of-strategy-portfolio position-size rule quoted above. There is no blanket per-position size rule for A. Position size is now a per-thesis risk budget the AI sets and justifies at every entry and every add, expressed as Capital at Risk — for A's long-equity instrument, the full position notional, since A carries no price-based stop-loss and so no discount applies. The property credited to the retired cap above — separating sizing from narrative strength and applying uniformly regardless of bias mechanism — was specifically a consequence of sizing being fixed and AI-independent. It cannot survive the swap to AI-set, per-thesis sizing even in principle, because size is now exactly a function of the same judgment 2.4/2.13/2.14/2.19 already bias, and no replacement property with that mechanical, bias-independent character exists at the per-thesis layer. What replaces it is a two-layer control with a materially larger residual, not a restoration of the old property:*

- ~~*OUTER layer — still mechanical, still bias-independent.* Hard envelopes, versioned policy rather than judgment: per-name aggregate CaR (all tranches, this strategy) ≤10% of A's strategy portfolio value; per-strategy total deployed CaR ≤75%. This is the only part of the retired cap's property that survives, and it survives at a 5× looser bound (2% → 10% per name).~~ **[rev 9, owner directive 2026-08-05 — THE OUTER LAYER NO LONGER EXISTS.** `Experiment_Parameters.md` rev 19 retired both hard CaR envelopes (per-name ≤10%, per-strategy deployed ≤75%); code enforcement was removed from `strategy_math/common.py` and `c_options_math.py` the same day. There is no numeric ceiling on position size at any level, so no version of the retired flat 2% cap's protective property survives, at any bound. **This is not a return to the pre-Rev-43 baseline either:** Rev 40 (2026-07-21, owner directive) had already authorized unlimited-count adds to an open position in the same name — `strategy/03_strategy_a.md`'s Adding-to-an-existing-position rule states "no cumulative cap on the number of tranches a single name may accumulate" — so that baseline was already unbounded for any multi-tranche thesis; the current regime is unbounded for every A thesis, single- or multi-tranche. Surviving compensation is wholly procedural (seven-factor justification + mandatory adversarial attack on size); the −50% deployed-TWR drawdown kill is a detector that fires after a loss is realized, not a cap that prevents it. Owner accepted the trade-off: no stop-losses means size is the only risk lever and the AI is trusted with it.]**
- *INNER layer — judgment-dependent, and therefore exposed to the same confluence.* The per-thesis risk budget is disciplined by: (a) a mandatory size justification, recorded in `events.decision_log`, against a fixed seven-factor list — falsifiability of the invalidation criteria, time to resolution, instrument loss profile, correlation with the existing book, regime fragility, liquidity/exit-ability at the intended size, and conviction as an ordinal tier only (never an explicit probability multiplied into a sizing formula; a size with no recorded justification is not a valid order); and (b) a mandatory adversarial attack on the size itself, extending A's existing counter-argument entry criterion to state what the position costs if the thesis is wrong and argue whether that loss is acceptable. This attack is a same-model check on a same-model sizing decision, subject to the same closure this section's Constraint 2 already documents for entry criterion 4 — i.e. graded discrimination, not clean elimination. Entry criterion 4's ~30% empirical bias-reduction / ~70% residual, established under Constraint 2 above, is carried over to this size-attack instance of the same check by structural analogy — the mechanism (same-model check on a same-model output) and its self-reference closure are identical — not because the size-attack's own bias-reduction rate has been separately measured; no independent empirical estimate exists for the size-attack specifically, so the ~30%/~70% split should be read here as an analogically-grounded approximation, not a freshly-derived figure (rev 9; framing corrected rev 10).
- *Residual, stated quantitatively (rev 9 corrected).* Under `Experiment_Parameters.md` rev 19 (owner directive, 2026-08-05), there is no numeric ceiling on position size at any level, so worst-case per-thesis loss from a bias-inflated conviction is bounded only by A's strategy portfolio itself. That governing ceiling is the same regardless of tranche count, not a separately-derived figure per branch (rev 10 tightened; rev 9 stated the two branches side by side): for a single-tranche thesis it bounds the loss on that one tranche, and for a multi-tranche thesis — Rev 40 (2026-07-21, owner directive) authorizes unlimited-count adds to a single name, and `strategy/03_strategy_a.md`'s Adding-to-an-existing-position rule states "no cumulative cap on the number of tranches a single name may accumulate" — it bounds the aggregate CaR across all of that name's tranches the same way. There is no current numeric bound to state a ratio against relative to the retired 2% figure, single- or multi-tranche alike. *(Historical note, not a current figure: for the transitional window `Experiment_Parameters.md` rev 18 was in force, 2026-07-28 to 2026-08-05, the bound was a 10%-of-portfolio per-name envelope; rev 19 retired it.)* Allocation of this unbounded budget is judgment-dependent rather than fixed. Sourced rather than asserted (rev 10; rev 9's citation was an undefined "A2 2026" pointer, corrected here): per the 2026 per-strategy constraint audit (`Experiment_Parameters.md`'s "A2" task; `Annual_Constraint_Audit.md` §2.2 Strategy A), A's own 2.13 citation status is flat — best-measured Claude ECE 0.120 vs. the previously documented 0.122 low end, no reduction. 2.18 is not in A's own citation graph in that per-strategy audit (it was added to Section 5's disadvantage list independently, in rev 8, on the sizing-confluence argument stated there) but is the document-wide Step 1 master-gate finding for 2.18 in the same `Annual_Constraint_Audit.md` (§2.0) — STRENGTHENED, Tier 1, ineligible for reduction — sourced onward to `Annual_AI_Foundation_Sweep.md`'s 2026 annual full re-derivation ("A1 2026"): in-window evidence includes covert-sabotage and record-tampering rates across multiple vendors' models under objective pressure, and a Claude model mislabeling 85.6% of judge calls when truthful labels conflicted with an inferred higher-order goal. So this residual is not shrinking: 2.13 shows no reduction and 2.18's underlying evidence has grown stronger, not weaker, since A's foundation revision. Partially offsetting: under the old fixed cap, 2.13/2.26 overconfidence was unmitigated AND unmeasurable in the sizing dimension (every trade was the same size); with ordinal conviction recorded per entry, size-vs-outcome becomes a directly measurable calibration series. This is a detection improvement, not a magnitude mitigation, and does not close the residual above. The −50% deployed-TWR drawdown kill is the only quantitative backstop on this residual, and it is a detector that fires after a loss is realized, not a cap that prevents it.
- *Confluence (unchanged in kind, wording only).* The sizing decision remains exposed to the same confluence of disadvantages affecting conviction-to-size — 2.4 (over-fit confidence), 2.13 (asymmetric optimism), 2.14 (recency-driven conviction), 2.19 (buried-framing-driven conviction) — now joined by 2.18 (instruction adherence over capital preservation) and 2.26 (RL-post-training-induced decision-token overconfidence) as the named compensating-control targets for the mandatory-adversarial-attack-on-size control (see Section 5). Neither the procedural discipline nor post-hoc measurement eliminates these biases; rev 19 retired the outer envelope, so no numeric layer now bounds a biased thesis's loss.

*Constraint 1 — Public-information only.* Any thesis that cannot be reconstructed from public documents at entry is not a tradeable thesis in A. No private channel checks, no expert network, no supplier/customer discussions, no tipping. Enforcement: if during thesis construction Claude finds itself reasoning from "per industry contacts" or "per channel checks" or equivalent, the thesis is abandoned. This is a methodological exclusion, not an AI_Edges-flowing exclusion.

*Constraint 2 — 2.4 narrative over-fit (rev 4 added, rev 5 reframed, rev 6 mechanism-grounded).* Per Section 5, 2.4 is the on-point failure mode for A's core mechanism (AI narrative synthesis). Confrontation: 2.4's operational signature — an over-fit narrative — is not at-entry identifiable, by a self-reference mechanism. Narrative quality is itself the model-level judgment that 2.4 biases. The strategy's only available at-entry discriminator is Claude's own assessment of narrative coherence and predictive plausibility, and that assessment is exactly what 2.4 distorts. By self-reference, no in-distribution discriminator can exist outside the biased judgment — the biased generator and the biased discriminator are the same weights producing the same kind of output. This parallels Constraint 3's mechanism-level argument (weight-level retrieval has no observable surface feature for the buried-framing form), with the locus of non-identifiability being self-referential model judgment rather than retrieval-mechanism location.

*Partial identifiability acknowledged (rev 6 addition).* Entry criterion 4 (adversarial counter-argument) achieves graded discrimination, not zero. The ~30% bias-reduction figure reflects partial discrimination on the subset of cases where the over-fit is severe enough to break under sustained adversarial pressure (i.e., where the biased counter-argument generator can find decisive flaws despite sharing the bias). The ~70% residual is the regime where the biased counter-argument generator and the biased thesis generator agree — the over-fit is mild enough or self-consistent enough that within-distribution adversarial probing does not surface decisive flaws. By self-reference, no further within-distribution check can resolve this residual; only out-of-distribution signals (realized post-entry returns) can. This graded view is consistent with the mechanism-level non-identifiability claim above: full at-entry identifiability would imply the discriminator is operating outside the biased judgment, which by self-reference it cannot.

*Flowing limitations from 2.4 (per req 7's "exclusions OR limitations" wording — rev 5 reclassification, rev 6 narrowed):*

- *Entry criterion 4 (adversarial counter-argument required, no decisive flaw).* Motivated explicitly by 2.4 with an empirical ~30% reduction figure. Reclassified in rev 5 from "reliance" to flowing limitation per req 7. The counter-argument check is a graded within-entry filter operating on every candidate thesis, catching cases where over-fit is severe enough to break under adversarial probing.

(~~The 2% position size cap, previously listed here in rev 5, is now in the cross-constraint flowing limitation at the top of this subsection — it bounds exposure for theses driven by any of multiple confluent biases, not 2.4 alone.~~ **[Rev 43, owner directive, 2026-07-28 — superseded: there is no flat 2% cap; the operative bound is the thesis's AI-chosen Capital-at-Risk budget ~~within the hard envelopes, per-name aggregate CaR ≤ 10% of strategy portfolio value and per-strategy deployed CaR ≤ 75%~~ **— owner directive 2026-08-05 retired both envelopes, so the budget itself is the only bound and it has no ceiling**.]** The successor mechanism is still discussed once at the cross-constraint level rather than per-constraint, for the same reason rev 6 moved it there — to avoid asymmetric per-constraint attribution.)

*Residual:* the ~70% post-counter-argument bias is structural and not removable by prompting. Entry criterion 4 reduces magnitude on the within-distribution-detectable subset; the cross-constraint position-size cap bounds amplification per thesis; neither eliminates the underlying weight-level bias on the residual subset where biased generator and biased discriminator agree. Post-hoc detection runs through Section 4 edge-decay indicators (hit-rate decline, beta-dominance pattern). The ~70% residual is acknowledged at the strategy level (Known Limitations #4) and partially compensated at the experiment level via the 30-trade gate evaluation and the 36-month mark-to-market kill trigger per Experiment_Parameters.md.

*Constraint 3 — 2.19 look-ahead bias (rev 5 reframed for parallel structure with Constraint 2).* 2.19 is a binding AI_Edges disadvantage for A's core edge: foundation-model weights encode outcomes of historical events, and A's narrative-synthesis mechanism can pattern-match to specific historical setups whose outcomes are remembered rather than analyzed. The Scaling Paradox (larger models show this bias worse) means the constraint is structural and not diminishing with capability improvements. Confrontation: 2.19 has two operating forms with different at-entry identifiability properties.

- *Transparent-citation form — partially at-entry identifiable (rev 7 reframing).* When the thesis explicitly cites a named historical setup as primary support, the citation is an observable surface feature at entry. This admits a graded discriminator (entry criterion 6): the thesis does not rest primarily on analogy to a specific named historical setup. The test is whether removing the historical-analogue portion causes the remaining reasoning to fail to support the directional call — if yes, the thesis is inadmissible. Historical context as calibration (typical drug approval timelines, sector margin distributions, typical earnings surprise magnitudes at the same company) is admissible because these are institutional or current-company facts, not outcome memory. *Why graded, not clean (rev 7):* the primacy test ("does the remaining reasoning fail to support the directional call") invokes narrative-quality assessment, which is closed over by 2.4-style self-reference per Constraint 2's argument. The biased discriminator running entry criterion 6 is the same weights running entry criterion 4. Entry criterion 6 is therefore a graded discriminator parallel to entry criterion 4 — it catches the subset of transparent-citation cases where biased primacy judgment correctly identifies the citation as primary, and misses the sub-residual where biased primacy judgment fails self-referentially (e.g., the thesis cites a historical analogue and Claude's self-assessment incorrectly judges the remaining reasoning to support the directional call when it does not). The empirical fraction of transparent-citation cases caught by entry criterion 6 is unknown but bounded above by 100% and below by the corresponding ~30% figure for entry criterion 4's same-mechanism graded discrimination.
- *Buried-framing form — NOT at-entry identifiable.* When outcome memory shapes the framing without being cited as the support, the bias is operating through weight-level retrieval rather than through any observable surface feature. Claude self-auditing for this form is itself subject to 2.19-adjacent biases compounded with 2.4 self-reference. No graded discriminator is possible because no at-entry partition exists.

*Scope of strategy-level control (rev 7 reframing):* entry criterion 6 is a graded discriminator catching a fraction of the transparent-citation form of 2.19 operation; the buried-framing form is not caught at all, and the sub-residual of the transparent-citation form (where biased primacy judgment fails) joins it in Known Limitations #3. Both Constraint 2 and Constraint 3 are now treated as admitting only graded discriminators rather than clean exclusions, which is consistent with the self-reference closure that rev 6 established for Constraint 2 — once self-reference is accepted as the mechanism for non-identifiability, no within-distribution check escapes it for either constraint. The principled distinction between Constraint 2 and Constraint 3 is therefore not "exclusion vs. limitation" or "identifiable vs. non-identifiable," but the *underlying mechanism asymmetry*: 2.4 is uniformly closed under self-reference; 2.19 has a transparent-citation form where syntactic features (an explicit citation) provide partial leverage even when narrative-quality assessment is biased, and a buried-framing form where no syntactic features exist. Entry criterion 6 is partially effective on the transparent-citation form because the syntactic citation is at-entry identifiable even when the primacy assessment of it is biased; the criterion fails on cases where the biased primacy assessment overrides the syntactic signal.

**Dated failure indicators for monthly review:**

- [Each monthly review] Hit rate on closed A trades; cumulative
- [Each monthly review] Review any A position held > 9 months: is thesis still playing out, or drifting toward time-stop? Force reassessment.
- [At 15 trades] Preliminary edge check
- [At 30 trades] Gate evaluation
- [Each monthly review] For any closed A trade in the prior month: was the outcome driven by thesis (idiosyncratic) or by market beta over the holding period? Apply the corrected beta-dominance formula above. If beta-dominated, note.

**Known limitations (rev 4):**

Per Experiment_Parameters.md rev 12 stopping rule, items surfaced during adversarial review that are not resolved by the strategy's own design are carried here as documented limitations. Each is checked at every monthly pre-mortem-indicator review.

1. **Fee-dominated phase at small strategy-portfolio scale.** Per Experiment_Parameters.md, fee-dominated underperformance alone does not terminate the strategy — only the gate or kill triggers act on fee-drag-inclusive metrics. A will spend some of its early life in fee-dominated territory. *Review trigger:* at the 15-trade preliminary edge check, compute the counterfactual excess return net of fees vs gross; if the gross would have been acceptable, fee drag is the binding constraint.

2. **12-month hard stop is a blunt instrument.** Force-exit on thesis-slow is diagnostic but also potentially cost-producing if the thesis would have resolved in month 13–15. *Review trigger:* at the 30-trade gate evaluation, compute counterfactual returns if 12-month time-stop exits had been held an additional 6 months each; if materially better, the hard stop is too aggressive.

3. **2.19 not meaningfully mitigated at the strategy level — buried-framing form plus transparent-citation sub-residual (rev 4 first surfaced, rev 7 expanded).** Entry criterion 6 is a graded discriminator on 2.19's transparent-citation form, catching the subset where biased primacy judgment correctly identifies an explicit historical-setup citation as primary support, and missing the sub-residual where biased primacy judgment fails self-referentially. 2.19's buried-framing form (where outcome memory shapes the framing without being cited as the support) is not caught at all because no at-entry surface feature exists. Together these constitute the unmitigated 2.19 risk at the strategy level. Claude self-auditing for either form is subject to 2.19-adjacent biases compounded with 2.4 self-reference. The Scaling Paradox compounds at the model-generation level. *Status:* accepted residual risk. *External check:* 36-month mark-to-market kill trigger per Experiment_Parameters.md is the ultimate fallback. *Review trigger:* if the beta-dominance pattern (Section 4, indicator 3) fires across any 10-trade window AND the hit-rate-decline pattern fires in the same window, the failure mode may be 2.19 (either form) rather than other candidates (2.8 homogenization, 2.14 recency, 2.4 narrative over-fit); flag for monthly review even if no single indicator has crossed its threshold.

4. **2.4 residual ~70% is structural and not mitigated by the strategy (rev 4 addition, rev 5 reframed for symmetry with 2.19).** Per Section 5 Constraint 2 (rev 5 reframed): 2.4's signature is not at-entry identifiable, so no universe exclusion is possible. Entry criterion 4 reduces bias by ~30%; ~70% remains. *Status:* accepted residual risk. *Review trigger:* same as 2.19 — beta-dominance + hit-rate-decline in the same 10-trade window is the signature.

5. **Section 6 trade-count-triggered indicators ("at 15 trades", "at 30 trades") are not strictly calendar-dated.** At 15–25 trades/year active pace, the 15-trade milestone hits at approximately month 8 and the 30-trade milestone at approximately month 14–18. *Status:* accepted as satisfying the "dated" spirit of req 6 (gloss added rev 10: `Experiment_Parameters.md`'s "Strategy pre-mortems" list, sixth bullet — "a dated set of expected failure indicators that can be checked at monthly reviews") via assigned-to-review-timepoint reading.
