**Self-containment statement:** This pre-mortem enumerates within its own text the strategy's thesis, eligibility rule, entry criteria, exit rules, declared trade frequency, router activation rule, money-loss scenarios, SGOV-underperformance scenarios, edge-decay indicators, specific AI_Edges disadvantages posing greatest risk, dated failure indicators, binding core-edge constraint confrontation, and known limitations. Short tags accompany AI_Edges disadvantage numbers; full definitions are in the attacker-prompt inline block.

#### 1. Strategy B thesis and mechanism

**Thesis.** AI's narrative synthesis identifies situations where the market's immediate reaction to a public event (earnings, FDA decision, guidance update, regulatory action) has over- or under-shot relative to the information content of the event. The mispricing resolves over weeks as the market fully digests the event's narrative context. B's edge is in evaluating the quality of a realized market reaction to known public information, rather than predicting a future event's outcome. B is structurally exposed to 2.20 (textbook-rational penalty) because the strategy's mechanism is precisely the textbook-rational instinct — that prices return to fundamental value — applied after an overreaction. The router's HIGH-VIX exclusion is a partial regime-level mitigation; no mechanism-level mitigation exists. (Rev 1 thesis claimed B "compensates 2.20"; rev 2 retracts this inversion.)

**Differentiation from A.** A enters before event; B enters after. A and B never simultaneously hold positions in the same name.

**Instrument eligibility.**
- US-listed common equity; Market cap ≥ $2B at entry; 30-day ADV ≥ $10M; Long or short; ~~2% position size at entry~~ **[Rev 43, owner directive, 2026-07-28] AI-chosen risk budget per thesis — no blanket per-position figure.** Capital at Risk (CaR — the loss if the thesis is wrong, measured at entry): long equity = full notional (no stop-loss, so no discount); short equity = notional × the short stop distance (the rev 3 short-side stop-loss below, retained unchanged, is now the CaR-defining bound). ~~Bounded by hard envelopes: per-name aggregate CaR ≤ 10% of strategy portfolio (all tranches summed); per-strategy total deployed CaR ≤ 75%.~~ **[owner directive 2026-08-05 — both envelopes RETIRED; the CaR budget is unbounded numerically.]** Sizing is justified per-thesis against a fixed seven-factor list (falsifiability of invalidation criteria; time to resolution; instrument loss profile; correlation with the existing book; regime fragility; liquidity/exit-ability at size; conviction as an ordinal tier only, never a probability multiplied into a sizing formula) and attacked on size as well as direction — per `Experiment_Parameters.md`'s sizing policy, which extends B's existing adversarial counter-argument entry criterion (entry criterion 4) to also attack size. **[Rev 9, cycle 8 AR_orc — honesty correction, Strategy C rev 10 model]** This extension is currently specified as sizing-policy prose rather than as a discrete, numbered, gating sub-requirement within entry criterion 4's own text below, a narrower form of compensation than a hard gate would be; live `decision_log` entries (2026-07-29 FTV, 2026-08-03 MTZ) confirm it operates in practice on every recent B thesis. No options.

**Entry criteria.**
1. Public event occurred within the last 10 trading days, producing immediate price reaction of ≥ 5% in either direction (close-to-close on event day).
2. Narrative synthesis concludes the market reaction is materially over- or under-sized relative to the event's fundamental implications, grounded in: event details, company fundamentals, comparable historical reactions to similar events at similar companies (retrieved, not recalled).
3. Thesis has explicit convergence target (rev 4 index-list strict enumeration): either (a) a numerical price level OR (b) a specific event drawn from this strictly enumerated closed list named at entry — "next earnings release," "next FDA decision date," "next FOMC meeting," or "inclusion announcement in one of: S&P 500, Russell 1000, or Nasdaq 100." No additional event types are admissible; no other indexes are admissible. The convergence target is immutable from entry. Specific timeline: entry through convergence expected within 60 days.
4. Adversarial counter-argument doesn't identify a decisive flaw — specifically, attacker must consider whether the market reaction is information-driven rather than sentiment-driven (if information-driven, "mispricing" is actually correct pricing). Per Section 5 binding-constraint Constraint 1 below: the counter-argument check is a graded discriminator with ~30% empirical bias-reduction magnitude, not a clean filter.
5. No A position currently open in the same name.

**Exit rules.**
- Convergence target reached (numerical price target or named-event occurrence); OR
- Thesis invalidation: new public information changes the situation; OR
- Timeline expiry at 60 days from entry; OR
- For short positions: borrow rate spike above 10% annualized.
- For short positions (rev 3 addition, T1-A fix): short-side stop-loss — close the short if the underlying rises ≥ 25% from short-entry price. ~~Caps single-trade short loss at 0.5% of strategy portfolio at 2% sizing.~~ **[Rev 43, owner directive, 2026-07-28 — superseded: sizing is no longer flat 2%.]** This stop is retained unchanged and is now the CaR-DEFINING bound for a short thesis, not a supplement to a fixed size: short CaR = notional × 25%, so a short's stated risk budget and its notional are related by that fixed factor. **Rev 19 removed the per-name ceiling:** the AI-chosen short CaR budget has no numeric cap. **Asymmetry note:** for the same AI-chosen CaR budget, a short can carry roughly 4× the notional of a long because the 25% stop defines short CaR while long CaR equals notional one-for-one. This is a loss-profile input to the seven-factor sizing justification, not a separate permission or ceiling.

Not exit-triggering: adverse mark-to-market without news (long positions only — short positions have the rev 3 stop-loss above); general market moves.

**Directional posture (rev 9, cycle 8 AR_orc self-containment fix).** B is long-biased in practice: through 2026-06-22, 0 of ~108 B theses produced a short entry, and every SHORT framing examined at entry criterion 4 was declined — SP1 (sell-side bull-ratification) is the dominant decline pattern. Spot-validation of the declines confirms they avoided losers, not winners (see the Rev 36 empirical note under Section 5 Constraint 2 for the full derivation). Decision: the SP1/2.20 short gating is not to be loosened on the strength of this observation; the only short-enabling lever is a router relaxation, deferred to a future pre-mortem.

**Declared expected frequency.** 20–30 trades per year over active periods.

**Router activation rule.**
- Technical: SPY Trend State ≠ DOWN AND VIX Regime ≠ HIGH.
- Fundamental question **(monthly template answers — annotation added rev 11; this is the same parenthetical Strategy C's router rule already carries, and it is supplied here because cycle 10 found the question stated as an operative activation criterion with no resolver named anywhere in this artifact. The question text itself is unchanged.)**: Is the current environment one in which event reactions show measurable mean reversion at 2–8 week horizons, or is the market in a regime where reactions are fully informative (no mean reversion to exploit)?

**How the Fundamental question is resolved (rev 11 added per cycle-10 AR_orc W1, which found it presented as an operative activation criterion with no data source, threshold, cadence or resolver — failing screens (iii) and (iv)).** The question is not a free-standing gate B evaluates for itself. It is one input to a routine that already exists and already resolves it on a fixed cadence; rev 11 reproduces that machinery inline for self-containment rather than inventing any part of it.

- **Resolver.** The **M1b routine** ("Strategy Mapping and Activation Calls"), which emits a binary **ACTIVATE / DO-NOT-ACTIVATE** call for every roster strategy, with written reasoning, in its immutable output format.
- **Data source.** M1b reads *only* the latest strategy-blind `FUNDAMENTAL_AXIS` regime scores produced by the M1a routine (`state.current_regime` / `events.regime_events`). M1b is deliberately blinded from raw macro, policy and earnings sources; M1a is deliberately blinded from the strategy sections. The answer to B's Fundamental question is therefore derived from a regime scoring that never saw this strategy. **There is no B-specific fundamental data feed, and its absence is a design property of the two-routine blinded architecture, not an omission in this artifact.**
- **Cadence.** Monthly — M1b runs after M1a on the first trading day of each month. Between monthly runs the standing call governs, unless an out-of-cycle router review is triggered *and* its threshold is met; a review whose threshold is not met leaves the standing call in force.
- **Threshold.** Deliberately **none**. The Technical leg is Boolean and mechanically evaluable; the Fundamental leg is a judgment whose *output* is Boolean. That asymmetry is intended — supplying a numeric threshold here would be machinery, and this revision does not do it.
- **Operative effect.** The call is recorded in `events.regime_events` (scope `STRATEGY_ACTIVATION`) and consumed by the M4 routine. Mechanical reconciliation overrides are applied **after** the call and are one-directional — they can only turn ACTIVATE into DO-NOT-ACTIVATE, never the reverse; the override binding on B is `shock_overlay = acute`, which deactivates every strategy. A DO-NOT-ACTIVATE call blocks **new** B entries for the period; it does **not** force exits of open positions.
- **Residual, registered not closed.** Because the Fundamental leg carries no threshold and no B-specific source, its answer is auditable only as a routine's recorded judgment, not as a recomputable number. That residual is real and is registered as **Known Limitation #14** below rather than papered over. Rev 11 adds no threshold, no data source and no resolver — the resolver and cadence already existed and the threshold is deliberately absent; this block records them so the artifact stands alone.

#### 2. Systematic money-loss scenarios

1. **Information-driven moves misread as overreactions.** Moves that look over-extended actually reflect correct absorption of event information. "Mispricing" is pricing. B's thesis is wrong on the core question.
2. **Short-squeeze dynamics on short positions.** The +25% stop defines stated short CaR as notional × 25% in ordinary execution, but rev 19 places no ceiling on that stated CaR. A gap can execute beyond the stop and exceed the declaration. See Known Limitation #7.
3. **Narrative over-fit on reaction quality (per 2.4).** "The market overreacted" is always a plausible story; ~70% residual after counter-argument check (per Section 5 Constraint 1, rev 2 graded discriminator framing).
4. **Homogenization of post-event mean-reversion trades (per 2.8).** B is entering the same names, on the same direction, as many other AI operators running similar strategies; synchronized exit dynamics.
5. **Base-rate neglect (per 2.15).** Reactions that feel overdone may have high historical base rates of continuation, not reversal. No explicit base-rate gate exists at entry; see Known Limitations #3.
6. **Behavioral-irrationality persistent regime (per 2.20, rev 2 added, rev 8 register note per cycle 7 W3).** B's mechanism assumes prices return to fundamental value; in regimes where the market remains behaviorally irrational for extended periods (bubbles, panic-driven sell-offs persisting beyond 60-day timeline), B's convergence theses fail systematically. Router HIGH-VIX exclusion mitigates the worst cases but not ~~slow-burn~~ **[Rev 8, cycle 7 W3 — "slow-burn" struck: this wording implied rarity that is not established; see Known Limitations #4 for the heterogeneous-vs-homogeneous-population distinction this regime's frequency depends on]** behavioral regimes that don't trigger HIGH VIX.

#### 3. SGOV-underperformance scenarios (market conditions under which SGOV wins, rev 2 rewrite)

1. **High short-rate regime (SGOV's hurdle is high).** When Fed funds rate is ≥ 4%, SGOV yields 4%+ at negligible risk. B must clear hurdle net of drag (commissions, bid-ask, STCG tax on 0–60 day hold) plus variance. Typical convergence magnitude on a 5%+ post-event reaction is 2–4% on a successful trade; high-rate regime forces breakeven thesis move higher than median realized convergence.
2. **Low cross-sectional dispersion regime.** When return dispersion across post-event names is compressed, post-event reactions are uniformly small and the convergence trade has too little magnitude per trade to clear the SGOV hurdle on a portfolio basis. B's edge is inherently in differentiated reactions; uniform-reaction regimes drown the signal.
3. **High-correlation regime (factor-driven dynamics).** When name-level returns are dominated by factor exposure rather than idiosyncratic event reactions, the post-event mispricing thesis is overwhelmed by macro factor moves over the 60-day timeline. Thesis can be directionally correct on the event-reaction question but realized return is dominated by factor noise.
4. **Persistent behavioral-irrationality regime (rev 8 register note per cycle 7 W3).** When the market remains behaviorally irrational for extended periods (across multiple 60-day windows), B's convergence-to-fundamentals theses time-expire systematically before the market corrects. Router HIGH-VIX exclusion catches the high-volatility version of this regime; the low-volatility persistent-irrationality version (slow drift away from fundamentals) is uncaught. **[Rev 8, cycle 7 W3] Section 2 #6 above and Known Limitations #4 name this same regime; nothing here should be read as implying it is infrequent relative to the HIGH-VIX-caught majority — see Known Limitations #4 for the heterogeneous-vs-homogeneous-population distinction this regime's frequency depends on.**

#### 4. Edge-decay indicators

- Hit rate on convergence direction declining across consecutive 10-trade windows. (Note: 50% hit rate is the random baseline, not an edge-decay threshold; expected hit rate at edge is unknown ex ante and will be calibrated post-30-trade gate. See Known Limitations #5.)
- Short-side positions disproportionately underperform long-side (suggests short theses are weaker or squeeze-exposed).
- Average time-to-convergence for winning trades approaches 60 days (thesis is arriving at the timeline expiry; edge is marginal).
- Convergence-vs-expiry closure ratio declining (per Section 6; depends on the rev 2 tightening of convergence-target definition in entry criterion 3).

#### 5. Specific AI_Edges disadvantages posing greatest risk

- **2.4 (narrative over-fit):** central risk. B's mechanism is exactly narrative synthesis evaluating reaction quality — the activity 2.4 names as predictively-weak. *Mitigation:* entry criterion 4 (graded counter-argument discriminator, ~30% empirical bias-reduction). *Residual:* ~70%, weight-level, not removable by prompting. **[rev 12 provenance flag, 2026-08-24 — the ~30%/~70% split above is UNSOURCED. It originates in `AI_Trading_Foundation.md` §1.3's unattributed sentence ("the bias is only reduced by about 30% — it is hard-wired in the model's weights"), uncited since the repository's first commit (`e7be4c0`, 2026-05-08); A1's 2026-07-28 annual re-derivation searched for a source at all four evidence levels with two independent agents and recorded it ABSENT (`Annual_AI_Foundation_Sweep.md` §C.3), remedy NEW RESEARCH — not a replication on the current model line — and `AI_Trading_Foundation.md` §§1.3 and 2.4 both carry that flag verbatim. 2.4's EXISTENCE claim and the narrative-over-fit MECHANISM are unaffected; only the MAGNITUDE is unsupported. Read ~30%/~70% here as an unsourced working assumption, not a measurement. Swept identically across all five strategy pre-mortems' primary 2.4 loci in one pass rather than only the two AR_orc-flagged loci (Strategy C `premortem-C-2026-a3` cycle 15 T1-4; Strategy E `premortem-E-2026-a3` cycle 11 T1-1).]** See Section 5 binding-constraints Constraint 1 below.
- **2.8 (homogenization):** post-event reversal is a commoditized AI strategy. *Mitigation:* ~~sector concentration cap (3 per GICS sector)~~ **REMOVED per Rev 35 (owner directive) — 2.8's homogenization residual is now monitored-only (Section 4 hit-rate decay), not capped at entry.** *Residual:* cross-AI-operator crowding remains unaddressed (and the same-sector concentration it partially bounded is now uncapped). See Section 5 binding-constraints Constraint 3 below.
- **2.20 (textbook-rational penalty, rev 8 residual register corrected per cycle 7 W3):** B's mechanism IS this instinct. Rev 1 thesis claimed B "compensates 2.20"; rev 2 retracts. *Mitigation:* router HIGH-VIX exclusion (entry-only) is partial regime-level mitigation — unchanged. *Residual:* ~~slow-burn behavioral regimes that don't trigger HIGH VIX remain unmitigated.~~ **[Rev 8, cycle 7 W3]** behavioral regimes that don't trigger HIGH VIX remain unmitigated — **not established to be rare; see Known Limitations #4 for the heterogeneous-vs-homogeneous-population distinction this residual's frequency depends on.** See Section 5 binding-constraints Constraint 2 below and Known Limitations #4.
- **2.15 (base-rate neglect, rev 2 downgrade, rev 6 mechanism analysis added per cycle 5 T1-A):** 2.15's empirical 85% error rate on Bayesian base-rate tasks operates at entry criterion 2's narrative synthesis. The synthesis weighs current event details against retrieved comparables; 2.15 over-weights semantic congruence ("this event looks like X-type") between current event and retrieved comparables, while under-weighting base-rate priors on reaction magnitudes ("how often does X-type post-event reaction actually mean-revert"). Entry criterion 2's "comparable historical reactions" requires a comparables consultation, not a base-rate computation, so 2.15's distortion operates directly on the synthesis output. *Mitigation:* none implemented at strategy level. Adding an explicit base-rate gate would be a strategy-design change beyond cycle-1 scope. *Residual:* structural; see Known Limitations #3. **[rev 13 provenance flag, 2026-09-07 — the "85% error rate" stated at the head of this entry is UNSOURCED, established by a dedicated re-derivation from the primary sources for this revision rather than carried across from the sibling flag at Strategy C. **Origin:** `AI_Trading_Foundation.md` §2.15 asserts it in a bare sentence with no paper, benchmark or dataset attached — *"Empirically confirmed at scale. On standard Bayesian base-rate tasks, AI exhibits error rates of ~85%."* **What the annual re-derivation found:** A1's 2026-07-28 FULL traversal (`Annual_AI_Foundation_Sweep.md` §2.15) recovered no support for the magnitude at any level — L1 ABSENT, L3 ABSENT, L4 phenomenon-only with no percentage recoverable from either retrieved source, and at L2 a single adjacent study, NBER working paper **w34745** ("Behavioral Economics of AI: LLM Biases and Corrections", 2026), whose base-rate item includes a Claude model but whose PDF body was undecodable, so the Claude-specific rate could not be extracted and the study is recorded `[UNCONFIRMED]`: it "confirms a study *exists* at L2; confirms nothing about the magnitude." Cross-level verdict **SPARSE**; transfer assessment "The 85% figure has no in-window support at any level"; tag `ABSENT-FROM-RECENT-RESEARCH [all levels]`. The one in-window signal that touches the question at all points the other way (`2507.17951`, larger models show greater Bayesian coherence) and fails A1's guardrail 1 outright. `Annual_Constraint_Audit.md`'s 2.15 row records **Reduction: NONE**, basis "SPARSE; replication FAIL; magnitude → VERSION-PENDING" — note that NONE is its reduction verdict, not a supporting-evidence field, which the escalation that produced this flag mis-stated. **What the foundation document already says, and what it does not:** §2.15 is not itself unflagged — it carries a **VERSION-PENDING REPLICATION** flag (every magnitude in the item was measured on Claude Opus 4.7, the prior model of record) and the A1 fade-review flag recording this magnitude as an ABSENCE whose remedy is NEW RESEARCH, not replication. The defect was therefore never in the foundation document; it was that THIS artifact restated the figure carrying none of that qualification. **Affected / unaffected:** 2.15's EXISTENCE claim and the semantic-congruence-over-prior MECHANISM are untouched; only the MAGNITUDE is unsupported. Read "85%" here as an unsourced working assumption, not a measurement. The figure is NOT removed. **Why this is a calibration item and not a Tier 1:** unlike the `~30%`/`~70%` split flagged at this section's 2.4 entry — which supplies 2.4's stated numeric residual and is load-bearing for it — the `~85%` figure is INERT at both of its loci here. This entry's own mitigation is "none implemented at strategy level" and its residual is the qualitative label "structural"; the cross-constraint flowing limitation names 2.15 only in its exposure list and performs no arithmetic with the number ("No mechanical magnitude layer remains"); and no entry criterion, gate, threshold, sizing rule or review trigger anywhere in this pre-mortem keys off it. **Scope:** flagged at both Strategy B loci in one pass (this entry and Known Limitation 3), closing the pair that Strategy C's rev 18 recorded out-of-route and escalated rather than swept (AR_orc `premortem-C-2026-a3` cycle 17 T2-2).]** Rev 5 left this entry as null-mitigation acknowledgment without mechanism analysis; cycle 5 T1-A surfaced this as asymmetric Section 5 treatment relative to 2.14.
- **2.13 (miscalibration on convergence targets, rev 6 added per cycle 5 T1-A, rev 7 mitigation rewritten per cycle 6 T1-A):** 2.13's empirical 80% CI containing outcomes ~69% of the time means AI-generated price forecasts are systematically optimistic with hard-wired bias. B's entry criterion 3 admits "a numerical price level" as a convergence target option. Convergence targets set by a model with persistent return-forecast optimism are systematically unreachable, biasing exit composition toward timeline-expiry over convergence and reducing the strategy's effective hit rate. *Mitigation:* entry criterion 4 (counter-argument check) is the only real mitigation, and it is partial — it reduces 2.4-class biases by ~30% empirically and may reduce 2.13-driven target unrealism only insofar as the counter-argument step explicitly examines target magnitude (which it is not specifically required to do). 60-day timeline expiry caps the *duration* of capital tied up in optimistic-target theses but does NOT mitigate the named harm (exit-composition bias and hit-rate reduction); cycle 6 surfaced that earlier rev 6 wording incorrectly cited timeline expiry as a mitigation when timeline expiry IS the failure manifestation. *Residual:* effectively unmitigated at the strategy level beyond entry criterion 4's partial filtering; see Known Limitations #11. Section 4's convergence-vs-expiry closure ratio is the post-hoc detection mechanism. Adding a real mitigation (e.g., bounding convergence targets by empirical historical reaction-magnitude percentiles) would be a strategy-design change beyond cycle-6 scope.
- **2.14 (recency bias, rev 5 added per cycle 4 T1-β):** B's entry criterion 1 requires the event within the last 10 trading days, which places the entry-week reaction in 2.14's most-recent-week-weighting bucket. The narrative synthesis required by entry criterion 2 must compare this 10x-weighted recent reaction against historical comparables in earlier weight buckets. 2.14 distorts this comparison systematically toward the recent reaction. *Mitigation:* 60-day timeline expiry partially mitigates by forcing exit before entry-week dominance becomes a stable reference for re-evaluation; entry criterion 4 (counter-argument check) is the proximate filter on recency-driven theses with the same ~30% reduction figure that applies to 2.4. *Residual:* recency-bias-specific residual is unspecified empirically and rolled into Known Limitations #2 alongside 2.4. The cross-constraint preamble already lists 2.14 as one of the five disadvantages flowing into the position size cap; rev 5 elevates 2.14 to a Section 5 explicit entry per req 4.
- **2.17 (algorithm appreciation):** B may favor algorithmic-screened post-event setups even when human-discretionary attention would flag issues. *Mitigation:* none; observable through monthly review qualitative check.
- **2.18 (instruction adherence over capital preservation):** long downside equals the AI-chosen notional and has no numeric ceiling; short stated CaR is notional × 25%, also uncapped, and gaps can exceed it. The 60-day expiry, seven-factor record, mandatory size attack, and short stop remain. *Residual:* one long thesis may consume the full strategy portfolio; correlated longs and short gaps can produce larger realized damage before kill triggers detect it.
- **2.26 (RL-post-training-induced decision-token overconfidence) — enumerated rev 11 per cycle-10 AR_orc W3.** Cycle 10 found 2.26 cited as load-bearing in the cross-constraint flowing limitation below while being undefined, untagged, and absent from this enumeration (failing screens (ii) and (iv)). It is **enumerated here rather than struck from that sentence**, because the citation is substantively correct: under rev 19 the per-thesis budget is a judgment call, and 2.26 is precisely a bias on judgment-call commitment. *Definition, reproduced inline for self-containment:* reinforcement-learning post-training induces systematic overconfidence at the decision token — the model commits to a call more strongly than its evidence supports — a distinct mechanism from 2.13's numeric miscalibration, which is why both are listed. *Where it bites B:* the size decision. `Experiment_Parameters.md` §Position size factor 7 admits conviction **ordinally only** and forbids multiplying a raw model probability into a sizing formula, naming 2.26 alongside 2.13 and 2.18 as one of the three disadvantages the mandatory adversarial attack on size exists to compensate. *Mitigation:* ordinal-only conviction closes the arithmetic channel; the mandatory size attack is the named compensating control. Both are procedural. *Residual:* neither bounds magnitude — an overconfident ordinal tier still yields a larger budget, and post-rev-19 no numeric ceiling stops it. See Known Limitations #12 and #13.

**Binding core-edge constraints (req 7 confrontation, rev 2 added):**

B's core edge is AI narrative synthesis evaluating the quality of realized market reactions. Three distinct constraints materially affect this edge and are confronted explicitly. The principled distinction between which constraints admit flowing exclusions vs. flowing limitations is at-entry identifiability of the bias signature — **[Rev 9, cycle 8 AR_orc self-containment fix, restated inline rather than left as a bare cross-reference]** i.e., whether the confound a disadvantage produces can be recognized and screened at the moment of entry, supporting a flowing exclusion (a hard admissibility filter that keeps the biased case out), or only manifests or becomes detectable after the fact, supporting only a flowing limitation (a bounding or monitoring mechanism applied to a case already admitted). This distinction was first articulated in Strategy A's pre-mortem (rev 7) and is applied identically here without re-deriving it.

*Cross-constraint flowing limitation (rev 19 propagation; rev 8 historical derivation retained below; disadvantage list corrected rev 11 per cycle-10 AR_orc W3 and its accompanying Tier 3).* No mechanical magnitude layer remains. The AI-chosen budget is exposed to 2.4, 2.13, 2.14, 2.15, **2.17**, 2.18, 2.20, and **2.26**; the seven-factor record and mandatory size attack are procedural only. **[Rev 11: two corrections to this list, which cycle 10 found internally inconsistent in both directions. (a) **2.17 (algorithm appreciation) is ADDED** — it is enumerated in Section 5 above with an explicitly null mitigation, and it bears on sizing through the same channel as the rest (a budget justified partly on classical-method outputs inherits 2.17's over-deference to them), yet it was silently omitted from this list. (b) **2.26 is RETAINED and now carries a definition** — it was cited here as load-bearing while undefined, untagged and absent from Section 5; rather than strike a correct citation, rev 11 enumerates and defines 2.26 in Section 5 above, so this reference now resolves within the artifact. The asymmetry cycle 10 identified — a defined, enumerated disadvantage omitted while an undefined one was included — is closed in both directions rather than only the one the Tier-1 item named.]**

- ~~**Outer layer (historical Rev 43 envelope era):** hard envelopes, versioned policy rather than judgment — per-name CaR ≤10% and per-strategy deployed CaR ≤75%.~~ **[Rev 19, 2026-08-05 — both envelopes retired. No mechanical outer layer remains; the −50% deployed-TWR kill detects loss after the fact.]** Rev 40's unlimited-count adds explain why the preceding historical flat rule had already ceased to bound aggregate per-name risk.
- **Inner layer (judgment-dependent, bias-exposed):** the per-thesis risk budget is disciplined by a fixed seven-factor justification recorded at every entry and add and the mandatory adversarial attack on size. This is procedural discipline, not a numeric cap.
- *Long positions, per-thesis bound:* ~~the cap bounds worst-case per-thesis loss at 2% of strategy portfolio in uncorrelated regimes (long downside is mechanically bounded at -100% of position, so 2% × 100% = 2%).~~ **[Rev 43, superseded; rev 19, 2026-08-05, retired the successor envelopes as well.]** Long-equity CaR is full notional — there is no stop-loss, so no discount — and has no numeric ceiling. A single long thesis may consume the full strategy portfolio; the drawdown kill detects the loss after the fact.
- *Long positions, strategy-portfolio exposure:* correlated concurrent longs can lose simultaneously in behavioral-irrationality regimes. The historical Rev-43 10% / ~30% examples are superseded by rev 19: neither per-thesis nor strategy-level sizing has a numeric ceiling. See Known Limitation #12.
- *Short positions, exposure:* the +25% stop defines stated CaR as notional × stop distance but does not cap it; gaps can exceed stated CaR. The historical Rev-43 10%/40%-notional arithmetic is superseded by rev 19. See Known Limitations #7 and #12.
- *Short positions, strategy-portfolio exposure:* no total-short-exposure cap exists. The removed sector-count cap and retired CaR envelopes provide no current bound; correlation is monitored only. See Known Limitation #12.

**Residual (rev 19 propagation):** the mechanical, bias-independent protection is gone. The procedural controls are exposed to the same judgment failures they address. Size-vs-outcome becomes measurable, which improves detection but supplies no magnitude bound.

The cycle history on this preamble is itself the saturation pattern: cycle 2 surfaced the architectural hole in the rev 2 wording (T1-A); rev 3 attempted resolution by adding the short-side stop-loss; cycle 3 surfaced execution-risk in the stop and a new long-side concurrency gap (T1-1 and T1-2); cycle 7 (W1/W2) surfaced that the entire bound was keyed to a since-retired flat rule and understated the true concurrent-drawdown figure by ~5×. Rev 4 honestly characterized the bounds rather than asserting them as structural caps; rev 8 re-derives those bounds under the sizing regime actually in force, at a materially larger and explicitly-stated residual.

*Constraint 1 — 2.4 narrative over-fit on reaction quality (rev 5 simplified for T1-γ).* B's core mechanism is the activity 2.4 names: narrative synthesis evaluating whether a market reaction "looks overdone." Confrontation: 2.4's operational signature — an over-fit narrative about reaction quality — is not at-entry identifiable, by self-reference. The same self-reference mechanism documented in Strategy A pre-mortem rev 7 Constraint 2 applies: deciding which comparables are "similar," weighing their magnitudes against current reaction, and judging whether the reaction is "materially over- or under-sized" all involve narrative-quality assessment, which is itself the model-level judgment that 2.4 biases. By self-reference, no in-distribution discriminator exists outside the biased judgment.

*Procedural input — retrieved comparables (rev 5 acknowledgment, not a flowing limitation).* Entry criterion 2 requires "comparable historical reactions to similar events at similar companies (retrieved, not recalled)." This is a procedural discipline that forces the synthesis to engage with retrieved data; it is not a flowing limitation in the req 7 sense. Rev 3 attempted to formalize it as "partial quantitative leverage" (cycle 3 T1-3 found this self-refuted because comparable-selection is itself biased synthesis). Rev 4 attempted "coherence constraint" (cycle 4 T1-γ found this still relies on the biased session as discriminator — same closure Part 2 names as the problem). Rev 5 stops trying to extract a flowing limitation from the retrieved-comparables requirement and acknowledges it as procedural only.

*Flowing limitation:* entry criterion 4 (counter-argument as graded discriminator, ~30% empirical magnitude reduction; ~70% residual). *Residual:* the ~70% post-counter-argument bias is structural; post-hoc detection runs through Section 4 edge-decay indicators. Rev arc on this constraint: rev 2 imported Strategy A's tight-closure language verbatim (cycle 2 T1-C); rev 3 attempted to add partial quantitative leverage from retrieved comparables (cycle 3 T1-3 found this self-refuted); rev 4 attempted coherence-constraint reframing (cycle 4 T1-γ found this still self-referential); rev 5 drops the Part 1 framing entirely and treats retrieved comparables as procedural-only. See Known Limitations #2.

*Constraint 2 — 2.20 textbook-rational instinct embedded in the mechanism (rev 8 residual register corrected per cycle 7 W3).* B's mechanism IS the textbook-rational instinct — that prices return to fundamental value — applied after an overreaction. Confrontation: 2.20 is not a bias B can mitigate at the strategy level because it IS the strategy. The router's HIGH-VIX exclusion is a partial regime-level mitigation (entry-only), filtering the high-volatility version of behaviorally-irrational regimes. *Flowing limitation:* router HIGH-VIX exclusion (entry-only; does not apply to open positions) — **unchanged; this rev 8 pass is a description-only correction, not a gating change.** *Residual:* ~~slow-burn behavioral regimes that don't trigger HIGH VIX remain unmitigated; the 60-day timeline expiry is the binding open-position backstop.~~ **[Rev 8, cycle 7 W3 — register corrected]** behavioral regimes that don't trigger HIGH VIX remain unmitigated; the 60-day timeline expiry is the binding open-position backstop. **The prior "slow-burn" framing presumed these regimes are rare — a homogeneous-population reading of 2.20 (see Known Limitations #4 below for the full, self-contained explanation of the heterogeneous-vs-homogeneous distinction and the upstream A1 2026 finding it draws on). This pre-mortem does not adopt a frequency figure of its own for Strategy B, but no longer describes this residual as though it were necessarily a tail case.** See Known Limitations #4. *Empirical note (Rev 36, owner directive, 2026-06-23 short-bar revisit):* the 2.20-embedded-mechanism prediction is borne out asymmetrically on the short side. Through 2026-06-22, 0 of ~108 B theses produced a short entry and every SHORT framing examined was declined — SP1 (sell-side bull-ratification) is the dominant criterion-4 pattern (32 of 103 NO-GOs), with SP5 in-window-binary (e.g. INTC 6/18) next. Spot-validation confirms the declines avoided losers, not winners: the declined shorts held or extended rather than faded (INTC 6/18 rumor-pop, declined SP5, extended +5% post-decision to $140.94 vs the ~$121 fade target). Decision: do **not** loosen the SP1/2.20 short gating; B is long-biased in practice (see the Strategy B *Directional posture* note in Section 1), and the only short-enabling lever is a router relaxation, deferred to a future pre-mortem.

*Constraint 3 — 2.8 homogenization on commoditized AI strategy.* Post-event mean reversion is heavily replicated; multiple AI operators may crowd the same names and direction. Cross-operator crowding is not identifiable at entry. The former sector count cap, flat 2% cap, and 10%/75% envelopes are all retired. **No mechanical at-entry magnitude bound remains.** The residual is monitored post-trade through Section 4/6; experiment-level kill triggers detect losses after the fact. See Known Limitations #2.

#### 6. Dated failure indicators for monthly review

- [Each monthly review] Hit rate on closed B trades; long-side vs. short-side breakdown.
- [Each monthly review] Check current short borrow rates on active short legs.
- [At 15 trades] Preliminary edge check.
- [At 30 trades] Gate evaluation; calibrate edge-decay hit-rate threshold per Known Limitations #5.
- [Each monthly review] For any closed B trade: did it close on convergence (target hit) or timeline expiry? Ratio of convergence to expiry closures is diagnostic. Per rev 2 entry criterion 3 tightening, "convergence" requires numerical price level or named-event occurrence — no ex-post redefinition.
- [Each monthly review, rev 5 added per cycle 4 T1-δ, rev 6 metric updated per cycle 5 T1-C, rev 8 lagging-indicator conjunction added per cycle 7 orchestrator addition 2, rev 9 conjunction retired per cycle 8 AR_orc Tier 1] Concurrent active-position monitoring across active B positions. Compute (a) count of currently-open B positions, ~~(b) sum of absolute long exposures as fraction of strategy portfolio,~~ **[owner directive 2026-08-05 — metric (b) REMOVED; with no numeric sizing ceiling an aggregate-exposure threshold carries no information about risk. Operating_Protocols.md KL#12. Do not reinstate it or substitute another exposure threshold.]** (c) sum of absolute short exposures as fraction of strategy portfolio, (d) average pairwise daily-return correlation across all active long B positions over the prior 20 trading days (requires at least 3 concurrent longs to compute; otherwise dormant). ~~If (b) exceeds 10% AND (a) ≥ 2 [Rev 43, owner directive, 2026-07-28, rev 8 recalibration per cycle 7 orchestrator addition 2 — the count conjunction is added because a single maximally-sized long thesis can now reach (b) = 10% alone under the ≤10%-per-name CaR envelope, with zero concurrent-position correlation; requiring (a) ≥ 2 restores (b)'s original crowding-detection purpose using an existing metric, not a new threshold] OR (d) exceeds 0.5, flag for KL #12 escalation evaluation~~ **[Rev 9, cycle 8 AR_orc Tier 1 — struck: with metric (b) retired 2026-08-05 this "(b) exceeds 10%" leg referenced a metric, and a ceiling, that no longer exist; and the "(a) ≥ 2" conjunction never restored real discrimination even at the old ceiling — a single thesis sized at (or, since rev 19, above) that level plus one arbitrarily small second position satisfied the conjunction while being the definitionally non-crowded, single-dominant-thesis case KL #12 exists to exclude. No replacement exposure threshold is substituted, per the 2026-08-05 owner directive above.]** If (d) exceeds 0.5, flag for KL #12 escalation evaluation per the leading-indicator rules in KL #12 (rev 6 correlation-matrix detection; rev 9 — (d) is now the sole trigger, both here and in KL #12 below). Cycle 5 T1-C surfaced that rev 5's "maximum drawdown" wording on (d) was inconsistent with KL #12's portfolio-aggregate wording and admitted two incompatible operational readings; rev 6 unifies on the correlation metric. ~~**Rev 9 note:** (d) requires ≥3 concurrent longs to compute; a 1-dominant-thesis-plus-1-trivial-position book has at most 2 longs, so (d) is correctly dormant there — this is the honest behavior for a non-crowded book, not a coverage gap, since a book that thin cannot be the correlated-crowding failure Section 2 #6 and Constraint 2 name regardless of dollar exposure.~~ **[Rev 10, cycle 9 AR_orc W3 — struck and corrected; the rev 9 note above was itself mathematically wrong.** (d) requires ≥3 concurrent longs to compute — an operational dormancy threshold, unchanged by this revision. But the rev 9 defense of that threshold was false: at exactly 2 longs the average pairwise correlation over the single available pair of distinct names is well-defined and equal to that pair's own correlation coefficient — not trivially 1, and not undefined (undefined only applies at exactly 1 long, where no pair exists at all). So a book of exactly two large, highly correlated longs is NOT "too thin to be the failure mode": it sits squarely inside the correlated-crowding failure Section 2 #6 and Constraint 2 both name, and (d) simply does not fire on it — not because the case is mathematically excluded, but because the dormancy threshold is set at 3. This is a real, presently-live detection gap, not a coverage non-issue; it is registered as Known Limitation #13 below, including the 2026-08-09 measurement that Strategy B currently holds exactly two open positions. Closing the gap (e.g. making (d) computable at N=2) would change the definition of a live monitoring indicator and is explicitly out of scope for this documentary revision.]**

#### 7. Known limitations (rev 2 added)

1. **Fee-dominated phase at small strategy-portfolio scale.** Accepted. Review trigger at 15-trade preliminary edge check: compute counterfactual excess return net of fees vs. gross.

2. **2.4 + 2.8 residuals at the strategy level.** ~70% post-counter-argument 2.4 residual is structural. 2.8 homogenization is observable post-trade only via hit-rate decline across windows. Review trigger: if hit-rate-decline pattern fires across any consecutive 10-trade windows, flag for monthly review with both 2.4 and 2.8 as candidate failure modes.

3. **2.15 (base-rate neglect) is not mitigated at the strategy level (rev 2 acknowledgment).** Entry criterion 2's "comparable historical reactions" consultation does not implement a base-rate gate. Empirical 2.15 error rate is ~85%. **[rev 13 provenance flag, 2026-09-07 — the "~85%" restated here is UNSOURCED; the full derivation is at this section's Section 5 2.15 entry and is not repeated. In short: `AI_Trading_Foundation.md` §2.15 attaches no source to the figure and already carries both a VERSION-PENDING REPLICATION flag and an A1 fade-review ABSENCE flag over it; A1's 2026-07-28 full traversal returns cross-level verdict **SPARSE** and tag `ABSENT-FROM-RECENT-RESEARCH [all levels]`, its one adjacent L2 study (NBER w34745) recorded `[UNCONFIRMED]` with an undecodable PDF; `Annual_Constraint_Audit.md`'s 2.15 row records Reduction NONE, basis "SPARSE; replication FAIL". 2.15's existence claim and mechanism are unaffected; only the magnitude is unsupported. Read "~85%" as an unsourced working assumption, not a measurement. It is inert at this locus: this limitation's acceptance of the residual, and its hit-rate review trigger below, rest on the argument that no base-rate gate exists — not on the number, which nothing here computes from. The figure is NOT removed, and neither this limitation nor its review trigger is changed by this flag.]** The strategy accepts this residual rather than implementing a base-rate gate at this stage; adding a gate would be a strategy-design change beyond cycle-1 scope. Review trigger: if hit-rate on convergence-direction calls < expected rate over a 30-trade window (calibrated post-gate; see #5), the failure mode may be 2.15 rather than 2.4 or 2.8.

4. **2.20 behavioral regime exposure (rev 8 register-corrected per cycle 7 W3).** ~~Router HIGH-VIX exclusion is partial; slow drifts away from fundamentals that don't trigger HIGH VIX remain unmitigated. The 60-day timeline expiry forces exits on slow-burn theses but does not prevent entries in slow-burn regimes.~~ **[Rev 8, cycle 7 W3, orchestrator-confirmed Tier 1 — register corrected, not a gating change: this Known Limitation's original vocabulary ("slow-burn," "residual," "ultimate fallback" below) presumes a rare-tail reading of the unmitigated regime. Reproduced inline for self-containment (this pre-mortem cannot rely on the reader consulting `AI_Trading_Foundation.md`): an upstream A1 2026 annual-foundation-sweep finding on 2.20 draws a heterogeneous-vs-homogeneous-population distinction. Under a HOMOGENEOUS-population reading (market participants are uniformly rational or uniformly bubble-prone), a persistent-irrationality regime is a bimodal, comparatively rare event — the reading this Known Limitation's original "slow-burn"/tail-event register presumed. Under a HETEROGENEOUS-population reading (a realistic mix, where only a MINORITY of participants are bubble-prone/behaviorally-biased), A1 2026 found that persistent-irrationality regimes can form roughly as often as not — i.e., this is not reliably a tail case. This pre-mortem previously never stated which reading it assumed; the tail-event vocabulary was only coherent under the homogeneous reading. No Strategy-B-specific frequency figure is derived or asserted here — the roughly-as-often-as-not finding is A1's, upstream and general, not a B-specific measurement, and this text does not treat it as one.]** Router HIGH-VIX exclusion is partial; drifts away from fundamentals that don't trigger HIGH VIX remain unmitigated — under the heterogeneous reading above, potentially a common failure mode rather than a rare one. The 60-day timeline expiry forces exits on such theses but does not prevent entries in such regimes. *External check:* 36-month mark-to-market kill trigger per Experiment_Parameters.md remains the backstop ~~— is the ultimate fallback~~ **[Rev 8: "ultimate fallback" struck — under the heterogeneous reading, a 36-month backstop for a potentially-common regime is a materially weaker practical protection than the same backstop would be for a genuine rare/tail event, and this text should not imply the two are equivalent. The backstop mechanism itself is unchanged.]**. Review trigger: if convergence-to-expiry ratio from Section 6 declines materially across consecutive monthly reviews, flag for behavioral-regime reassessment.

5. **Expected hit-rate baseline is not stated in rev 2.** Section 4's <50% threshold language is the random baseline, not an edge-decay threshold. Expected hit rate at edge is unknown ex ante and will be calibrated empirically at the 30-trade gate. Review trigger: at 30-trade gate, compute realized hit rate and set the edge-decay threshold at (realized hit rate − 1.5 SE) for subsequent windows.

6. **Long/short asymmetry not mechanically separated (rev 2 acknowledgment per cycle-1 T1-6).** B treats long and short as a single mechanism with shared eligibility, sizing, timeline, and entry criteria. Statistical asymmetries are real (different base rates, different financing dynamics, different tail behavior, different crowding dynamics). Adding per-side mechanical specifications would be a strategy-design change beyond cycle-1 scope. *Mitigation in current design:* Section 4 indicator 2 (short-side underperforms long-side) and Section 6 borrow-rate check provide ex-post detection; no ex-ante constraint. Review trigger: if short-side hit rate < long-side hit rate by ≥ 15 percentage points over any 20-trade window, escalate to design-change reassessment.

7. **Stop-loss asymmetry and gap-execution residual on shorts (rev 19 propagation).** The +25% stop defines stated short CaR as notional × 25%, but no numeric ceiling limits that declaration and a gap can exceed it. Long CaR is full notional with no stop or ceiling. The 60-day expiry, seven-factor record, mandatory size attack, and short stop remain procedural/operational controls. *Residual:* a long thesis can lose the full strategy portfolio; short gaps can exceed stated CaR.

*Review triggers (rev 5 split into two cadences per cycle 4 T1-ε):*
- *Per-trade trigger (rev 5 added):* after any closed trade with realized short loss > 1% of strategy portfolio (i.e., ~~the stop blew through more than 2× the target cap of 0.5%~~ **[Rev 43, owner directive, 2026-07-28 — superseded: there is no flat 0.5% target cap; short CaR is now notional × 25%, thesis-specific]**) OR realized long loss > 1.5% of strategy portfolio, escalate to design-change reassessment within the next monthly review. This trigger fires immediately upon trade closure, not at the 30-trade gate. Rationale: the failure mode is a single-trade catastrophic loss event; gating its detection to a 30-trade cadence would defer the escalation by potentially many months while the residual remains live.
- *30-trade gate trigger (retained from rev 4):* at the 30-trade gate, compute the worst-case single-trade loss across all closed trades and the distribution of losses; even if no single trade triggered the per-trade rule, systematic-pattern review (e.g., multiple trades clustering near the per-trade threshold) escalates to design-change reassessment.

8. **Coordination with Strategy A is one-directional (rev 2 acknowledgment per cycle-1 Tier 2).** Entry criterion 5 blocks B-after-A in the same name; nothing addresses A-after-B (A entering a name where B currently holds a position on the same thesis). Review trigger: at each monthly review, audit whether any A entries occurred in names with concurrent B positions; if so, flag for cross-strategy coordination review.

9. **Section 6 trade-count-triggered indicators ("at 15 trades," "at 30 trades") are not strictly calendar-dated.** Accepted as satisfying "dated" via assigned-to-review-timepoint reading.

10. **2.19 (look-ahead bias) connection to entry criterion 2 (rev 2 acknowledgment; rev 10 "Scaling Paradox" term defined inline per cycle 9 AR_orc W5).** Entry criterion 2's "comparable historical reactions to similar events at similar companies (retrieved, not recalled)" addresses retrieval contamination but not analysis contamination from outcome-encoded weights. ~~The Scaling Paradox compounds this.~~ **[Rev 10, cycle 9 AR_orc W5 — struck and replaced: this pre-mortem used the term "the Scaling Paradox" without defining it anywhere in the artifact. Reproduced inline for self-containment (this pre-mortem cannot rely on the reader consulting `AI_Trading_Foundation.md`), per the same convention used at Known Limitation #4: "Scaling Paradox" was `AI_Trading_Foundation.md` item 2.19's sub-claim that larger models exhibit look-ahead-bias contamination more severely than smaller ones. A1 2026's annual sweep REMOVED that sub-claim outright — two independent searches found no supporting model-size sweep, and in-window evidence (Profit Mirage, arXiv 2510.07920; One-Switch, arXiv 2605.23959) instead finds contamination vulnerability tracks architecture family, not model scale. Only the scaling-direction claim was withdrawn; the underlying contamination mechanism (outcome-encoded weights biasing analysis of historical setups) was not. Accordingly, this Known Limitation no longer cites a Scaling Paradox as an active compounding factor: no established model-size relationship currently compounds 2.19's contamination risk for B in either direction. The underlying contamination mechanism itself is unaffected and remains this KL's core concern.]** *Status:* accepted residual risk. Review trigger: shared with #2 and #3 (hit-rate decline across windows).

11. **2.13 (miscalibration) on convergence targets (rev 2 acknowledgment).** AI-generated convergence price targets are systematically optimistic per 2.13's empirical 80% CI containing outcomes ~69% of the time. *Status:* accepted residual. Review trigger: at 30-trade gate, compute realized convergence magnitude vs. entry-time target magnitude; if realized < 0.7× target on average, calibrate.

12. **Long-side concurrent-position correlation gap (rev 19 propagation).** B has no per-thesis, per-name, total-deployed, or position-count ceiling. In behavioral-irrationality regimes, concurrent longs can become positively correlated and compound losses; short correlation has the additional gap-execution residual. *Mitigation:* HIGH-VIX exclusion is entry-only and the 60-day expiry limits duration, not magnitude. The 36-month mark-to-market kill is an after-the-fact external check.

*Review trigger (rev 6 correlation-matrix detection per cycle 5 T1-B/T1-C; rev 9 consolidated to a single indicator per cycle 8 AR_orc Tier 1):* at each monthly review, compute the average pairwise daily-return correlation across all active long B positions over the prior 20 trading days. Use this single unambiguous metric across both this trigger and Section 6's exposure-monitoring bullet. Escalate to design-change reassessment if:
- *(a) Leading indicator (rev 6 primary; rev 9 — now the sole KL#12 trigger):* average pairwise correlation across active long B positions > 0.5 over the prior 20-trading-day window. Rationale: typical equity cross-sectional baseline pairwise correlation is approximately 0.3 in non-stress regimes; > 0.5 indicates regime-driven correlation, which is the specific failure mode KL #12 names (behavioral-irrationality slow-burn correlated drift). The 0.5 threshold is a starting calibration parameter — it is a defensible default but not derived from B-specific empirical data; calibration will be refined as the strategy accumulates a track record. Operational application: requires at least 3 concurrent long positions to compute; below this count the trigger is dormant, an operational threshold unchanged by this revision. ~~(with 1 or 2 longs the metric is undefined or trivially 1)~~ **[Rev 10, cycle 9 AR_orc W3 — struck: mathematically false. With exactly 1 long there is no pair and the metric is genuinely undefined. But with exactly 2 longs the average pairwise correlation over the single available pair of distinct names is simply that pair's own correlation coefficient — a real, well-defined number, not trivially 1 (trivially-1 would require two identical return series, e.g. two tranches of the same name, which is not the general two-long case). The dormancy below 3 longs is therefore a chosen operational threshold, not a mathematical necessity, and it is retained unchanged here — this pre-mortem does not alter the indicator. See Known Limitation #13 for the detection gap this dormancy leaves open at exactly two correlated longs.]**
- **[owner directive 2026-08-05 — LAGGING INDICATOR (b) REMOVED ENTIRELY. It keyed on total long exposure > 10% of strategy portfolio, a threshold calibrated when every position was a fixed 2% (and later ≤10% CaR) tranche. With both CaR envelopes retired, 10% exposure may be one deliberately concentrated thesis or six small ones and the metric cannot tell them apart. Leading indicator (a), average pairwise correlation > 0.5, is now the SOLE KL#12 trigger — and matters more, not less, since correlated positions defeat whatever the per-trade bound is and that bound is now unbounded. Do not reinstate an exposure threshold.]** ~~*(b) Lagging indicator (rev 4 retained as backup, rev 8 recalibrated per cycle 7 orchestrator addition 2):* total long exposure > 10% of strategy portfolio AND count of currently-open B long positions (Section 6 metric (a)) ≥ 2 AND any of Section 4 indicators 1, 3, 4 fires. This catches cases where leading indicator (a) didn't fire but downstream effects appear post-trade.~~ **[Rev 9, cycle 8 AR_orc Tier 1 — struck rather than left standing beneath the removal notice above. Two independent reasons: (i) the metric it keyed on is retired, so it now refers to nothing; (ii) even judged on its own rev-8 terms it did not do what it claimed — the ≥2 conjunction is satisfied by one thesis sized at (or, since rev 19, above) the old ceiling plus one arbitrarily small second position, which is definitionally the non-crowded, single-dominant-thesis case this indicator exists to exclude, not a case it correctly catches. No conjunction, threshold, or replacement metric is substituted for indicator (b); KL#12 has one trigger now, not two, and Section 4 indicators 1/3/4 (hit-rate decay, time-to-convergence drift, convergence-vs-expiry ratio) remain the only post-hoc backstops for a correlated-drawdown episode that indicator (a) misses because it never reached 3 concurrent longs.]**

Cycle 4 T1-α surfaced that rev 4's AND-conjunction was gated on lagging outcome indicators (post-hoc to the failure mode). Rev 5 introduced a leading indicator at "concurrent long mark-to-market drawdown >15% on any single trading day"; cycle 5 T1-B surfaced that this trigger was structurally blind to the named failure mode (slow-burn correlated drift is by construction not a single-day acute-drawdown event). Rev 6 replaces with a realized-correlation metric that fires on the actual regime signal. Cycle 5 T1-C surfaced metric-definition inconsistency between Section 6's "maximum drawdown" wording and KL #12's portfolio-aggregate wording; rev 6 unifies on the correlation metric across both locations. Cycle 7 orchestrator addition 2 surfaced that Rev 43's CaR-sizing change silently invalidated indicator (b)'s discriminating power (a single maximally-sized thesis can now trip the 10%-exposure leg alone, with no crowding); rev 8's (a) ≥ 2 position-count conjunction attempted a fix but did not restore real discrimination (cycle 8 AR_orc Tier 1, independently reproduced with a sharper counterexample: max-size thesis + trivial second position still trips it). Rev 9 does not attempt a further conjunction — indicator (b) is retired outright (consistent with, and completing, the 2026-08-05 owner directive above), and KL#12 relies on indicator (a) alone, with its dormant-below-3-longs limitation stated honestly rather than papered over by a conjunction that could not actually discriminate. Cycle 9 AR_orc found rev 9's own honesty framing itself contained a math error (the "trivially 1" claim above) and, worse, that the framing had been used to wave away a live gap rather than register it; rev 10 corrects the arithmetic at both sites above and, rather than touching indicator (a)'s definition or its dormancy threshold, registers the resulting gap explicitly as Known Limitation #13 below.

13. **KL#12's sole surviving trigger has no active detection at exactly two correlated longs (rev 10 added per cycle 9 AR_orc W3, the decisive item).** With lagging indicator (b) retired (rev 9) and leading indicator (a) dormant below 3 concurrent longs by the trigger's current operational threshold (unchanged by this revision — see the arithmetic correction in Section 6 and in KL #12 trigger (a) above), a book holding exactly two large, positively correlated long B positions has zero active KL#12 detection, and post-rev-19 no numeric per-thesis, per-name, or aggregate ceiling bounds how large those two positions can be. This gap is not a mathematical necessity of the correlation metric itself — at N=2 the metric is well-defined, equal to the single pair's own correlation coefficient, not undefined or trivially 1 — it follows entirely from the dormancy threshold being set at 3, a design choice this pre-mortem does not alter here: making the trigger computable at N=2, or substituting an N=2-capable proxy, would change the definition of a live monitoring indicator, which is machinery, not documentation, and is out of scope for this documentary-only revision. *Status:* accepted, presently-live residual, registered here rather than closed; the machinery question (whether and how to make the trigger N=2-capable) is left for a separate, non-documentary route. *Live condition, measured 2026-08-09 (cited in the triggering AR_orc cycle-9 review):* Strategy B holds exactly two open positions, B:MSCI:2026-07-27 and B:ISRG:2026-07-21; the only backstops active for this book are the −50% deployed-TWR drawdown kill and the 36-month mark-to-market kill (Known Limitation #4), both after-the-fact, high-threshold mechanisms rather than early-warning detection. Review trigger **(rev 11, replacing rev 10's trigger outright per cycle-10 AR_orc W2 — the prior trigger read "shared with KL#12 — if either backstop fires on a two-long book, treat KL#13 as the diagnosed cause rather than logging it solely under KL#12," which was wholly parasitic on two after-the-fact backstops, carried no independent monitoring content of its own, and so broke the field convention every other numbered Known Limitation here follows; it fails screen (iii) because a trigger that only fires when a −50% drawdown or a 36-month m2m kill has *already* fired cannot detect the gap it nominally exists to detect. Per the queued scope caution, the remedy below is an interim compensating control plus a named closure deadline and owner — the correlation indicator itself is NOT redefined, extended, or made N=2-capable by this revision)**, in three parts:
   **(a) Independent monthly observation.** At each monthly review, count B's concurrently open long positions. If the count is exactly 2, record in that month's review that KL#13's detection gap is LIVE for the current book, together with the two positions' combined CaR as a percentage of B's deployed capital. This attests a condition already readable from `state.current_positions`; it defines no new indicator, moves no threshold, and gates no entry or exit.
   **(b) Escalation condition, derived not asserted.** Escalate to design-change reassessment of the N=2 dormancy threshold if (a) records the gap LIVE at three consecutive monthly reviews, **or** if the two positions' combined CaR reaches **50% of B's deployed capital** at any monthly review. The 50% figure is not a fresh parameter: it is the level at which the two positions' *joint total loss alone* reaches the existing −50% deployed-TWR drawdown kill — i.e. the point at which the only remaining backstop is exactly co-extensive with strategy termination, and the after-the-fact detector is doing all of the work. Below that level the backstop retains headroom; at or above it, it does not.
   **(c) Named closure deadline and owner.** The machinery question — whether to make the correlation trigger N=2-capable or substitute an N=2-capable proxy — is owned by the **Strategy B design-change reassessment route** and must be closed at or before **the 30-trade gate (Known Limitation #5's calibration checkpoint) or the next annual A2 per-strategy constraint audit for Strategy B, whichever comes first.** "Closed" means either the trigger is made N=2-capable, or a written decision to leave it dormant, with reasons, is recorded in `events.decision_log`. If that deadline passes with neither, KL#13 is to be re-raised as a Tier 1 item at the next adversarial cycle rather than carried forward unchanged again. The prior "shared with KL#12" attribution rule is retained as a *diagnostic* convention — if either backstop does fire on a two-long book, KL#13 is the diagnosed cause rather than a bare KL#12 entry — but it is no longer this limitation's review trigger.

14. **The router's Fundamental leg is auditable only as recorded judgment, not as a recomputable number (rev 11 added per cycle-10 AR_orc W1).** Section 1's router activation rule has two legs. The Technical leg (SPY Trend State ≠ DOWN AND VIX Regime ≠ HIGH) is Boolean and independently recomputable from stored regime state. The Fundamental leg is a question answered monthly by the M1b routine from M1a's strategy-blind `FUNDAMENTAL_AXIS` scores; its resolver, inputs, cadence and operative effect are now reproduced in Section 1, but it carries **no numeric threshold by design**, and there is no B-specific fundamental data feed against which a past ACTIVATE / DO-NOT-ACTIVATE call could be independently re-derived. Consequence: a reviewer can verify *that* the question was answered, by whom, on what date, and with what stated reasoning (`events.regime_events`, scope `STRATEGY_ACTIVATION`), but cannot recompute *whether* the answer was right except through the same judgment channel that produced it — the same self-reference closure Known Limitation #2 names for 2.4, applied here to the activation decision rather than to a thesis. *Status:* accepted, presently-live residual; this is a property of the two-routine blinded architecture (M1a blind to strategies, M1b blind to raw macro), which is deliberate and is not re-opened here. Quantifying the Fundamental leg would be machinery and is out of scope for a documentary revision. Review trigger: at each 30-trade gate, compare B's realized hit rate across months the Fundamental leg answered ACTIVATE against months it answered DO-NOT-ACTIVATE while the Technical leg was clear (i.e. months where the Fundamental leg alone was decisive). If the two populations show no separation over at least 6 decisive months, the Fundamental leg is not discriminating and should be escalated to design-change reassessment — either given an explicit evaluable basis or removed as a nominal criterion.
