#### 1. Strategy C thesis and mechanism

**Thesis.** AI's narrative synthesis (edge 1.1, which uses cross-report contradiction surfacing 1.4 as one synthesis technique rather than as a separately-load-bearing edge — rev 5 per cycle 4 T1.C) can identify earnings releases, FDA PDUFA decisions, and FOMC meetings where Claude's directional view has meaningful divergence from the options market's pricing of the event. Defined-risk structures cap maximum loss at a pre-committed premium, which is the structural compensation for AI_Edges 2.18 (instruction adherence over capital preservation) — see Section 5's 2.18 entry for the base/cascade precision split within that premium figure (rev 12, 2026-08-10, per AR_orc review `premortem-C-2026-a3` cycle 11 W5). Event-dated options concentrate the thesis into a known resolution window matching Claude's analytical cycle (compensating 2.1 execution latency).

**Instrument eligibility rule.** Any defined-risk options structure around a qualifying event where:
- Maximum loss at expiration is deterministically computable at entry from strike prices and net debit/credit (no reliance on assumptions about future volatility)
- Maximum loss including early-assignment cascade scenarios is also deterministically computable at entry (~~rev 19, rev 20~~ **rev 4** conventions pinned per cycle 3 T1.β) **[rev 13 correction, cycle-12 AR_orc T1-A: "rev 19, rev 20" cited Strategy.md's live owner-directive rev-counter for an event that belongs to THIS pre-mortem's own rev-counter. Cycle 3's T1.β findings were fixed in this artifact's rev 4 — the numbering the document itself uses for the identical event at Known Limitation 9 ("rev 4 conventions pinned per cycle 3 T1.β") and the numbering the cycle-history table implies (rev 3 was reviewed at cycle 3; its fixes landed in rev 4). "Rev 19"/"Rev 20" in this section remain CORRECT wherever they refer to the `Experiment_Parameters.md` rev-19 sizing supersession — that usage is untouched throughout; only the cascade-convention, sell-side and dual-path citations were mislabeled.]**: for any structure with one or more short legs, classical-method delegation must compute the worst-case loss assuming short-leg assignment pre-expiration. Three conventions: mark-to-market reference is at assignment instant; implied-move horizon for the 2× scaling is the full-structure-expiration horizon; multi-expiration variants (calendars, diagonals) are excluded from eligibility.
- Total capital at risk for the structure is ≤ ~~2% of C's strategy portfolio value at entry~~ **the thesis's AI-chosen risk budget (Rev 43, owner directive, 2026-07-28)** ~~, itself bounded by the hard envelopes: per-name aggregate CaR ≤ 10% of C's strategy portfolio value and per-strategy deployed CaR ≤ 75%~~ **[owner directive 2026-08-05 — both hard CaR envelopes RETIRED; no numeric sizing ceiling remains at any level. Read every 'bounded by the envelopes' clause above as superseded: the only surviving sizing discipline is procedural (seven-factor justification + mandatory adversarial attack on size). C's own defined-risk rail — max_loss incl. the early-assignment cascade — is UNCHANGED and still binds the structure to its own declared budget; what is gone is any ceiling on what that budget may be.]** **[rev 12, 2026-08-10, per AR_orc review `premortem-C-2026-a3` cycle 11 W1 — the owner-directive bracket above formerly described this rail as "dual-path verified"; that overstated the cascade component. Accurately: only the base (non-cascade) max-loss component is dual-path verified; the early-assignment cascade component (`cascade_max_loss`) is single-path, regression-tested against golden values only (see Section 5's 2.11/2.12/2.18 entries). Per Strategy.md's Rev 44 addendum to this identical live eligibility rule (2026-08-10, verified over a 16,280-structure randomized sweep of everything `c_options_math.py` can construct, zero counter-examples — **[rev 14 provenance annotation, closing the cycle-13 Tier 3 sweep item: this run searched the repository for that sweep and it is NOT reproducible from anything committed.** The figure originates in commit `84b8efe`'s message (2026-08-10), which states its parameters — credit call/put spreads, iron condors, both butterfly types; DTE 3–45d; vols 10–120%; implied move 1–200%; max observed cascade/base 1.0000000022 — but that commit does not touch `c_options_math.py` at all, and no script, test or output artifact producing 16,280 structures exists at any commit. `tests/test_options_math.py` carries hand-computed golden-value cascade regressions, not a randomized sweep. The same pattern surrounds two unrelated in-code figures (`~5900 randomized structures`, `a 20k-case randomized sweep`), each recorded as prose from an ad-hoc run. **What survives on committed evidence is the ANALYTIC argument, which is stated in full immediately above** — every short leg's payoff is ≤ 0 by construction, so the assignment-scenario loss cannot exceed the structure's own defined-risk maximum — and that argument, not the sweep count, is what this pre-mortem relies on. The sweep should be read as corroboration the reader cannot re-run, not as evidence they can. Committing the sweep script is a repo change outside this documentary route; the identical unsupported figure also stands in the live `## Strategy C` eligibility rule, which route (B) may not edit. **Both are referred forward.]** — `cascade_max_loss` is in practice bounded above by the base figure for every such structure, so the operative `total_max_loss` figure resolves to the dual-path-verified base figure — but that is a proven property of this specific computation, not a description of the cascade component itself carrying dual-path verification.]**, considering both standard max-loss and bounded-early-assignment-loss
- Structure expiration is within 1-45 days of entry
- A qualifying event is scheduled strictly before expiration
- Long calls, long puts, debit spreads, credit spreads, iron condors, butterflies, and their single-expiration variants are permitted if they meet max-loss and sizing constraints. Multi-expiration variants excluded (~~rev 20~~ **rev 4** — rev 13 correction, cycle-12 AR_orc T1-A; same cascade-convention pinning event as the eligibility bullet above).

**Qualifying events.** Corporate earnings releases (US-listed companies, confirmed date from company IR); FDA PDUFA dates (confirmed FDA calendar or company disclosure); FOMC meetings (confirmed Fed calendar). Other event types (analyst days, product launches, conference presentations, M&A-related, legal rulings, index rebalances) are excluded at the strategy level.

**Deferral behavior.** If no structure satisfying eligibility can be constructed for a thesis at current strategy portfolio size, the thesis is deferred. C's strategy portfolio ~~remains in SGOV~~ **remains parked [Rev 38, owner directive, 2026-07-15] — SGOV historically, VOO from the 2026-07-15 cutover forward; see Operating_Protocols.md §13**. As portfolio grows, eligibility progressively expands without rule change. Deferred theses are not logged as missed opportunities.

**Entry criteria (all must be met):**
1. A qualifying event is scheduled within 45 days
2. Claude produces a directional thesis (bullish, bearish, or volatility-directional) synthesized from: last 4 earnings call transcripts, last 10-Q and 10-K filings, recent sell-side report synthesis (retrieved, not recalled), sector peer context. **Sell-side handling clause (~~rev 19~~ **rev 3** per cycle 2 T1.7/T1.8 — rev 13 correction, cycle-12 AR_orc T1-A, one of the two loci the review named as previously unnamed):** theses depending on private-information-class signals (specific channel-check claims, KOL references, management non-public guidance) are inadmissible even when laundered through sell-side reports. Thesis must be reconstructible from primary public sources without sell-side's private-information-derived conclusions as load-bearing rationale.
3. Adversarial counter-argument is produced in the same session before entry; no decisive flaw identified. **[rev 13 added per AR_orc `premortem-C-2026-a3` cycle 12 T1-B — the LOAD-BEARING item, which found the mandatory adversarial attack on size carrying no stated consequence for a negative verdict, this criterion never stating whether a "not acceptable" conclusion is a decisive flaw, and the operative content sourced only to an external document (screens (iii) and (iv) FAIL). **The criterion's own wording above is UNCHANGED.** What follows reproduces the external content inline so this artifact stands alone, and states the consequence that already follows from the criterion as written.**
   **(a) The size attack, reproduced inline** from `Experiment_Parameters.md` §Position size (rev 18, owner directive 2026-07-28): *"Every strategy's existing adversarial counter-argument entry criterion is extended: the counter-argument must attack the size as well as the direction — it must state what the position would cost if the thesis is wrong, and argue whether that loss is acceptable."* For Strategy C, "every strategy's existing adversarial counter-argument entry criterion" **is this criterion 3**. The same section states its own consequences in terms this criterion inherits: *"A size that survives no adversarial attack is not sized, it is asserted,"* and *"a size with no recorded justification is not a valid order."*
   **(b) The consequence of a negative verdict, now stated.** A size attack concluding that the loss is **not acceptable** is a decisive flaw *within this criterion's own words*: the criterion requires that no decisive flaw be identified, the size is part of what is being committed, and a loss the attack itself judges unacceptable is not a lesser finding than a directional one. Therefore **criterion 3 is NOT satisfied at that size, and the entry is not admissible at that size.** The AI may re-derive a smaller risk budget and re-run the attack against the new figure — any re-sized budget carries its own seven-factor justification recorded in the thesis's `events.decision_log` entry — but committing at a size the attack has judged unacceptable is barred. There is no advisory reading under which the entry proceeds unchanged. This adds no gate: it is the only reading this criterion's existing "no decisive flaw" wording and EP's "not sized, it is asserted" language jointly admit, and it introduces no threshold, computation, or rule.
   **(b2) The seven-factor-justification half of the same two-layer control — its consequence, now stated (rev 14 added per AR_orc `premortem-C-2026-a3` cycle 13 T1-2; screen (iii) FAILED).** Part (a) above already reproduces `Experiment_Parameters.md` §Position size's *second* consequence sentence — *"a size with no recorded justification is not a valid order"* — but rev 13 spent that sentence stating the consequence for the **attack** half of the control only, and left the **justification** half, its sibling clause, with no stated consequence at all. Rev 14 states it, and states it the same way, because it is the same criterion and the same governing sentence. **The seven factors, reproduced inline so this criterion stands alone** (identical to the list Section 5 enumerates): *falsifiability of the invalidation criteria; time to resolution; the instrument's loss profile; correlation with the existing book; regime fragility; liquidity and exit-ability at size; and ordinal-only conviction — never an explicit probability in the sizing arithmetic.* **The consequence: a thesis whose `events.decision_log` entry records no seven-factor justification for its stated risk budget has NOT satisfied criterion 3, and the entry is not admissible at that size.** This follows by exactly the reading part (b) applies to the attack half: a budget nobody justified is a size that was *asserted* rather than sized, and EP's own words make an unjustified size "not a valid order" — a disqualifying finding, not a lesser one. A record that names the seven factors without addressing them *for this thesis* is a missing justification for this purpose, not an inadequate one to be weighed; the criterion asks whether the justification exists, not how good it is. The remedy is identical to (b)'s and equally available before entry: record the justification, or re-derive a smaller budget and record that one. There is no advisory reading under which the entry proceeds on an unjustified size.
   **(c) What this does NOT do.** It does not bound how large a budget the attack may clear — post-rev-19 there is no numeric ceiling, and the attack remains a same-model check on a same-model decision, graded rather than eliminative (see Section 5's 2.18 residual, which is unchanged). C's max-loss rail at entry criterion 4 (`max_loss` ≤ the thesis's stated risk budget) is a separate and independent constraint that continues to bind regardless of this criterion's outcome: it bounds the structure against its declared budget, never the budget itself. **[rev 14 addition — the enforcement honesty this annotation owes its reader.]** Neither (b) nor (b2) is enforced by code, and this annotation must not be read as claiming otherwise. `fn_order_guard_options` checks the crafted structure's `max_loss` against the thesis's stated budget plus the malformed-input and defined-risk rails; **it reads no sizing-justification field and no size-attack verdict, and no threshold attaches to either.** The structured sizing fields on the decision-log entry (`car_pct`, `risk_budget_pct`, `capital_at_risk`, `size_pct`) are **record-only** — a 2026-08-05 audit found all four NULL on every thesis-construction and action-conversion row written since Rev 43, which is the measured evidence AR_orc cited at cycle 13. So what (b) and (b2) state is the criterion's correct *reading*, binding on the session that authors the thesis and auditable after the fact from `events.decision_log`; it is not a machine gate, and a session that simply never records either artifact is stopped by nothing but its own discipline. That residual is registered as Known Limitation 13 with its own review trigger (Section 7) and a matching Section 6 monthly item, rather than closed silently here — and authoring a code-level check is machinery, outside this documentary route.]**
4. Classical-method delegation produces: max loss ≤ **the thesis's stated risk budget, which has no numeric ceiling under rev 19** (including bounded early-assignment cascade per the eligibility rule), explicit breakeven(s), and P&L at a minimum of five outcome scenarios. **Dual-path verification (scope corrected rev 12, 2026-08-10, per AR_orc review `premortem-C-2026-a3` cycle 11 W2 — matching Strategy.md's own Rev 44 correction to this identical live clause):** verification of the **base (non-cascade)** max loss must be performed by two independent code paths — closed-form formula and Monte Carlo simulation — and both must agree within $1 before entry; disagreement defers the thesis. The **early-assignment cascade** component (`cascade_max_loss`) has no Monte Carlo counterpart and is single-path, regression-tested against golden values only (see Section 5's 2.11/2.12 entries); for a structure where the cascade term binds, the operative figure `total_max_loss = max(closed_form, cascade)` carries the dual-path guarantee for its non-cascade component alone. Both components are still computed and combined into the figure the options order-guard receives; this correction narrows the redundancy claim, not the computation or the $1 threshold. This rail binds the structure to its declared budget; it does not cap that budget.
5. Entry is at least 1 trading day before the event (no same-day entries — avoids intraday-only thesis windows per 2.2)

**Exit rules.** Default: hold to expiration (defined-risk structure IS the stop; no price-based intervention). Early exit if any of: (a) event resolves earlier than scheduled with thesis resolved; (b) pre-event public news invalidates the thesis (FDA advisory panel vote opposite to expected PDUFA direction, company pre-announces earnings, FOMC inter-meeting action); (c) position reaches ≥ 80% of max profit before expiration (take profit, computed by code).

Not exit-triggering: adverse price movement in underlying with no thesis-invalidating news; general market moves; time decay (theta is priced in at entry).

**Declared expected frequency (rev 6 split per cycle 5 T1.γ, rev 7 transition note added per cycle 6 T2.b; Rev 19 sizing update).** Two declarations corresponding to two router states, with a review-phase note for the post-scope-widening transition:
- *Under current HYBRID-FOMC routing:* 0-8 trades per year. Floor 0 reflects deferral-driven realized frequency at small portfolio sizes; ceiling 8 reflects FOMC-meeting-count cap (Fed schedules 8 meetings per year).
- *Under full-eligibility router state (post scope-widening per KL #12 gate):* 8-15 trades per year. Earnings seasons (4 per year) provide most trades; FDA PDUFA dates and FOMC meetings (8 per year) supplement. Realized frequency is sensitive to portfolio size — at small sizes, many theses defer; as portfolio grows, deferral rate falls and realized frequency climbs within this range.
- *First-five-trade review phase (Rev 19 propagation):* ~~the first 5 earnings/PDUFA trades after scope widening are at 1% sizing per structure, later reframed as half the thesis's otherwise-chosen risk budget~~ **both numeric constraints are retired.** These trades use the normal no-ceiling seven-factor sizing justification and mandatory adversarial attack on size. The phase is a monitoring state, not a separate frequency or sizing state; its trades contribute to the full-eligibility 8-15/year band and receive the n=1 red-flag review in KL #12 option (b).

Cycle 5 T1.γ surfaced that rev 5's single declaration ("8-15 trades/year over active periods") was structurally unreachable under current HYBRID with FOMC-only ceiling of 8; rev 6 disambiguated. Cycle 6 T2.b noted the transition wasn't enumerated; rev 7 added it, and Rev 19 converted it from a sizing state to a review-only phase.

**Router activation rule.** Technical: SPY Trend ≠ DOWN. Fundamental: monthly qualitative judgment. Per cycle-1 divergence review for C completed 2026-04-25, current state is HYBRID ACTIVATE — FOMC events only; corporate earnings, FDA PDUFA, and vol-directional theses across all event types are DO-NOT-ACTIVATE pending M2 review. Effect on this pre-mortem: failure-mode analysis below covers all three event types because the HYBRID state may resolve to full ACTIVATE at M2 or later, but operational trade eligibility currently restricts to FOMC events only.

#### 2. Systematic money-loss scenarios

1. **Options market IV pricing absorbs directional edge.** Even when Claude's directional view is correct, IV priced into the structure at entry is high enough that theta and bid-ask spread eat the expected payoff. Net: correct directional calls with negative P&L.
2. **Event reactions anti-correlated with AI expectations (per 2.8 homogenization).** Synchronized AI positioning drives pre-event prices such that events move against AI consensus on resolution. Claude's thesis is the consensus thesis; the fade is the edge; C is on the wrong side.
3. **Narrative feedback loops.** Earnings calls and filings increasingly contain AI-influenced language (management using AI tools in prep); Claude's synthesis agrees with AI-shaped narratives that don't predict underlying business reality.
4. **Fee-dominated phase at small portfolio scale.** At ≤ $7K total experiment capital, ~~2% sizing × per-strategy allocation~~ **a risk budget of any ordinary size × per-strategy allocation (Rev 43, owner directive, 2026-07-28 — thesis-scaled sizing lets a high-conviction thesis clear the fee-dominated floor that a flat 2% of a small strategy portfolio could not, but does not eliminate the problem at this scale)** produces very small per-structure capital. Most qualifying events produce no executable structures (deferred). The few that execute have transaction costs (commissions, spreads, bid-ask on low-priced options) that swamp alpha. Edge may not be observable until portfolio grows past fee-dominated threshold. This is also identified as a binding-constraint contributor in Section 5.
5. **Code-execution failure on max-loss verification (per 2.12).** C's ~~"max loss ≤ 2%"~~ **"max loss ≤ the thesis's stated risk budget" (Rev 43, owner directive, 2026-07-28)** guarantee is fully delegated to code per entry criterion 4. A bug in the P&L scenario generator or max-loss check converts the entire defined-risk premise into an undefined-risk position while Claude believes the structure is bounded. Highest-leverage failure mode created by the mitigation architecture itself.
6. **Early assignment on credit spreads.** American-style equity options can produce early assignment of the short leg without the long leg being exercised, creating an unhedged equity position briefly. The "defined-risk" claim assumes the structure is held intact through expiration; early assignment is a structural exception to that assumption.
7. **Look-ahead retrieval contamination on historical event analysis (per 2.19).** Entry criterion 2 demands synthesis of historical earnings transcripts and filings. For setups the model has seen post-cutoff, retrieval can substitute for analysis without the model knowing — Claude believes it is analyzing when it may be retrieving the outcome.

#### 3. SGOV-underperformance scenarios

1. **Low-dispersion event regime.** Many events produce small, undifferentiated moves; C enters, pays premium, event produces mild move, expires near zero. Net accumulated premium payments over a season exceed SGOV yield.
2. **Thin-margin NORMAL VIX regime.** C operates with thin structural margins in NORMAL VIX; a few adverse outcomes consume the entire positive-EV buffer.
3. **High short-rate regime.** SGOV's hurdle rises with the short-rate regime; C must outperform a higher benchmark.
4. **Persistent macro-dominated tape (HYBRID-state context).** Per the M1 fundamental DNA reasoning that produced the HYBRID router state, macro-dominated tapes suppress individual-catalyst dispersion for earnings and PDUFA events. While the HYBRID state restricts trades to FOMC events in such regimes, an extended macro-dominated regime caps C's effective trade frequency below the 8-15/year baseline.

#### 4. Edge-decay indicators

- **Primary metric (rev 9 added per cycle 8 T1.ι): cumulative EV per trade < 0 over rolling 10 trades.** Expected value per trade computed as (hit rate × average win) − ((1 − hit rate) × average loss), where wins and losses are measured as P&L per unit of capital at risk. EV per trade is mechanism-agnostic — positive EV = positive edge regardless of structure type or payoff geometry. This metric escapes the structure-classification problem that cycles 7-8 surfaced at the hit-rate threshold surface (long-debit vs. premium-selling structures have different breakeven hit rates that no single threshold can capture, but EV is breakeven-neutral). Cycle 8 T1.ι surfaced that even the rev 8 stratified hit-rate thresholds (45%/60%) produced same-locus residuals on iron condors at typical event-window credit/width ratios; rev 9 makes EV the load-bearing detection metric.
- **Hit rate by structure subset (rev 9 acknowledged as supplementary approximation per cycle 8 T1.λ).** "Hit" defined as: structure closed with positive P&L AND the magnitude prediction the thesis made was directionally correct. For bullish/bearish theses, the underlying moved in the predicted direction by the event's resolution; for long-vol theses, realized vol over the structure's holding period exceeded IV at entry; for short-vol theses, realized vol was less than IV at entry. Trades closed via 80% take-profit count as hits if the magnitude prediction was directionally correct at the close timestamp; trades closed at expiration with positive P&L count as hits if the magnitude prediction was correct over the full holding period. Three thresholds operate in parallel, computed across closed trades segmented by structure type:
  - *Long debit verticals* (long calls, long puts, vertical debit spreads): hit rate < 45% over rolling 10 trades. Derivation: 1:2 to 1:3 risk/reward asymmetry placing breakeven 25-33%; 45% sits in lower-tail margin of positive expectancy.
  - *Butterflies* (long butterflies, narrower profit zone): hit rate < 35% over rolling 10 trades. Rev 9 sub-class added per cycle 8 T1.λ — butterflies have distinct natural hit rate (~30-40%) due to narrow profit zone at the body strike, distinct from vertical debit spreads.
  - *Premium-selling structures* (credit spreads, iron condors, iron butterflies): hit rate < 60% over rolling 10 trades. Rev 9 added iron butterflies under premium-selling per credit-at-entry classification per cycle 8 T1.λ. Derivation: 1:0.5 to 1:1 inverted asymmetry placing breakeven 50-67%.

  *Caveat (rev 9 per cycle 8 T1.ι/T1.λ acknowledgments):* these structure-conditional hit-rate thresholds are approximations derived from typical R/R asymmetry within each class. Within-class variance is real — narrow-credit iron condors at typical event-window R/R (1:0.20-0.30) have breakeven 75-83%, materially above the 60% threshold. The hit-rate signals are supplementary because the EV metric handles the asymmetric-payoff measurement directly. If a hit-rate signal fires below threshold and EV remains positive, the implication is hit rate is below class-typical but realized R/R is favorable enough to compensate; investigate but don't auto-trigger edge-decay flag.
- **Win/loss magnitude ratio < 1.0** over rolling 15 closed trades. With defined-risk structures, max loss ≈ premium paid; max win varies by structure type. Adjusted ratio normalizes for structure type by computing magnitudes as fraction of capital at risk per trade. Less-than-1 ratio over a rolling window indicates that even when thesis is correct, payoff is insufficient relative to losses on incorrect theses.
- **Cumulative excess real return vs. SGOV negative at 15 closed trades.** Lagging confirmation indicator.
- **80%+ take-profit firing rate < 30% of profitable trades.** If most profitable trades are riding to expiration without hitting take-profit, the strategy's expected payoff is being consumed by post-event drift rather than captured at the event resolution point.

#### 5. Specific AI_Edges disadvantages posing greatest risk

- **2.1 (execution latency, rev 4 added per cycle 3 T1.α, rev 5 reframed per cycle 4 T1.A):** Section 1 thesis explicitly names 2.1 as a compensated disadvantage. AI's analysis cycle is on the order of minutes-to-hours, slower than market-making infrastructure. The mitigation has two components with different completeness:
  - *Structural component:* event-dated options concentrate the thesis into a known resolution window matching Claude's analytical cycle. This structurally removes the millisecond-latency competition — Claude doesn't need to react in milliseconds because the trade resolves at the event, not at the next price tick. Entry criterion 5 (≥ 1 trading day before event) further removes intraday-decision pressure. The structural component is complete for the millisecond-latency-competition class.
  - *Partial component:* intraday IV/price drift between Claude's thesis formulation and order execution can move the structure's at-entry parameters, producing a different (worse) entry than the parameters Claude analyzed. The dual-path verification (entry criterion 4) and classical-method delegation re-compute parameters at execution time, but the thesis-quality assessment was based on earlier-time market state.
  *Residual:* intraday-drift adverse-execution exposure, bounded by the gap between thesis-time and execution-time market state (typically minutes for liquid optionable underlyings, but can be longer if thesis formulation occurs overnight pre-market). Detection mechanism: rev 5 adds Section 6 monthly indicator that compares thesis-time vs execution-time at-entry parameters across closed trades; flag if cumulative average adverse drift > 0.2% of capital at risk per trade. Cycle 4 T1.A surfaced that rev 4's "structural, not partial" framing was internally contradictory with the residual; rev 5 separates the components.
- **2.4 (narrative over-fit on event-outcome direction):** entry criterion 3 is a graded discriminator with substantial structural residual. Rev 19 removed the former magnitude backstop; the seven-factor record and size attack are judgment-dependent. *Residual:* post-hoc detection through hit-rate decay.
- **2.6 (no access to private information):** load-bearing for earnings and PDUFA theses. Public-information-only construction is the strategy's ceiling. Direct private-information access is impossible; indirect access via sell-side report retrieval (entry criterion 2) is bounded by the **rev 3** sell-side handling clause that forbids private-information-derived conclusions as load-bearing rationale. Qualifying-event whitelist separately excludes 2.6-heavy event types (analyst days, M&A, channel-check-driven setups). Mitigation: **rev 3** sell-side handling clause + qualifying-event whitelist (see Constraint 1). **[rev 13, cycle-12 AR_orc T1-A sweep — TWO loci NOT named by the review: both citations here read "rev 19 sell-side handling clause," propagating the same mislabel the review found at the clause itself. Constraint 1 already cites the identical event correctly as "rev 3 reframed per cycle 2 T1.7/T1.8."]** Residual: structural disadvantage on earnings/PDUFA where direct private-information edge dominates; FOMC less exposed.
- **2.8 (homogenization on commoditized AI strategy):** AI-driven event trades are crowded.   - **Early-assignment cascade component** — computed by `cascade_max_loss`, which the eligibility rule requires be folded in via `total_max_loss = max(closed_form, cascade)` whenever it is the larger figure (any structure with a short leg where the assignment-scenario loss exceeds the base loss — credit spreads, iron condors, butterflies, and debit spreads are exposed by construction, since all four carry a short leg under the eligibility rule's general trigger; a debit spread's short leg is covered by a further-out-of-the-money long leg rather than sold naked, but the eligibility rule's "for any structure with one or more short legs" trigger and `c_options_math.py`'s own `short_legs = [leg for leg in structure.legs if leg.quantity < 0]` filter do not exempt it — rev 12, 2026-08-10, per AR_orc review `premortem-C-2026-a3` cycle 11 W6, corrected from a list that omitted debit spreads). This component is single-path (regression-tested against golden values, not cross-checked against an independent second implementation — see the 2.11/2.12 entries above) and, unlike the base loss, it IS assumption-dependent: it rests on the 2× implied-move adverse-move scaling that Known Limitation 9 documents as a model parameter which actual adverse moves can exceed. Where the cascade term is the operative figure, the CaR number is **not** "exactly known... not assumed" in the sense the base-loss language correctly claims for itself — it is a single-path computation built on the same adverse-move assumption KL9 already flags. *(rev 12, 2026-08-10 — noted for completeness, not changing the above: Strategy.md's Rev 44 addendum to the live eligibility rule (2026-08-10) establishes, over a 16,280-structure randomized sweep of everything `c_options_math.py` can construct with zero counter-examples, that the cascade term is in practice never the operative figure — `cascade_max_loss` is provably bounded above by the base loss for every constructible structure. The single-path/assumption-dependent characterization above remains accurate as a description of the cascade computation itself; this sweep result narrows how often that computation is the one actually load-bearing for CaR, not what the computation is.)*

  So: "exactly known at entry, not assumed" is an accurate description of C's sizing control relative to the equity strategies' notional exposure — but it describes the base max-loss specifically, not `max_loss` as a whole whenever the cascade term binds. It does **not** make the CHOICE of how much CaR to spend on a given thesis any less judgment-dependent either way — 2.18 is a disadvantage about judgment overriding capital preservation, not about arithmetic, and the budget — unconstrained by any numeric envelope since both were retired (owner directive 2026-08-05, see the bracketed annotation above) — is now an output of that same judgment alone. The options order-guard still enforces `max_loss ≤ stated risk budget` mechanically at order-craft time — that machinery rail is unchanged by Rev 43 — but the number it enforces against is itself AI-chosen rather than fixed, and now has no ceiling at any level.

  Two layers now do 2.18's compensating work, with a materially larger residual than the retired cap left:
  - ~~*Outer layer — still mechanical, still bias-independent:* the ≤10%-per-name / ≤75%-per-strategy-deployed hard envelopes. Every surviving control is procedural and therefore AI-dependent — the seven-factor justification and the mandatory adversarial attack on size — which is exactly the control class 2.13 (miscalibration), 2.18 (instruction adherence over capital preservation) and 2.26 (RL-induced overconfidence) warn is unreliable, and which A2 2026 confirmed is not improving. The owner accepted this trade-off explicitly: with no stop-losses, size IS the risk lever, and the AI is trusted to set it.]** **[rev 17 self-containment repair, 2026-08-30, per AR_orc `premortem-C-2026-a3` cycle 16 Tier 3. That is precisely why it belongs in this enumeration: a procedural control operated BY the same model — a seven-factor justification it writes, an adversarial size attack it grades — cannot be prompted or instructed out of the bias it exists to check.   - *Inner layer — judgment-dependent, and therefore 2.18-exposed:* the AI-chosen risk budget itself, disciplined by (a) a fixed seven-factor justification (falsifiability of invalidation criteria, time to resolution, instrument loss profile, correlation with the existing book, regime fragility, liquidity/exit-ability at size, and ordinal-only conviction — never an explicit probability in the sizing arithmetic) recorded in the thesis's decision-log entry, and (b) a mandatory adversarial attack on the size: per `Experiment_Parameters.md`'s sizing policy, the existing adversarial counter-argument requirement (entry criterion 3) is extended so the counter-argument must also attack the size — state what the position would cost if the thesis is wrong and argue whether that loss is acceptable.

  *Residual (rev 19 propagation — quantified, not eliminated):* with both envelopes retired, a 2.18-driven sizing failure can allocate up to the full strategy portfolio to one thesis. The adversarial size attack is a same-model check on a same-model decision—graded, not eliminative—and the seven-factor record improves measurement, not magnitude control. C's max-loss rail still ensures the structure does not exceed its own declared budget — dual-path verified for the rail's base, non-cascade component; single-path for the cascade component (rev 12, 2026-08-10, per AR_orc review `premortem-C-2026-a3` cycle 11 W1 — see the base/cascade split above) — but nothing caps the budget itself. Kill triggers detect the resulting loss after the fact.
- **2.19 (look-ahead bias on historical event analysis):** entry criterion 2's historical-transcript synthesis can produce retrieval-substituting-for-analysis for setups in the model's training data. Claude believes it is analyzing when it may be retrieving the outcome. Mitigation: none implemented at strategy level. Residual: structural; see Known Limitations.
- **2.7 (regime maladaptation):** options behave differently across market regimes. Mitigation: router technical signal SPY Trend ≠ DOWN partially deactivates in down regimes; HYBRID router state (post-cycle-1 divergence review) restricts to FOMC events in macro-dominated tapes. Residual: regime types underrepresented in training data are not router-detectable.

**Binding core-edge constraints (req 7 confrontation):**

C's core edge is AI narrative synthesis evaluating directional likelihood of event outcomes against options-market IV pricing. Three distinct constraints materially affect this edge.

*Cross-constraint flowing limitation (rev 19 propagation).* The AI-chosen risk budget has no numeric ceiling and therefore provides no mechanical magnitude bound against the 2.1/2.4/2.13/2.15/2.18 confluence. The surviving sizing controls are procedural: the seven-factor justification and mandatory adversarial size attack. C's defined-risk structure still guarantees max loss—including the bounded early-assignment cascade—is no greater than the declared budget. *(rev 12, 2026-08-10, per AR_orc review `premortem-C-2026-a3` cycle 11 W1 — corrected: "dual-path verified within $1" above overstated the cascade component's coverage. The base, non-cascade component of that guarantee is dual-path verified within $1; the early-assignment cascade component is single-path, regression-tested against golden values only (see Section 5's 2.11/2.12/2.18 entries).)* This bounds execution to the declaration, not the declaration's size.

*Constraint 1 — 2.6 no access to private information (rev 3 reframed per cycle 2 T1.7/T1.8).* C's earnings and PDUFA universe overlaps directly with the domain where private-information channels (channel checks, expert calls, KOL networks, management access) typically provide directional edge to professional investors. C cannot access any of these directly. Furthermore, sell-side reports retrieved per entry criterion 2 may incorporate analyst channel-check intelligence, expert-call notes, and management-meeting context — providing indirect 2.6 access through analyst laundering; this indirect access is bounded above by the quality of primary research and willingness to publish private-information-derived conclusions, but it is non-zero. *Flowing limitation (rev 3 per cycle 2 T1.8):* entry criterion 2's sell-side handling clause forbids theses where sell-side's private-information-derived conclusions are the load-bearing rationale; thesis must be reconstructible from primary public sources without sell-side analyst-laundered private content. This is a strategy-design addition derived from 2.6 confrontation, not a relabel of the pre-existing qualifying-event whitelist (which separately excludes 2.6-heavy event types like analyst days and M&A). *Residual:* structural disadvantage on earnings and PDUFA theses where private information dominates IV-priced expectations remains; FOMC theses are less 2.6-exposed because Fed signaling is primarily public. See Known Limitations.

*Constraint 2 — 2.4 narrative over-fit on event-outcome direction.* C's mechanism is narrative synthesis evaluating directional likelihood. Self-reference closure: Claude's only at-entry discriminator on whether the thesis is over-fit is Claude's own assessment, which is biased by 2.4. *Flowing limitation:* entry criterion 3 (adversarial counter-argument as graded discriminator, ~30% empirical magnitude reduction; ~70% residual). *Residual:* the ~70% post-counter-argument bias is structural; post-hoc detection via Section 4 hit-rate decay. **[rev 15 provenance flag, 2026-08-24, AR_orc `premortem-C-2026-a3` cycle 15 T1-4 — the ~30%/~70% split above is UNSOURCED. It originates in `AI_Trading_Foundation.md` §1.3's unattributed sentence ("the bias is only reduced by about 30% — it is hard-wired in the model's weights"), uncited since the repository's first commit (`e7be4c0`, 2026-05-08); A1's 2026-07-28 annual re-derivation searched for a source at all four evidence levels with two independent agents and recorded it ABSENT (`Annual_AI_Foundation_Sweep.md` §C.3), remedy NEW RESEARCH — not a replication on the current model line — and `AI_Trading_Foundation.md` §§1.3 and 2.4 both carry that flag verbatim. 2.4's EXISTENCE claim and the narrative-over-fit MECHANISM are unaffected; only the MAGNITUDE is unsupported. Read ~30%/~70% here as an unsourced working assumption, not a measurement. This is this strategy's primary 2.4 locus, since Section 5's own 2.4 entry does not restate the figure. Swept identically across all five strategy pre-mortems' primary 2.4 loci in the same pass rather than only this locus and Strategy E's (`premortem-E-2026-a3` cycle 11 T1-1).]** See Known Limitations.

*Constraint 3 — 2.12 code-execution dependence on the mitigation architecture itself (rev 3 flowing limitation added per cycle 2 T1.3).* The mitigation for 2.11 (numerical precision) and the loss-bounding claim are both code-delegated. *(rev 10, 2026-08-03 — superseded figure: this sentence previously stated the loss-bounding claim as "max loss ≤ 2%". This code-delegation point is unaffected by the figure's retirement: whichever number `max_loss` must stay under, the computation of `max_loss` itself is still code-delegated — the redundancy question this constraint addresses is about that computation, not about the cap's value.)* A bug in the code stack performing max-loss checks, a wrong-input call construction, or Claude misinterpreting code output could in principle convert defined-risk into undefined-risk while Claude believes the structure is bounded. *Flowing limitation:* entry criterion 4's dual-path verification clause requires max-loss verification by two independent code paths (closed-form formula AND Monte Carlo P&L scenario simulation) that must agree to within $1 per structure before entry. Disagreement defers the thesis. The redundancy bounds 2.12 risk **for the max-loss computation specifically** to scenarios where both paths share the same bug or both receive the same wrong input — a substantially smaller failure surface than single-path verification. *(rev 10, 2026-08-03 — scope note: breakeven and the P&L-scenario set, named as code-delegated at the top of this paragraph, are single-path — see the 2.11/2.12 entries in Section 5. The redundancy bound described here does not extend to them.)* *(rev 11, 2026-08-06 — further scope note: even "for the max-loss computation specifically" above overstates the redundancy bound's reach. Dual-path verification covers the base, non-cascade max-loss (`max_loss_closed_form`/`max_loss_monte_carlo`) only. The early-assignment cascade term (`cascade_max_loss`), folded into the operative figure via `total_max_loss = max(closed_form, cascade)` whenever it is the binding term (any structure with a short leg where the assignment-scenario loss exceeds the base loss), is single-path — regression-tested (`tests/test_options_math.py`) but not independently cross-checked. For a structure where the cascade term binds, the redundancy bound described here does not extend to the operative max-loss figure. See the 2.18 entry for the full reconciliation with Known Limitation 9's 2×-implied-move-assumption caveat.)* *Residual:* for the base (non-cascade) max-loss — shared-bug failure across both computation paths (e.g., wrong strike price input to both); shared-environment failure (e.g., wrong underlying ticker, wrong expiration date used for both); operator misreading of agreed-upon output. For breakeven/P&L scenarios, and for the early-assignment cascade component of max-loss when it binds — the full single-path failure surface, not narrowed to shared-failure scenarios. Review trigger: at each monthly review, manually verify max-loss computation on at least one closed trade; any discrepancy escalates immediately (KL #8).

*Account-scale constraint (acknowledged but not elevated to formal Constraint 4).* At current $6,946 total experiment capital, ~~2% per-strategy allocation~~ **[Rev 43 retired the flat 2%; Rev 19 then retired the 10%/75% successor envelopes. C now uses an AI-chosen Capital-at-Risk budget with no numeric ceiling. Those theses defer per the deferral mechanism. This is captured in money-loss scenario 4 and Known Limitation 1.

Two things were lost: the "(currently <0 over rolling 10 trades)" parenthetical on (a), and KL 11's two DISTINCT qualifying clauses — "based on realized EV distribution" for (a), "based on realized hit-rate distribution within the corresponding structure subset" for (b) — which had been collapsed into a single shared "against the realized distribution".     Strictly documentary — this bullet makes the existing KL 4 / KL 7 / KL 11 / KL 13 15-trade-gate readings reachable from the checklist a monthly session actually executes; it invents no new indicator, no new threshold, no new magnitude and no new consequence verb, and it gates no entry. It also assigns no owner: KLs 4, 7 and 11 remain UNOWNED (see the rev-17 scope statement at the ownership-sweep note above), because a trade-count gate has no monthly-cadence executor to inherit and naming one would originate a monitoring obligation this document states nowhere. **[rev 18, 2026-09-02, per AR_orc `premortem-C-2026-a3` cycle 17 T1-1 (sustained core) — "REACHABLE FROM THE CHECKLIST A MONTHLY SESSION ACTUALLY EXECUTES" IS STRUCK FROM THIS BULLET, AND WHAT IS TRUE IN ITS PLACE IS WORSE FOR THIS BULLET THAN THE STRUCK CLAIM WAS. This is the canonical statement for the unowned side of the correction; the Owner bracket on Known Limitation 13 is its counterpart for the owner-assigned side. **The struck claim.** Revs 15 and 17 built this bullet on the premise that Section 6 is the surface a monthly session mechanically executes and Section 7 is not. This document never established that premise — every stated fact about M4's reading behaviour in the reviewed range is a read-scope grant, and not one of them says Section 6 — and against the live plan the premise was false: at the time it was written, no step of M4's, and no step of any routine in `Claude_Task_Plan.md`, walked any pre-mortem Section 6. What this bullet CAN support is the narrower statement, which is what it now makes: **Section 6 is the section THIS DOCUMENT designates as its monthly checklist, and consolidating a reading here places it where an executor can be assigned.** Whether any routine walks it is a fact about that routine's spec, established there or not at all. **What is established, as of 2026-09-02.** `Claude_Task_Plan.md` §M4 section I (:3257, added 2026-09-01) walks the M4-assigned loci in this file every fire — and it excludes THIS bullet, by name and deliberately: "Section 6 bullets that name no owner are DELIBERATELY out of scope and must NOT be swept in … that includes Strategy C's hit-rate, take-profit-firing-rate and intraday-drift bullets and its `[At 15 closed trades]` consolidation (which states outright that 'KLs 4, 7 and 11 remain UNOWNED')." Section I cites this bullet's own no-owner sentence as its reason for skipping it, which is correct — assigning an executor here would originate a monitoring obligation, which is machinery and out of route — and leaves the plain consequence: **the KL 4, KL 7 and KL 11 readings consolidated here are executed by no routine in the fleet.** The KL 13 reading in this same bullet is the one exception, and only because KL 13 carries its own M4 assignment. **So this bullet did not make three of its four readings fire, and it cannot.** It makes them legible in one place and gives them a locus an owner could attach to. That is worth having and it is all this is. Rev 15 said "reachable"; rev 17 said "reachable from the checklist a monthly session actually executes"; both overstated, and this bracket is the correction rather than a further claim. **Nothing is invented to close the gap** — no window, magnitude, consequence verb, sample method or owner. The gap is the owner's, is escalated at its own locus (`ops.alerts` `premortem_live_gate_defect` `6c670d35-c44a-416d-b3e6-73209e66fc3f`, whose resolution note refers the fleet-wide residue separately as `premortem_indicator_layer_unowned`), and is disclosed here rather than papered over.]**
- [At 20 closed trades] If cumulative excess real return < -5%, flag for design-change reassessment in the monthly review.
- [At 30 closed trades] Gate evaluation per Experiment_Parameters.md.

The monthly-review-cadence indicators above operate continuously regardless of trade-count milestones; the trade-count gates supplement but do not replace monthly cadence. Cycle 1 surfaced that rev 1's trade-count-only anchoring failed req 6; rev 2 establishes monthly-cadence indicators alongside trade-count gates.

#### 7. Known limitations

1. **Fee-dominated phase at small strategy-portfolio scale.** Accepted; resolves automatically as portfolio grows. Review trigger: at each monthly review, if > 80% of qualifying events deferred, flag for portfolio-growth gate evaluation. **Owner: M4 (Monthly Action Conversion)** — **[rev 16, 2026-08-25, per AR_orc `premortem-C-2026-a3` cycle 15 T2-3. 2. **2.6 (no private information) residual on earnings and PDUFA theses.** Public-information-only construction means Claude is competing against private-information-equipped professionals on event types where channel-check/KOL/management-access sources dominate. Residual is structural for earnings and PDUFA. FOMC theses are less exposed (Fed signaling primarily public). *Status:* accepted residual. Review trigger: if hit rate on earnings and PDUFA theses (separately tracked) < 40% over 10 closed trades each, escalate to design-change reassessment with potential universe restriction. **[rev 16, 2026-08-25, per AR_orc `premortem-C-2026-a3` cycle 15 T2-3 — this trigger names no executing owner and none is assigned here, deliberately. Unlike Known Limitation 1's, its condition is trade-count-anchored ("over 10 closed trades each") and no Section 6 checklist bullet carries it, so naming an executor would originate a monitoring obligation this document states nowhere — machinery, outside this documentary route, and the same ground on which rev 14 declined to invent a magnitude for KL 3. The gap is DISCLOSED rather than closed: as written, this trigger is evaluated only by whichever session next reads this limitation, which is not a scheduled surface, so it can go unfired indefinitely. Closing it means adding a Section 6 bullet with a named owner; that is a machinery change and stays escalated.]**

3. **2.4 + 2.8 residuals at the strategy level.** Per Constraints 2 and 3 confrontation. Review trigger: hit-rate decline pattern across consecutive 10-trade windows.

4. **2.13 miscalibration on directional theses.** No mitigation beyond entry criterion 3 partial filter. *Status:* accepted residual. Review trigger: at 15-trade gate, compare realized hit rate against entry-time-stated thesis confidence; if confidence systematically exceeds realized, calibrate. **[rev 17 disclosure, 2026-08-30, per AR_orc `premortem-C-2026-a3` cycle 16 Tier 3, written in the manner rev 16 disclosed Known Limitation 2's gap rather than closing it. That is not the same as FIREABLE, and the difference is stated here so the two are not conflated. As written this trigger specifies: no **window** (over how many closed trades, or what span, the comparison is taken); no **test** (no statistical or arithmetic comparison is named — "compare" is the only instruction); no **magnitude** (no gap between stated confidence and realized hit rate separates a fire from a pass, which is what "systematically" is doing the work of and cannot do); and no **consequence** ("calibrate" escalates nothing, records nothing, halts nothing and re-derives nothing that this document specifies). The honest consequence: a session reading this line can reach either verdict in good faith, and a miss leaves no trace anywhere — so this trigger cannot be relied on as a live control on 2.13, which matters because 2.13 is one of the three disadvantages Section 5's 2.18 entry names as making C's surviving procedural sizing controls unreliable. No window, test, magnitude or consequence verb is invented to close it: that is machinery, outside this documentary route, on the same ground rev 14 used declining to invent a magnitude for KL 3 and rev 16 used declining to assign KL 2 an owner, and the cycle-16 trigger context names it OUT OF SCOPE explicitly. This trigger also remains UNOWNED — see the rev-17 scope statement at the ownership-sweep note above.]** **[rev 18, 2026-09-02, per AR_orc `premortem-C-2026-a3` cycle 17 T1-1 (sustained core) — the disclosure above is RIGHT about this trigger's own defects and WRONG about the one thing it credits rev 17 with, so the credit is withdrawn and the defects stand. **Withdrawn:** "so the trigger is now REACHABLE from the checklist a monthly session executes." Consolidating this reading into Section 6 placed it in the section THIS DOCUMENT designates as its monthly checklist; it did not make any session read it. `Claude_Task_Plan.md` §M4 section I (:3257, added 2026-09-01) — the only step in the plan that walks any locus of this file — excludes unowned Section 6 bullets by name and names the `[At 15 closed trades]` consolidation among them, so this reading is walked by no routine. The two defects compound rather than substitute: a trigger no routine reads, which even a routine that read it could satisfy either way. **Nothing is invented to close either half** — a window, test, magnitude or consequence verb is machinery, and an executor for a trade-count gate would originate a monitoring obligation this document states nowhere. 5. **2.14 recency-weighting on input data uncalibrated.** 4-quarter input window provides minimal historical anchor. *Status:* accepted residual.

6. **2.15 base-rate neglect on event prediction.** No base-rate gate at entry. *Status:* accepted residual.

7. **2.19 look-ahead bias on historical event analysis.** Retrieval-substituting-for-analysis on setups in training data. *Status:* accepted residual. Review trigger: at 15-trade gate, audit subset of closed theses for retrieval-vs-analysis attribution; if > 30% appear retrieval-driven, escalate.

8. **Code-execution dependence on max-loss verification (per Constraint 3, rev 3 mitigated; scope corrected rev 12, 2026-08-10, per AR_orc review `premortem-C-2026-a3` cycle 11 W3).** Rev 3 added dual-path verification: two independent code paths (closed-form formula + Monte Carlo simulation) must agree to within $1 per structure before entry. This bounds 2.12 risk to shared-bug failures across both paths, shared-environment input errors, or operator output misreading — **for the base (non-cascade) max-loss component only.** The early-assignment cascade component (`cascade_max_loss`) is single-path — regression-tested against golden values, not cross-checked against an independent second implementation (see Section 5's 2.11/2.12 entries and Constraint 3) — so for a structure where the cascade term binds, this control's shared-failure-only bound does NOT extend to the operative max-loss figure; the monthly manual verification below is that component's sole independent check, not a third path atop two. *Status:* mitigated at strategy level via flowing limitation, for the base component; for the cascade component, the residual is the full single-path failure surface (per Constraint 3), narrowed only by this review trigger's monthly manual check, not by shared-bug reasoning. Review trigger: at each monthly review, manually verify max-loss computation on at least one closed trade (third independent verification path for the base component; the only independent verification path for the cascade component on a cascade-bound trade); if any discrepancy found between either code path's output and manual computation, escalate immediately.

9. **Early assignment on credit spreads, butterflies, iron condors (rev 3 bounded per cycle 2 T1.4, rev 4 conventions pinned per cycle 3 T1.β).** Eligibility-rule bounded early-assignment-cascade computation now has three pinned conventions: mark-to-market reference at assignment instant; full-structure-expiration horizon for the 2× implied-move scaling; multi-expiration variants excluded. *Status:* mitigated at strategy level via eligibility tightening with pinned conventions. *Residual:* (a) the 2× implied-move adverse-move assumption is a model parameter; actual adverse moves can exceed 2× in extreme regimes; (b) the assignment-instant convention assumes the operator/code closes the resulting equity position immediately at the worst-plausible-adverse mark — operational delays in closing the position can produce additional drift loss; (c) multi-expiration exclusion materially reduces strategy working space (calendars, diagonals removed from eligibility). Review trigger: any early-assignment event with realized loss exceeding the at-entry bounded computation escalates to design-change reassessment. **[rev 16, 2026-08-25, per AR_orc `premortem-C-2026-a3` cycle 15 T2-3 — this trigger names no executing owner, and unlike Known Limitations 1 and 2 it does not require a scheduled one: it fires on a specific observable event (an early-assignment whose realized loss exceeds the at-entry bounded computation), which the session handling that assignment necessarily observes at the moment it occurs, and Section 6's Code-stack health check independently escalates any max-loss discrepancy immediately per Known Limitation 8. Recorded so that the asymmetry with KL 13's explicit "Owner: M4" reads as deliberate rather than as an omission of the same class as KL 2's.]**

10. **2.18 cumulative cross-structure adherence drift.** Per-trade defined-risk caps catastrophic individual loss but cumulative drain across many bad theses bounded only by 30-trade gate. *Status:* accepted residual.

11. **Edge-decay metric calibration refinement at 15-trade gate (rev 19 per cycle 2 T1.6, rev 8 stratified per cycle 7 T1.θ, rev 9 EV-primary per cycle 8 T1.ι/T1.λ).** Section 4 primary metric is EV per trade (mechanism-agnostic). Supplementary signals are structure-conditional hit-rate thresholds (45% long debit verticals; 35% butterflies; 60% premium-selling) acknowledged as approximations. At 15 closed trades, calibrate-refine: (a) the EV threshold (currently <0 over rolling 10 trades) based on realized EV distribution; (b) each structure-conditional hit-rate threshold based on realized hit-rate distribution within the corresponding structure subset; (c) confirm or revise the EV-primary / hit-rate-supplementary architecture based on which signal correlates more reliably with subsequent realized excess return. Rev 9 acknowledged that no clean structure-classification scheme captures the full payoff-geometry space at this resolution depth — within-class variance is real, especially for narrow-credit iron condors with breakeven 75-83% above the 60% threshold. EV-primary architecture is the saturation-stop response to that fact.

12. **HYBRID router state restricts current trade eligibility to FOMC events (rev 5 scope-widening gate added per cycle 4 T1.D, rev 6 adjudication and option (b) procedurally specified per cycle 5 T1.α, rev 7 inline-specified and live-small reframed per cycle 6 T1.δ/T1.ε).** Pre-mortem failure modes 2-7 cover all event types because HYBRID may resolve to full ACTIVATE at M2 or later. Operational trade eligibility is currently FOMC-only per Decision_Log entry 2026-04-25.

    *Scope-widening gate.* Section 5's 2.6 entry notes that FOMC theses are less 2.6-exposed than earnings/PDUFA because Fed signaling is primarily public. Joint implication: Section 6 indicators measured during the HYBRID-FOMC-only period probe the strategy's behavior under the regime where 2.6 is least binding. A green-light reading on Section 6 indicators during HYBRID is therefore uninformative about whether 2.6 will bind on earnings or PDUFA theses when the router widens.

    *HYBRID-period observations cannot authorize scope widening to earnings or PDUFA theses.* If the M2 or later fundamental update produces a router-state change that would re-include earnings/PDUFA in the eligible event set, the scope-widening decision requires a separate gate evaluation conditional on either:

    (a) **Post-HYBRID fundamental update reasoning that explicitly addresses 2.6 binding behavior on earnings and PDUFA event types** — including engagement with the cross-sectional dispersion empirical check (per Decision_Log Strategy C divergence review M2 follow-up), the sell-side handling clause's effective discrimination on earnings/PDUFA inputs, and the projected hit-rate-baseline differential between event types. *Adjudication mechanism (rev 7 inline specification per cycle 6 T1.ε, rev 8 routine architecture migration):* the post-HYBRID fundamental update document is subject to an Adversarial Review Attacker + Orchestrator routine pair per `Claude_Task_Plan.md` (queue entry type `scope-widening-adjudication`) — same operational structure as pre-mortem reviews and divergence reviews under routine architecture, applied here to scope-widening gate adjudication as a contained extension of the same protocol pattern. The attacker routine attacks whether the fundamental reasoning has adequately addressed the three required topics; orchestrator routine applies the four-screen test to the attacker output and emits a theater-check flag. Re-widening is authorized only on CONVERGENT theater-check + Tier 1 clean (or deployment-risk-acceptable per the four screens). Rev 6 referenced "EP rev 14 protocol" without verifying scope coverage; cycle 6 T1.ε surfaced that EP rev 14 §362's enumeration is closed-list (pre-mortem / divergence / termination / redistribution), not covering scope-widening adjudication. Rev 7 specified the mechanism inline; rev 8 updates the operational reference to the routine architecture without changing substantive policy. *(rev 2026-07-10 — Strategy Arsenal autonomy conversion, owner directive.)* The `Experiment_Parameters.md` review-type enumeration has since been extended to a superset that adds `scope-widening-adjudication`, `strategy-adoption` (conservative default REJECT), `strategy-retirement` (conservative default KEEP), and `out-of-table-resolution` (conservative default HOLD-current-state); scope-widening adjudication is now a first-class member of that list rather than a contained inline extension, though the inline mechanism above is retained for self-containment.

    (b) **Post-portfolio-growth re-evaluation when the strategy can actually execute non-FOMC trades.** *Conditions:* (i) at least 5 closed FOMC trades have produced cumulative excess return data sufficient to establish a hit-rate baseline for the strategy's mechanism on its currently-tradeable scope; AND (ii) ~~2% sizing × strategy-portfolio-allocation~~ **[Rev 43 retired the flat 2%; Rev 19 retired the 10%/75% successor envelopes. The test is whether C's AI-chosen, no-ceiling CaR budget at the then-current strategy portfolio value clears the chain's liquid minimums. The ~$25,000 figure ~~below~~ **[rev 16 correction, 2026-08-25, per AR_orc `premortem-C-2026-a3` cycle 15 T2-1 — "below" pointed at nothing. The string "25,000" occurs exactly ONCE in this section, inside this bracket itself; no such figure is reproduced anywhere below it, so a reader following the pointer found no referent. The figure is deliberately NOT restated or re-derived here: its derivation under the retired flat-2% rule is not recoverable from this document's own text, and supplying one would originate a number rather than describe it. It is named only to record that a 2%-era portfolio-scale reference once existed and is now historical. Nothing in this section reads it and it gates nothing; the operative test is the clause that follows.]** was derived under 2% sizing and is retained only as a historical reference point, not a threshold.]** enables liquid options-chain entries on representative earnings and PDUFA names. *First-five-trade review phase — detection, not a sizing cap (Rev 19 propagation):* the former 1%-per-structure "live-small" ceiling is retired with every other numeric sizing ceiling. The first 5 earnings/PDUFA structures use the normal seven-factor justification and mandatory adversarial attack on size, then receive the explicit per-trade adverse-trigger monitoring below. At n=5 the 95% CI on hit rate is approximately ±35 percentage points, so this phase cannot statistically compare the non-FOMC subset with the FOMC baseline; it is limited to red flags detectable at n=1:

    - (i) Any single first-five-phase trade realizes a loss exceeding the at-entry max-loss bound. This indicates a catastrophic structural break — either a 2.12 code-execution failure on max-loss verification (the bound was computed wrong) or a 2.19 retrieval contamination producing an unanticipated event scenario, or an early-assignment cascade exceeding the rev 4 bounded computation, or some other defect not contemplated by the eligibility rule. Reverts immediately to HYBRID-FOMC-only and triggers full design-change reassessment per Section 7.
    - (ii) (rev 8 reframed n=1 per cycle 7 T1.ζ, rev 9 review process specified per cycle 8 T1.κ) Any single first-five-phase trade where the participant catches a sell-side-handling-clause violation or input-source contamination after entry triggers immediate review. *Review process (rev 9):* review is conducted by the participant within 5 trading days of the trigger firing. Evidentiary standard: documentary check that the entry-criterion-2 inputs included the alleged violation source (e.g., the sell-side report containing the channel-check claim was retrieved, the thesis cites the report, and the violation language is present in the report). Disposition if review is inconclusive: defaults to "violation confirmed" (conservative bias on review). If review confirms violation, reverts to HYBRID and triggers entry-criterion-2 procedural review at the strategy-design level. False-flag filtering occurs at the review stage rather than the trigger-firing stage.
    - (iii) (rev 8 affirmation procedure specified per cycle 7 T1.η; rev 2026-07-10 automated per the Strategy Arsenal autonomy conversion, owner directive) End-of-first-five-trade review: after the 5th trade closes, a `scope-widening-adjudication` Adversarial Review (Attacker + Orchestrator routine pair per `Claude_Task_Plan.md`, fresh independent context) authors and evaluates a summary memo covering each red-flag category (consistent thesis-time vs execution-time IV gaps suggesting 2.1 residual exceeding tolerance; recurring 2.19 retrieval-vs-analysis ambiguity flags; 2.6 sell-side-laundering pattern detection; any other disadvantage manifesting more strongly than expected). Unrestricted-scope continuation requires an affirmative AR_orc "no red flags identified" verdict on a CONVERGENT theater-check with Tier 1 clean; the conservative default is preserved — default-to-no-objection is NOT sufficient, the review must produce an explicit memo and an explicit affirmative verdict. If any red-flag category is flagged (or the theater-check is not CONVERGENT), unrestricted-scope continuation is deferred and a fresh review cycle is required. This replaces the former human "participant" affirmation with the same routine adjudication path (a) already uses, and is the in-repo template for the fully autonomous `strategy-adoption` review (conservative default REJECT).

    The phase's remaining load-bearing function is catastrophic-break and contamination detection through n=1-detectable triggers, not smaller mandated exposure and not strategy-level statistical detection (which n=5 cannot support). Sizing throughout follows Rev 19's no-ceiling procedural discipline.

    Cycle 4 T1.D surfaced that without this gate, a green-light HYBRID period could authorize re-widening to event types whose binding-constraint behavior was never empirically probed during the observation window. Cycle 5 T1.α surfaced that rev 5's gate text was operationally inert (no adjudication, nonexistent paper-trade infrastructure). Cycle 6 T1.δ/T1.ε surfaced that rev 6's mechanism additions had statistical inertness at n=5 plus scope-coverage outsourcing. Rev 7 corrects via inline adjudication specification + conservatism-reframing of live-small phase + n=1-detectable per-trade triggers.

13. **Criterion-3 sizing discipline is documentary, not code-enforced (rev 14 added per AR_orc `premortem-C-2026-a3` cycle 13 T1-2).** Entry criterion 3's rev-13/rev-14 annotation states the consequence for both halves of the two-layer sizing control — a negative size-attack verdict (part b) and a missing seven-factor justification (part b2) each make criterion 3 unsatisfied at that size. **Nothing enforces either mechanically.** `fn_order_guard_options` checks the crafted structure's `max_loss` against the thesis's stated budget plus the market-order, malformed-input and defined-risk rails; it reads no sizing-justification field and no size-attack verdict, and no threshold attaches to either. The structured sizing fields on a decision-log entry (`car_pct`, `risk_budget_pct`, `capital_at_risk`, `size_pct`) are record-only, and a 2026-08-05 audit found all four NULL on every thesis-construction and action-conversion row written since Rev 43 — so a session that simply never records either artifact is stopped by nothing but its own discipline, and the omission is invisible until someone reads the entry. *Status:* accepted, presently-live residual — this is a **transmission and record-keeping** gap, not a coverage gap: the control governs and its consequence is now stated; what is unproven is that it reaches the desk on any given entry. **[rev 18, 2026-09-02, per AR_orc `premortem-C-2026-a3` cycle 17 ORC-MISS-3 (Tier 3) — this Status clause declares a gap addressed while leaving the gap operative, which is the disclosure class AR_orc asked the attacker to hunt, so it is split into the two limbs it was conflating. **Limb 1, MONITORING — materially changed since rev 17 and now genuinely reaching a scheduled surface.** The review trigger below is walked by `Claude_Task_Plan.md` §M4 section I (see the Owner bracket at the end of this limitation), and that is checkable rather than asserted: section I's `premortem-indicator-walk` receipt records this locus by name every cycle, vacuous or not. **Limb 2, ENFORCEMENT AT ENTRY — unchanged, and nothing in the 2026-09-01 M4 work touched it.** "What is unproven is that it reaches the desk on any given entry" remains exactly true: `fn_order_guard_options` still reads no sizing-justification field and no size-attack verdict, the four structured sizing fields are still record-only, and a session that records neither artifact is still stopped by nothing but its own discipline. A monthly audit detects the omission AFTER the entry; it does not prevent it. **Do not let limb 1's improvement be read onto limb 2.** That reading is what the phrase "a transmission and record-keeping gap, not a coverage gap" invites, because filing the defect under record-keeping makes an unenforced entry-time control sound like bookkeeping. On a strategy whose only surviving sizing discipline is procedural (Section 5's 2.18 entry), an unenforced size-attack requirement is a coverage gap at entry that a monthly receipt records rather than closes. The Status clause above is left standing rather than rewritten, per this document's append-and-correct convention; read it through this bracket.]** Authoring a code-level check is machinery and is escalated, not resolved here. **Review trigger:** at each monthly review, for every Strategy C thesis entered since the previous review, read that thesis's `events.decision_log` entry and confirm (a) a seven-factor justification for the stated risk budget is on record and (b) the adversarial counter-argument attacked the size. Record the number of entries checked and the number carrying each. **Owner: M4 (Monthly Action Conversion)** — the only monthly-cadence routine whose declared read scope includes `strategy/08_pre_mortems.md`, checked against the slice map rather than assumed, and the routine that already queries `events.decision_log` at that cadence. **[rev 18, 2026-09-02, per AR_orc `premortem-C-2026-a3` cycle 17 T1-1 (sustained core) and ORC-MISS-1 (Tier 1) — THE READ-SCOPE JUSTIFICATION IMMEDIATELY ABOVE IS STRUCK AS A BASIS FOR THIS ASSIGNMENT, and the assignment is re-grounded on a verified one. This is the canonical statement of that correction; the five sibling loci carrying the same clause cite this bracket rather than restate it. **What was wrong.** "The only monthly-cadence routine whose declared read scope includes `strategy/08_pre_mortems.md`, checked against the slice map rather than assumed" was an INFERENCE from the `Claude_Task_Plan.md` §Strategy-reading slice-map row, and the inference does not hold. That row names W3, W4, M4, Q4, A3, D1 and AR_orchestrator on identical terms and granted the file "for reviews", and M4 conducts no review — it only enqueues. The row now states the point in its own words (`Claude_Task_Plan.md`:82, qualifier widened 2026-09-01): "**This row grants READ ACCESS ONLY and is not evidence that any routine on it performs any check** … Do not infer an owner from this row — read the routine's own section for a step that reads the surface." Read scope is not execution, and this section asserted the second from the first at six loci across revs 14–17. **What is true instead — verified against the live plan on 2026-09-02, not inferred.** `Claude_Task_Plan.md` §M4 now carries section **I. PRE-MORTEM OWNER-ASSIGNED CHECK WALK** (:3257, added 2026-09-01), which directs M4 to load this file every fire, discover from the live file every cycle each locus whose own text assigns M4 as executing owner, evaluate each exactly as worded, and write an `events.decision_log` receipt at `entry_type='premortem-indicator-walk'` — mandatory even on a fully vacuous cycle, because "nothing was entered this month" and "the walk was skipped" are otherwise indistinguishable. M4's read-access scope (:3209) names this file explicitly; `scripts/check_routine_scope.py` couples `Owner:` assignments and walk steps bidirectionally in CI, so neither half can be removed silently; and M5 raises `premortem_indicator_walk_missing` when a month completes with no receipt. Known Limitations 3, 4, 7 and 11 accordingly remain UNOWNED and are walked by no routine — see the rev-18 disclosures at Section 6's `[At 15 closed trades]` bullet and at Known Limitation 4. **How this locus came to be right, recorded so the credit is not misplaced.** Not by SL2, and not by this section: route (B) may write only this strategy's pre-mortem section, so AR_orc escalated the M4 half directly at cycle 17 (`ops.alerts` `premortem_live_gate_defect` `6c670d35-c44a-416d-b3e6-73209e66fc3f`, since resolved) and it was closed at its own locus by an owner-directed session. Rev 18 records the outcome; it did not produce it, and it could not have.]** **Consequence for a specific flagged thesis:** the thesis is named in M4's own cycle decision-log entry as a criterion-3 record gap, and the missing artifact is reconstructed and recorded against the live position before the next monthly review. **Escalation:** any miss raises `ops.sp_raise_alert('warning','M4','premortem_live_gate_defect', …)` — an already-registered category for a documented control that does not reach the executed path, at `warning` rather than `critical` so it cannot enter `bigquery/107`'s blocking-criticals halt term — and promotes the code-level-check question from deferred to active. **Cumulatively at the 15-trade gate** (Known Limitation 11's existing calibration check), report the count carrying each artifact against the total, with every named exception. A review at which no new C theses were entered is recorded as such and is **not** a miss. This limitation defines no new indicator, moves no threshold and gates no entry.

Structural change definitions and mechanism-enforcement via classical-method delegation comparing reported categories at quarter-end to thesis-formation specified inline.

This operationalizes the band-not-point framing — at point estimate -3pp with SE ±4.5pp, the 95% upper CI bound is approximately +6pp, well above 0pp, so the flag does NOT fire because the band encompasses 0pp meaningfully. The flag fires only when both the point estimate is shortfalling AND statistical significance against zero is reached. The "primary" framing is downgraded — alpha-test becomes one indicator among the Section 4 portfolio (SGOV gap, invalidation count, theme concentration, EV-per-5-closes), with joint signal across multiple indicators required for actionable edge-decay determination.
