**Self-containment statement:** This pre-mortem enumerates within its own text the strategy's thesis, eligibility rule, deployment posture, deferral behavior, entry criteria, exit rules, declared trade frequency, router activation rule, money-loss scenarios, SGOV-underperformance scenarios, edge-decay indicators, specific AI_Edges disadvantages posing greatest risk, dated failure indicators, binding core-edge constraint confrontation, and known limitations. Short tags accompany AI_Edges disadvantage numbers; full definitions are in the attacker-prompt inline block.

#### 1. Strategy D thesis and mechanism

**Thesis.** AI's cross-document synthesis (edges 1.1 narrative synthesis and 1.4 cross-report contradiction surfacing — 1.4 used as a synthesis technique within 1.1 per Strategy C cycle 4 T1.C precedent) and cross-disciplinary integration (edge 1.10) identifies a small number of high-conviction equity names where the case for 12+ month holding rests on structural factors that take 1-3 years to play out. D is deliberately concentrated — 5-10 positions when fully deployed — because the synthesis edge is in identifying specific names worth committing to, not in producing diversified factor exposures. The strategy explicitly claims to compensate AI_Edges 2.23 (tax and fee drag) through LTCG-optimized holding periods.

**Permitted thesis subtypes (rev 4 added per cycle 3 T1-β; rev 5 typing rule added per cycle 4 T1-1).** D's universe consists of two explicit thesis structures: Subtype A — future-dated catalyst theses (primary driver is a future-dated event with publicly-known scheduled date, e.g., FDA decision, regulatory deadline, scheduled product launch); Subtype B — trend-continuation theses (primary driver is continuation of a quantifiable current trend metric for ≥ 12 months, e.g., "AAPL services revenue compounds at ≥ 18% YoY for next 4 quarters"). Both subtypes maintain no-qualitative-only invalidation discipline (see entry criterion 4 below). Both address 2.19 manifestation (a) (narrative pattern-matching) by requiring forward-anchored thesis structure rather than analogical reasoning. Cycle 3 T1-β surfaced that the rev 3 Subtype-A-only framing excluded legitimate trend-continuation theses (AAPL services, NVDA hyperscaler, COST flywheel, V/MA payments, BRK.B compounder); rev 4 expanded while preserving the no-qualitative-only discipline.

*Typing rule for dual-signal theses (rev 5 added per cycle 4 T1-1).* For theses where both a future-dated catalyst with publicly-known scheduled date AND a quantifiable trend metric are present in the thesis articulation, the typing decision is mechanism-enforced via date arithmetic against the resolution timing:
- Filing under **Subtype A only** is required when a future-dated catalyst is present but no quantifiable trend metric is articulated (the original rev 4 Subtype A case).
- Filing under **Subtype B only** is required when a quantifiable trend metric is articulated but no future-dated catalyst with publicly-known scheduled date is present (the original rev 4 Subtype B case).
- Filing under **Subtype A** is required for dual-signal theses when the catalyst's scheduled resolution date is ≤ 12 months from thesis-formation. The catalyst will resolve before the trend metric can complete its forward-verification window, so Subtype A's invalidation menu is the more time-relevant test.
- Filing under **Subtype B** is required for dual-signal theses when the catalyst's scheduled resolution date is > 12 months from thesis-formation. The trend metric's 12-month forward-verification will complete before the catalyst resolves, so Subtype B's invalidation menu is the more time-relevant test.
- Filing under **both subtypes simultaneously** is required when the catalyst's scheduled resolution date falls within the 12-month forward-verification window AND the trend metric's threshold can be evaluated independently of the catalyst's resolution. Invalidation criteria are then the union of Subtype A's invalidation menu AND Subtype B's invalidation menu — invalidation fires on either trigger.

The typing rule is mechanism-enforced via date comparison against thesis-formation date, not participant adjudication. Cycle 4 T1-1 surfaced that rev 4's subtype framing didn't specify primary-driver / typing determination for dual-signal theses, reintroducing the cycle 2 T1-A/B/C defendant-as-judge pattern at the typing stage. Rev 5 closes this with mechanism-enforced date arithmetic. Cycle 4 T1-2 (asymmetric invalidation menus exploitable via T1-1) collapses with this fix because the typing decision is no longer participant-adjudicated.

**Concentration as a design decision.** A 20+ name diversified narrative core is structurally close to a narrative-tilted index; D therefore retains its minimum-five floor and 30% GICS-sector cap. Under rev 19, neither an individual thesis nor aggregate same-name CaR has a numeric ceiling. The capital-preservation cost of concentration is accepted and governed only by the procedural risk-budget discipline plus after-the-fact kill triggers.

**Instrument eligibility rule.**
- US-listed common equity (ADRs acceptable for large-cap foreign-domiciled companies)
- Market cap ≥ $10B at entry (higher threshold than A/B because D's multi-year horizons expose it to more business-risk drift in smaller names)
- 30-day average daily volume ≥ $20M
- Position size: ~~2% of strategy portfolio at entry~~ **AI-chosen risk budget per thesis (Rev 43, owner directive, 2026-07-28).** No blanket per-position figure. The AI sets this thesis's Capital at Risk (for long equity, the full position notional — there is no stop-loss, so the honest worst case is total loss) as a percentage of strategy portfolio value at entry, justified in the decision-log entry against the seven-factor list in `Experiment_Parameters.md` §Position size, and attacked on size as well as direction by the adversarial counter-argument entry criterion. Bounded by the ~~hard envelopes: **per-name aggregate CaR ≤ 10%** of strategy portfolio (all tranches summed) and **per-strategy deployed CaR ≤ 75%**~~ **[owner directive 2026-08-05 — BOTH envelopes RETIRED. The risk budget has NO numeric ceiling at any level; sizing is governed solely by the seven-factor justification and the mandatory adversarial attack on the size].** Conviction enters as an ordinal tier only — never as a probability multiplied into a sizing formula (`AI_Trading_Foundation.md` 3a.1, 2.26).
- Concurrent position count: minimum 5 (floor retained); maximum removed (Rev 35)—no holdings-count ceiling. Rev 19 also removed the per-name and total-deployed CaR ceilings; the 30%-of-NAV GICS-sector concentration cap remains.
- Long-only

**Deployment posture.** D has no position-count ceiling and no numeric deployed-CaR ceiling. The minimum-five floor and 30%-of-NAV GICS-sector concentration cap remain. Any unused capital is parked; deployed TWR is measured on deployed capital only.

**Entry criteria (all must be met):**
1. Structural thesis articulated with 12+ month expected realization timeline, filed under one of the two permitted subtypes (rev 4 reframed per cycle 3 T1-β): Subtype A (future-dated catalyst — primary driver is a future-dated event with publicly-known scheduled date) or Subtype B (trend-continuation — primary driver is continuation of a quantifiable current trend metric for ≥ 12 months).
2. Thesis constructed from: last 8 quarters of earnings call transcripts (minimum), last 2 annual reports, competitive/sector context, regulatory and policy context, technological and secular theme context as applicable
3. Adversarial counter-argument specifically addresses: multi-year risk factors, management turnover risk, technological disruption risk, competitive erosion risk, concentration risk within D's existing book
4. Thesis specifies BOTH "completion" criteria (narrative fulfillment markers) AND "invalidation" criteria (observable pre-completion failure signals) — immutable through the position's life. (rev 4 subtype-specific per cycle 3 T1-β.) Subtype A invalidation: financial metric breach, regulatory milestone missed, contractual obligation failure, or publicly-disclosed corporate action. Subtype B invalidation: quantitative threshold on the trend metric (e.g., "growth rate falls below 15% YoY for 2 consecutive quarters"). Both maintain no-qualitative-only discipline.
5. Position does not produce > 30% concentration in any single GICS sector within D's book; additionally (rev 2 added per cycle 1 item 7, rev 3 reframed per cycle 2 T1-C as mechanism-enforced correlation test, rev 4 extended per cycle 3 T1-α to post-entry monitoring): for any two currently-held positions with trailing-252-day daily-return correlation > 0.6, both count toward the same correlation bucket. No more than 3 positions may share any correlation bucket. Bucket assignment computed by classical-method delegation. *Post-entry monitoring:* correlation matrix recomputed monthly; if any held-position pair exceeds 0.7 correlation post-entry, the pair is treated as a 2-position bucket for subsequent entry decisions, blocking further accumulation. Post-entry monitoring does not force exits. **[Rev 35 (owner directive): the count cap and the post-entry entry-blocking behavior described above are REMOVED — correlation-bucket membership is now monitored-only/informational (Section 6), neither capped nor entry-blocking. The 30%-of-NAV GICS-sector exposure cap is unchanged.]**
6. Entry is not triggered by short-term momentum; if the name is rallying hard in the trailing 30 days, defer entry until rally pauses (avoids buying at recency-bias-fueled peaks per 2.14)

**Exit rules.**
- Thesis completion: narrative fulfillment marker reached
- Thesis invalidation: specific at-entry-defined invalidation criteria met (NOT price action alone)
- Tax optimization: after 12 months holding, positions qualify for LTCG treatment. ~~When exiting on completion, prefer post-12-month exits where thesis permits.~~ **[REMOVED per Rev 39, owner directive, 2026-07-21 — exit timing is governed solely by thesis completion/invalidation; no preference to delay a completion-triggered exit for LTCG qualification. LTCG treatment still applies passively whenever the 12-month line has already been crossed by the time thesis criteria fire.]**
- No maximum hold. D's theses can legitimately take 2-3 years.

Not exit-triggering: short-term adverse price moves; quarterly results that don't bear on the multi-year thesis; macro environment shifts that don't invalidate the specific structural drivers.

**Declared expected frequency.** 3-8 trades per year over active periods, counting entries and exits as separate trades (a single 2-year hold is 2 trades over ~2 years). At fully-deployed steady-state of 5-10 positions, this implies thesis turnover of 1-4 names per year. *Critical property:* D's 30-trade gate will likely not be reached within any stable model-generation window. D's final determination comes at termination via one of the kill triggers — primarily the 36-month mark-to-market underperformance trigger or the foundation-change trigger, and secondarily drawdown.

**Router activation rule.** Technical: (SPY Trend State = UP OR SPY Trend State = NEUTRAL) AND Yield Curve Sustained Inversion Flag = NOT-SUSTAINED. Rationale: D is long-only equity with 12+ month minimum holding expectations. In DOWN regimes, initiating new long positions with multi-year time-to-resolution expectations adds macro risk with no compensation — defer. SUSTAINED yield curve inversion (> 18 months) has historically preceded regimes where multi-year equity theses compress under recessionary stress. Router deactivation does not force exits on existing D positions — existing positions run to their normal thesis-invalidation or exit conditions; D's multi-year theses would be systematically damaged by forced exits on transient regime changes.

#### 2. Systematic money-loss scenarios

1. **Concentrated long-only in multi-year bear regime.** D holds 5-10 names with no escape mechanism for existing positions; SUSTAINED yield curve inversion deactivates new entries but existing positions run to thesis outcomes, which may be catastrophic in severe recessions.
2. **Multi-year theses are pattern-matching (per 2.19 look-ahead bias).** The cross-document synthesis for long-horizon theses draws on historical analogues whose outcomes Claude implicitly knows; apparent thesis construction is actually outcome recall masquerading as forward analysis.
3. **Management turnover, technological disruption, competitive erosion within hold window.** Any of these within D's 2-3 year holding can invalidate a thesis; D's at-entry-defined invalidation criteria may not capture the specific form of disruption that materializes.
4. **Secular theme mean reversion.** "Theme X is the future of Y" was narratively strong at entry; thesis was participation in the theme; theme reverts to normal and D holds names without remaining alpha (per 2.20 textbook-rational penalty — D's narrative valuations don't anticipate bubble-and-revert dynamics).
5. **Narrative-synthesis edge decay (per 2.8 homogenization).** As models evolve and AI-driven equity selection homogenizes, the differential value of Claude's synthesis decreases; D's core edge dissipates while positions still run.
6. **2.23 LTCG-compensation residual.** The strategy thesis claims to compensate 2.23 through LTCG-optimized holding periods. Compensation is conditional on positions actually reaching the 12-month LTCG line. Positions exited via thesis-invalidation criteria before 12 months incur short-term capital gains rates, partially negating the 2.23 compensation. The entry-deferral rule for rallying names (entry criterion 6) can also push entries into less-tax-efficient timing.
7. **Concentration risk within narrative themes.** The 30% GICS-sector cap (entry criterion 5) prevents single-sector overweight, but multiple positions tracking a single secular theme can span GICS sectors (e.g., AI infrastructure buildout names span Technology, Industrials, Utilities, Communication Services). Sector cap doesn't catch theme-level correlation.

#### 3. SGOV-underperformance scenarios

1. **Deployed positions underperform SGOV over actual holding periods.** Even if directionally successful, deployed TWR may trail short-rate yield over D's 1-3 year holding windows in the regimes D operates within.
2. **36-month mark-to-market trigger becomes operative.** Universal across strategies; for D specifically because D will not hit the 30-trade gate first.
3. **~~SGOV~~-parked capital interaction [Rev 38, owner directive, 2026-07-15 — park vehicle is SGOV historically, VOO from the 2026-07-15 cutover forward; see Operating_Protocols.md §13].** The 80%+ that's not deployed does not count against D's measured TWR (per Experiment_Parameters.md deployed-capital measurement), but D must also not be so under-deployed that it fails to actually express a thesis at all. Persistent under-deployment (e.g., < 5 positions for extended periods) may indicate the strategy is producing no genuine multi-year conviction, in which case the 36-month MTM trigger evaluation would lack signal.
4. **Tax-and-fee drag exceeding LTCG compensation.** If thesis-invalidation rate on positions held < 12 months is meaningful, accumulated STCG plus transaction costs can exceed the LTCG benefit on completed-thesis exits.

#### 4. Edge-decay indicators

D's multi-year horizon means most edge-decay signals can't appear in monthly cadence. The indicators below are designed to fire as multi-year evidence accumulates, with monthly review checking progress against them rather than triggering on monthly data.

- **Edge-decay portfolio: beta-adjusted alpha at 24 months of active time, with explicit CI gate (rev 4 reframed per cycle 3 T1-γ; rev 5 CI-gated per cycle 4 T1-4).** Replaces rev 3's unadjusted deployed-TWR-vs-SPY ≥ 3pp threshold which confounded strategy alpha with realized beta deviation from 1.0. *Computation (rev 28 added to classical-method delegation):* (i) regress monthly D-deployed-TWR on monthly SPY returns over the 24-month window to estimate realized beta (β̂); (ii) construct beta-equivalent synthetic return = β̂ × monthly SPY return for each month; (iii) compare cumulative deployed TWR to cumulative synthetic; (iv) the alpha differential (Jensen's alpha equivalent at 24 months) is the alpha-test metric; (v) compute standard error on the alpha estimate via the regression intercept's standard error (rev 5 addition). *Threshold (rev 5 CI-gated):* alpha-test fires the edge-decay flag only when BOTH (a) alpha point estimate ≤ -3pp cumulative over 24 months, AND (b) the 95% upper CI bound on the alpha estimate ≤ 0pp. The CI gate operationalizes the band-not-point framing — at point estimate -3pp with SE ±4.5pp (typical β̂ noise of ±0.2 SE × cumulative SPY return ~0.22), the 95% upper CI bound is approximately +6pp, well above 0pp, so the flag does NOT fire because the band encompasses 0pp meaningfully. The flag fires only when both the point estimate is shortfalling AND statistical significance against zero is reached. *Rev 5 architectural change:* the alpha-test is no longer the singular "primary" edge-decay indicator. It is one indicator within the Section 4 portfolio (alpha-test, SGOV gap, invalidation count, theme concentration, EV-per-5-closes), with joint-signal-across-indicators required for actionable edge-decay determination. *Cycle 4 T1-4 surfaced that rev 4's 3pp threshold was statistically inert against acknowledged β̂ noise (SE on alpha ≈ ±4.5pp); rev 5 closes the gap with the CI gate AND the architectural reframing of alpha-test from primary to portfolio member. Beta-estimate-noise residual remains as KL — even with CI operationalization, finite false-negative rate on materially-decayed-but-not-significantly-so cases.*
- **SGOV-underperformance signal (separate from edge-decay): deployed TWR vs SGOV at 24 months of active time, gap ≥ 5pp cumulative.** Retained as a separate indicator (not edge-decay) per cycle 2 T1-D distinction. SGOV gap captures the experiment's overall question (does the strategy beat the cash benchmark) but cannot diagnose whether failure is from market beta drawdown or strategy edge erosion. SGOV gap firing without SPY gap firing → market beta drawdown, edge intact. Both firing → likely edge issue. SPY gap firing without SGOV gap firing → strategy positive vs. cash but underperforming equity passive → edge ambiguous.
- **Thesis-invalidation count ≥ 3 within any rolling 36-month window.** Replaces rev 1's "30% invalidation rate" indicator (which had no defined window or denominator on D's small position count). A rolling 36-month count threshold of 3 is checkable on D's actual cadence: at 3-8 trades/year frequency, ~5-13 closes per 36-month window; 3 invalidations indicates ≥ 23-60% invalidation rate, depending on total closes. The metric measures "theses don't survive their own forecast conditions" without confounding with completion exits.
- **Theme-concentration check (rev 2 added per cycle 1 item 7 in money-loss scenarios).** Number of currently-held positions tracking a single declared narrative theme exceeds 3. This catches theme-level correlation that the 30% GICS-sector cap doesn't (e.g., AI infrastructure names spanning 3+ GICS sectors). **[Rev 35: the paired max-3-per-theme *entry* cap is removed (owner directive); this Section 4 check is retained as an informational monitoring flag only — it no longer blocks entry.]**
- **Cumulative EV per closed position negative over rolling 5 closed positions.** Mechanism-agnostic edge-decay signal parallel to Strategy C rev 9. EV per closed position = (P&L on positions ever closed at completion) − (P&L on positions ever closed at invalidation), normalized per position. At D's small sample, this is a low-resolution signal but provides a non-hit-rate metric for edge presence.

Note: the rev 1 "sector concentration > 30%" indicator has been moved from this section. Per cycle 1 item 5: post-entry sector concentration > 30% can only happen via price drift in held positions (entry criterion 5 prevents new entries that would push sector above 30%), so the indicator measures portfolio-construction-drift, not edge decay. Section 6 retains the monitoring under the portfolio-construction-monitoring purpose.

#### 5. Specific AI_Edges disadvantages posing greatest risk

- **2.4 (narrative over-fit on multi-year structural thesis, rev 2 added per cycle 1 item 3):** *D's mechanism is literally narrative synthesis; 2.4 is the on-point failure mode for D's core operation.* Self-reference closure: Claude's only at-entry discriminator on whether the multi-year thesis is over-fit is Claude's own assessment, which is biased by 2.4. The strategy's "narrative-synthesis edge earns its keep" thesis claim is exactly what 2.4 distorts. *Mitigation:* entry criterion 3 (adversarial counter-argument with specific multi-year risk factor coverage) as graded discriminator (~30% empirical reduction parallel to A/B/C). Entry criterion 4 (immutable invalidation criteria specified at entry) provides post-entry resolution: if the invalidation criteria fire, the thesis is wrong regardless of how compelling its narrative remained. *Residual:* ~70% structural after counter-argument; post-hoc detection via Section 4 invalidation-count indicator and EV-per-closed-position indicator. **[rev 10 provenance flag, 2026-08-24 — the ~30%/~70% split above is UNSOURCED. It originates in `AI_Trading_Foundation.md` §1.3's unattributed sentence ("the bias is only reduced by about 30% — it is hard-wired in the model's weights"), uncited since the repository's first commit (`e7be4c0`, 2026-05-08); A1's 2026-07-28 annual re-derivation searched for a source at all four evidence levels with two independent agents and recorded it ABSENT (`Annual_AI_Foundation_Sweep.md` §C.3), remedy NEW RESEARCH — not a replication on the current model line — and `AI_Trading_Foundation.md` §§1.3 and 2.4 both carry that flag verbatim. 2.4's EXISTENCE claim and the narrative-over-fit MECHANISM are unaffected; only the MAGNITUDE is unsupported. Read ~30%/~70% here as an unsourced working assumption, not a measurement. Swept identically across all five strategy pre-mortems' primary 2.4 loci in one pass rather than only the two AR_orc-flagged loci (Strategy C `premortem-C-2026-a3` cycle 15 T1-4; Strategy E `premortem-E-2026-a3` cycle 11 T1-1).]** See Constraint 1.

- **2.6 (no access to private information, rev 2 added per cycle 1 item 3):** load-bearing for management-execution and regulatory-process theses. Multi-year management execution quality is exactly the domain where channel checks, expert calls, KOL networks, and management access provide the professional-investor edge that D structurally lacks. Entry criterion 1's permitted drivers include "management strategic plan" — this is 2.6's home territory. *Mitigation:* universe restriction per Constraint 3 (theses whose primary driver is "management execution quality" subjective assessment are excluded; permitted are theses whose primary driver is observable structural factors with public-document traceability). *Residual:* structural disadvantage on theses where management-execution component is partial driver. See Constraint 3.

- **2.7 (regime maladaptation, rev 2 added per cycle 1 item 3):** D commits to multi-year holds within whatever regime exists at entry. If the training distribution doesn't represent the current or imminent regime (e.g., post-pandemic stagflation with novel macro structure), thesis construction is mis-calibrated for the period during which D must hold. The router's SUSTAINED-inversion deactivation is a partial mitigation against the most acute regime mismatch (recessionary regimes). *Mitigation:* router SUSTAINED-inversion deactivation; partial. *Residual:* regimes underrepresented in training data that don't trigger the inversion flag are not router-detected; positions held through such regimes are exposed.

- **2.8 (homogenization on commoditized AI strategy):** AI-driven equity selection homogenizes; D enters consensus multi-year bets. Long-horizon narrative themes (AI, EVs, decarbonization, biotech frontier) are crowded. *Mitigation:* the 30% GICS-sector cap (retained) ~~and the rev 2 theme-concentration cap (max 3 positions per declared narrative theme)~~ partially address single-theme over-allocation. **[Rev 35: the max-3-per-theme entry cap is REMOVED (owner directive); theme over-allocation is now monitored-only via Section 4, not capped. The 30%-of-NAV sector exposure cap is unchanged.]** *Residual:* cross-AI-operator crowding on individual names is unaddressed at the strategy level; observable post-hoc via SGOV underperformance and edge-decay indicators.

- **2.13 (miscalibration on multi-year directional theses):** Claude's 12+ month theses are systematically overconfident. Entry criterion 3 is a partial counter-argument check; no numeric sizing bound remains under rev 19. *Residual:* structural overconfidence can directly inflate an unbounded budget.

- **2.14 (recency bias on input data):** even for 12+ month theses, Claude weights the most recent quarter's data ~10× per 2.14's hard-wired property when constructing thesis. The 8-quarter input window in entry criterion 2 provides historical anchor but doesn't override 2.14. Entry criterion 6 (defer entry on recently-rallying names) addresses one recency-bias channel (price-recency) but not the analytical-recency channel. *Mitigation:* 8-quarter input window; entry criterion 6 partial. *Residual:* analytical-recency bias on thesis construction; structural.

- **2.15 (base-rate neglect on multi-year thesis realization):** published base rates are weak and Claude's confidence is distorted. No strategy-level or numeric magnitude mitigation remains. *Residual:* structural; see Known Limitations.

- **2.17 (algorithm appreciation):** confidence in plausible-sounding long-horizon narratives beyond what empirical track record supports. Closely related to 2.13 but operates at the mechanism level: Claude over-weights its own multi-year narrative synthesis output relative to base rates of human discretionary multi-year forecast accuracy. *Mitigation:* entry criterion 3 partial. *Residual:* structural.

- **2.19 (look-ahead bias on multi-year retrospective synthesis):** *D's input set is the most outcome-contaminated training-data territory.* 8 quarters of historical earnings call transcripts plus 2 annual reports plus historical regulatory/competitive context is exactly the timeframe where Claude's training data includes the outcomes of the cases the synthesis draws comparison from. Apparent forward analysis is partially backward retrieval. *Mitigation:* universe restriction per Constraint 2 (thesis must rest on a future-dated structural driver — specific upcoming event, milestone, or regulatory deadline — rather than on continuation-of-past-pattern claims). *Residual:* even with the future-dated-driver requirement, the broader market context for the thesis remains in 2.19's hot zone. See Constraint 2.

- **2.20 (textbook-rational penalty, rev 2 added per cycle 1 item 3):** D's entry criterion 1 lists "thematic participation mechanism" as a legitimate structural driver. Thematic participation theses overlap heavily with 2.20's failure mode: AI prices near fundamental value with tight errors, failing to reproduce bubble formation. D may systematically miss bubble-driven secular themes that are profitable until they aren't, or enter mid-bubble while pricing the thesis as if fundamentals will catch up to price. *Mitigation:* entry criterion 6 (defer on rallying names) partial. *Residual:* structural.

- **2.23 (tax and fee drag — thesis-named compensated disadvantage, rev 2 added per cycle 1 item 3):** the strategy thesis explicitly claims to compensate 2.23 through LTCG-optimized holding periods. *Compensation mechanism:* ~~exit rules prefer post-12-month exits where thesis permits;~~ **[REMOVED per Rev 39, owner directive, 2026-07-21 — no active exit-timing preference; exit fires purely on thesis completion/invalidation, never delayed for tax purposes]** positions held to completion past 12 months incidentally qualify for LTCG treatment (D's multi-year theses commonly run past 12 months anyway), materially reducing tax drag vs. STCG-rate frequent-rebalance strategies when that happens. *Residual:* **(Rev 39 widens this residual)** compensation is now fully incidental — nothing nudges a completion/invalidation-triggered exit toward the 12-month line, so any exit firing shortly before 12 months incurs STCG with no mitigating mechanism. Positions exited via thesis-invalidation criteria before 12 months incur STCG, partially negating compensation. The entry-deferral rule (criterion 6) for rallying names can push entries into less-tax-efficient timing. Per Section 2 scenario 6 and Section 3 scenario 4.

**Binding core-edge constraints (req 7 confrontation, rev 2 added per cycle 1 item 2).**

D's core edge is AI narrative synthesis identifying multi-year structural theses on individual equity names. Three distinct constraints materially affect this edge.

*Cross-constraint flowing limitation (rev 19 propagation).* D's 30% GICS-sector cap still limits sector concentration, but the retired count caps and sizing envelopes provide no per-thesis or total-deployed magnitude bound. The 2.4/2.8/2.13/2.15/2.17/2.19 confluence is addressed only by the seven-factor budget record, mandatory adversarial size attack, and post-hoc monitoring.

*Section 5 / preamble cross-walk (rev 19 propagation):* no cap or envelope supplies magnitude-only mitigation for 2.4, 2.8, 2.13, 2.15, 2.17, or 2.19.

**Current procedural control (per `Experiment_Parameters.md` rev 19):**
- ~~*OUTER layer — still mechanical, still bias-independent:* the hard envelopes — per-name aggregate CaR ≤ 10% of strategy portfolio value, summed across all of a name's tranches; per-strategy deployed CaR ≤ 75% of strategy portfolio value. These are versioned policy, not judgment, and bound the blast radius of a single miscalibrated session — the same protective property the flat 2% cap had, but at a **5x looser bound** (2% → 10% per name). This is the only part of the old property that survives.~~ **[owner directive 2026-08-05 — THE OUTER LAYER NO LONGER EXISTS. Both envelopes retired; no numeric ceiling on size at any level. The bias-independent mechanical protection described above is GONE, not loosened again: nothing now bounds single-thesis blast radius, and the −50% deployed-TWR drawdown kill is a detector that fires after the loss, not a cap that prevents it. All surviving compensation is procedural and AI-dependent (seven-factor justification + mandatory adversarial attack on size) — the same control class 2.13 / 2.18 / 2.26 identify as unreliable. Stated bluntly here on purpose; the owner accepted the trade-off, reasoning that with no stop-losses size IS the risk lever and the AI is trusted to set it.]**
- *INNER layer — judgment-dependent, therefore bias-exposed:* the per-thesis risk budget itself, disciplined only by (a) the fixed seven-factor justification recorded in the thesis's decision-log entry — falsifiability of the invalidation criteria, time to resolution, instrument loss profile, correlation with the existing book, regime fragility, liquidity/exit-ability at the intended size, and conviction (ordinal tier only, never a probability multiplied into a sizing formula, per `AI_Trading_Foundation.md` 3a.1, 2.26) — and (b) the mandatory adversarial attack on the size (the counter-argument entry criterion must state what the position costs if the thesis is wrong and argue whether that loss is acceptable).
- *D-specific consequence of the seven-factor list (not generic boilerplate):* slow falsifiability and D's 12+-month, commonly 2–3-year resolution time argue for smaller budgets, but they are judgment inputs rather than ceilings.
- *Residual:* under rev 19 a bias-inflated conviction may consume the full strategy portfolio; the historical Rev-43 2%→10% / 5× arithmetic is superseded. The mandatory adversarial attack is a same-model, graded check, and kill triggers detect loss after the fact.
- *Not currently offsetting that residual — an open measurability gap, not a mitigation (Rev 8, 2026-08-10 — reworded per AR_orc `premortem-D-2026-a3` cycle 7 W1: the prior "partially offsetting" header asserted a present-tense mitigating effect that the corrected body beneath it, and KL18, both say does not exist):* under the flat rule, 2.13/2.26 overconfidence was both unmitigated AND unmeasurable in the sizing dimension — every trade was the same size, so outcomes carried no information about whether conviction was worth anything. With ordinal conviction now recorded per entry, ~~size-vs-outcome becomes a directly measurable calibration series, consumed by the Section 4 indicator portfolio. This is a detection improvement, not a magnitude mitigation.~~ **[Rev 7, 2026-08-06 — corrected: this claim was false and is withdrawn (AR_orc `premortem-D-2026-a3` cycle 6, 2026-08-05, independently re-enumerating Section 4 before grading). Section 4's five indicators — named here so the claim is inline-verifiable — are the beta-adjusted alpha-test at 24 months (CI-gated), the SGOV-underperformance gap at 24 months (≥5pp cumulative), the thesis-invalidation count (≥3 within any rolling 36-month window), the theme-concentration check (>3 positions on one declared narrative theme), and cumulative EV per closed position negative over a rolling 5 closes; Section 6's monitoring items add no sixth. None of the five reads conviction tier, records per-thesis size, or joins size to outcome — conviction-tier-vs-outcome calibration is not detected anywhere in this document, in Section 4 or otherwise. The ordinal-conviction field the risk-budget mechanism introduced makes such a series constructible in principle; nothing here currently builds or consumes it. This gap is recorded, not resolved, as Known Limitation 18 below — building the indicator is out of scope for this artifact-consistency revision. Blast-radius arithmetic, stated correctly: the "5x" figure above (2%→10%) describes only Rev 43's now-superseded change; per rev 19 (`Experiment_Parameters.md`, owner directive 2026-08-05, annotated earlier in this section), both CaR envelopes are retired outright and Rev 40's uncapped per-name tranche count was never re-capped, so a concentrated long-only book's worst-case single-thesis blast radius is now bounded only by the strategy portfolio itself, not by 10% and not merely 5x the retired 2% figure. The −50% deployed-TWR drawdown kill is the only quantitative backstop, and per `Experiment_Parameters.md` line 312 it is a DETECTOR that fires after a loss is realised, not a bound that caps it in advance.]** Probability-of-correctness for these biases is unaddressed at the strategy level (post-hoc detection via Section 4 indicators is the only recourse). The magnitude-only framing is principled — the cap is the right tool for bounding individual-thesis blast radius, but isn't the right tool for biasing thesis-correctness rates. **Not in confluence list (cap is non-mitigation):** 2.6 (mitigated by Constraint 3 observable-test, not by sizing); 2.7 (mitigated by router SUSTAINED-inversion deactivation, not by sizing); 2.14 (mitigated by 8-quarter window + criterion 6, not by sizing); 2.20 (mitigated by criterion 6 partial, not by sizing); 2.23 (compensated by LTCG-preferred exit, not by sizing). Six-of-eleven disadvantages route through cap as magnitude-only; five-of-eleven route through other mitigations. (Rev 8, 2026-08-10 — corrected per AR_orc `premortem-D-2026-a3` cycle 7 W4: the halves of this sentence were swapped; the six-item confluence set 2.4/2.8/2.13/2.15/2.17/2.19 above routes through the cap, and the five-item "Not in confluence list" immediately preceding routes through other mitigations. Both underlying lists were already correct; only this summary sentence's counts were transposed.) The asymmetry is principled, not a self-containment break.

*Constraint 1 — 2.4 narrative over-fit on multi-year structural thesis.* D's mechanism is narrative synthesis evaluating multi-year structural drivers. Self-reference closure parallel to Strategy A pre-mortem rev 7 Constraint 2, B Constraint 1, C Constraint 2: the only at-entry discriminator on whether the multi-year thesis is over-fit is Claude's own assessment. *Flowing limitation:* entry criterion 3 (adversarial counter-argument with specific multi-year risk factor coverage) as graded discriminator (~30% empirical reduction); entry criterion 4 (immutable disconfirmable invalidation criteria specified at entry) provides post-entry resolution mechanism — if invalidation criteria fire, thesis is wrong regardless of narrative compellingness. *Universe limitation flowing:* theses that cannot be reduced to disconfirmable invalidation criteria are excluded (theses must be falsifiable in finite time by observable events). *Residual:* ~70% structural after counter-argument; post-hoc detection via Section 4. See Known Limitations.

*Constraint 2 — 2.19 look-ahead bias on multi-year retrospective synthesis (rev 3 reframed per cycle 2 T1-A + T1-F, rev 4 expanded per-subtype per cycle 3 T1-β).* D's 8-quarter transcript + 2-annual-report input set is the most outcome-contaminated territory in training data. Multi-year retrospective synthesis is exactly the pattern-matching mode where outcome retrieval substitutes for forward analysis. 2.19 has two distinct manifestations on D's mechanism: (a) narrative-pattern-matching — Claude implicitly knows outcomes of historical analogues drawn from 8-quarter transcripts and uses them as if forward analysis; (b) outcome-retrieval-on-future-event-handicapping — even when thesis is anchored on a genuinely future-dated event, Claude's training data contains similar past events whose outcomes color the handicapping. *Flowing limitation per subtype (rev 4):*

- *Subtype A (future-dated catalyst theses):* primary driver must be a future-dated event with a publicly-known scheduled date that has not yet occurred at thesis-formation time (e.g., FDA decision date 2027-Q3, regulatory deadline 2026-12-31, contractual milestone, scheduled product launch). Participant-verifiable from public schedules without requiring metacognition. Universe limitation: theses anchored on past-pattern continuation without a specific future-dated catalyst are excluded from Subtype A.

- *Subtype B (trend-continuation theses):* primary driver is continuation of a quantifiable current trend metric for ≥ 12 months. The trend metric IS the test — the thesis structurally requires forward verification of the metric over the holding period rather than analogical reasoning to historical comparable cases. Universe limitation: theses where the "trend metric" cannot be reduced to a quantifiable forward-verifiable measurement are excluded from Subtype B.

Cycle 3 T1-β surfaced that the rev 3 Subtype-A-only framing excluded legitimate trend-continuation theses (AAPL services, NVDA hyperscaler, COST flywheel, V/MA payments, BRK.B compounder); rev 4 expands to both subtypes while preserving the no-qualitative-only discipline. Both subtypes structurally constrain narrative-pattern-matching (manifestation (a)) by requiring forward-anchored thesis structure rather than backward analogical reasoning. *Residual on manifestation (a) under both subtypes:* claims about *expected magnitude* of the catalyst-driven reaction or trend-continuation may still draw on outcome-retrieval from training data (the thesis structure is constrained but the magnitude estimation is not). *Residual on manifestation (b) — cycle 2 T1-F acknowledgment:* outcome-retrieval-on-future-event-handicapping is not addressed by the subtype constraints (a continuation-of-trend thesis can be free of analogical look-ahead while still using look-ahead-tainted reasoning to handicap the trend's continuation; a future-dated thesis can use look-ahead to handicap the event). No clean strategy-level mitigation exists for this residual; parallels Strategy A's rev 7 acceptance pattern on Constraint 3 transparent-citation form. See Known Limitations.

*Constraint 3 — 2.6 no access to private information on management-execution theses (rev 3 reframed per cycle 2 T1-B as observable-test on entry criterion 4, rev 4 expanded per-subtype per cycle 3 T1-β).* Multi-year management execution quality is exactly where channel checks, expert calls, KOL networks, and management access provide the professional-investor edge. *Flowing limitation per subtype:* entry criterion 4 (immutable disconfirmable invalidation criteria) is tightened — invalidation criteria must be public-information-observable in subtype-specific forms.

- *Subtype A:* invalidation criteria must be a financial metric breach (e.g., revenue growth falls below specified threshold), a regulatory milestone missed (e.g., FDA decision date passes without approval), a contractual obligation failure (e.g., specific contract breach disclosed in SEC filings), or a publicly-disclosed corporate action.
- *Subtype B:* invalidation criteria must be a quantitative threshold on the trend metric being continued (e.g., "growth rate falls below 15% YoY for 2 consecutive quarters"; "renewal rate falls below 85%"; "operating earnings growth turns negative for 2 consecutive quarters"). The threshold must be specified at thesis-formation and immutable through life. **Metric-immutability auto-invalidation rule (rev 5 added per cycle 4 T1-3):** if the underlying reportable category undergoes a structural change such that the metric specified at thesis-formation is no longer reported in its original form for ≥ 2 consecutive quarters, the thesis auto-invalidates as of the date the second non-conforming quarterly report is released. "Structural change" includes: (a) the metric is no longer disclosed at all, (b) the metric is rolled into a different aggregate that doesn't permit isolating the original quantity, (c) the metric's definition changes such that the at-entry threshold is no longer comparable to the post-change disclosure. Auto-invalidation is mechanism-enforced via classical-method delegation comparing reported categories at quarter-end to those documented at thesis-formation. The 2-consecutive-quarter requirement avoids auto-invalidation on transient one-off non-disclosures while keeping the response timely. Cycle 4 T1-3 surfaced that rev 4's immutability criterion didn't specify behavior under reporting redefinition (auto-invalidate / stay-open / re-specify branches all problematic); rev 5 closes the gap with the auto-invalidation rule.

Theses where invalidation can only occur via "management didn't execute well" without an observable public-document proxy or quantitative trend-metric threshold are excluded from both subtypes — these are the theses where 2.6 binds, because invalidation requires private-information edge sources to detect. The previous rev 2 framing used a primary/secondary management-execution driver categorization which was participant-adjudicated with no external referent (cycle 2 T1-B defendant-as-judge); the rev 3 reframing was verifiable from invalidation-criteria text without requiring categorization but applied only to the future-dated subtype; rev 4 expands to per-subtype. *Universe limitation flowing:* theses where invalidation criteria cannot be reduced to public-document-observable signals (Subtype A) or quantitative trend-metric thresholds (Subtype B) are excluded. *Residual:* (i) management-execution may still be a thesis driver if invalidation is observable through proxy public signals or trend-metric continuation, but the thesis quality at entry remains 2.6-exposed (Claude can't assess management quality at entry without private-information channels); (ii) financial-metric invalidation criteria can be gamed by reporting choices (e.g., adjusted-EBITDA framings); (iii) trend-metric invalidation thresholds can be set too lenient at entry (entry-time thesis-author can pick threshold that won't fire under most failure modes). See Known Limitations.

#### 6. Dated failure indicators for monthly review

D's multi-year horizon means monthly indicators check progress and monitor portfolio composition; multi-year gates trigger comprehensive review.

- [Each monthly review] Position-level thesis check: has any news, management event, regulatory development, or competitive move in the prior month affected the structural thesis of any D position? Force explicit per-position review with three categorical questions: (a) thesis still on track, (b) thesis modified, (c) thesis at-risk per invalidation criteria.
- [Each monthly review] Sector concentration monitoring (portfolio-construction-drift purpose, not edge-decay): if any GICS sector exceeds 30% of D's book via price drift, flag for explicit review. Note: this is monitoring, not auto-trigger; the 30% entry-rule applies to new entries but post-entry drift is acceptable as long as thesis remains intact.
- [Each monthly review, rev 2 added, rev 3 reframed per cycle 2 T1-C, rev 4 response-action added per cycle 3 T1-α] Correlation-bucket monitoring: classical-method delegation recomputes the trailing-252-day correlation matrix across all currently-held positions. *Response action:* ~~if any held-position pair exceeds 0.7 daily-return correlation (above the 0.6 entry-time threshold by margin), the pair is treated as a 2-position bucket for subsequent entry decisions — any new candidate entry that would join this bucket is blocked from entry. Post-entry monitoring does not force exits; it only constrains future entries.~~ Correlation regime change is real-world signal, not strategy defect; ~~the response action described above was designed to prevent accumulation of further correlated positions during stress regimes without forcing liquidation of existing positions whose theses remain intact.~~ **[Rev 9, 2026-08-13, per AR_orc `premortem-D-2026-a3` cycle 8 Tier 3 — strikethrough applied. Rev 8 added the Rev 35 bracket below but left the superseded response-action prose in plain text, so the bullet read as operative on first pass and REMOVED only on reaching its trailing annotation. Every other superseded-claim site in this section strikes the claim itself before the bracketed correction (see the LTCG exit-preference clause, the 2.8 theme-concentration cap, and the 2.23 compensation mechanism); this bullet now follows that convention. Nothing about the removal's substance changes — only its presentation, which previously buried a REMOVED rule in unstruck prose.]** **[Rev 35 (owner directive), annotation added Rev 8 2026-08-10 per AR_orc `premortem-D-2026-a3` cycle 7 orchestrator-found priority finding: the response action described above — treating a post-entry-emergent bucket (pair correlation > 0.7) as blocking further accumulation — is REMOVED, mirroring entry criterion 5's Rev 35 bracket. The monthly trailing-252-day correlation-matrix recomputation above is retained as an informational monitoring flag only; correlation-bucket membership, both at-entry and post-entry, is monitored-only/informational, neither capped nor entry-blocking. The 30%-of-NAV GICS-sector exposure cap is unchanged.]**
- [Each monthly review, rev 2] LTCG-line tracking: number of positions held with > 9 months elapsed (approaching 12-month LTCG line). If a position is approaching the LTCG line and thesis is approaching completion, exit timing review per the LTCG-preference exit rule.
- [Each monthly review] Deployment count: number of currently-held positions. If < 5 for ≥ 3 consecutive months, flag persistent under-deployment for evaluation.
- [At 12 months active time] First comprehensive D review: any position now eligible for invalidation-driven exit? Cumulative thesis-invalidation count to date.
- [At 24 months active time, rev 3 dual-benchmark per cycle 2 T1-D, rev 4 beta-adjusted per cycle 3 T1-γ, rev 5 CI-gated per cycle 4 T1-4] Second comprehensive review. Beta-adjusted alpha vs SPY (alpha-test indicator within Section 4 portfolio — flag fires only when alpha point estimate ≤ -3pp AND 95% upper CI bound on alpha ≤ 0pp; β̂ standard error ~±0.2 produces ~±4.5pp SE on alpha at typical SPY 24-month return magnitudes) and TWR vs SGOV (SGOV-underperformance signal, ≥ 5pp gap) cumulative. Joint-signal-across-Section-4-portfolio required for actionable edge-decay determination — alpha-test alone no longer primary. SGOV gap firing without alpha-test firing → market beta drawdown, edge intact. Multiple Section 4 indicators agreeing → edge issue. **[Rev 9, 2026-08-13, per AR_orc `premortem-D-2026-a3` cycle 8 W1 — ITEMIZED CHECKLIST ADDITION, the missing firing path.] Also at this review, itemized so it cannot be skipped by a reader executing this checklist:** compute the conviction-tier-vs-outcome calibration series for all D positions closed to date — join each closed thesis's recorded ordinal conviction tier and its recorded risk budget (both already present in that thesis's `events.decision_log` entry) to its realized outcome — and re-evaluate Known Limitation 18's question, namely whether a conviction-tier-vs-outcome indicator should be promoted into Section 4's edge-decay indicator portfolio. Rev 9 has already performed that evaluation once, on the population available at rev 9, and answered **not yet — record now, evaluate at this gate** (see KL18 for the reasoning and the deferral's stated basis). **This bullet is that deferral's landing surface.** Two outcomes only: promote the indicator into Section 4 with a stated response action, or record in `events.decision_log` a written re-deferral naming the population actually available and the next checkpoint. Silence at this gate is not one of the outcomes — prior to rev 9 this bullet itemized only the beta-adjusted alpha-test and the SGOV gap, which is why KL18's second firing path had never once fired.
- [At 36 months active time] Mark-to-market underperformance trigger becomes operative per Experiment_Parameters.md.
- [Rolling 36-month window, evaluated each monthly review] Thesis-invalidation count. If ≥ 3 within any rolling 36-month window, edge-decay flag fires.

#### 7. Known limitations

1. **D's 30-trade gate effectively inactive.** Per Strategy.md mechanism. Final determination at termination via kill triggers (36-month MTM, foundation-change, drawdown). *Status:* accepted property, documented.

2. **2.4 narrative over-fit residual after counter-argument (~70%, Constraint 1).** Self-reference closure means within-distribution adversarial probing cannot fully resolve over-fit; only post-entry observable invalidation can. *Status:* accepted residual. Review trigger: thesis-invalidation count ≥ 3 within rolling 36-month window.

3. **2.19 look-ahead bias residual after future-dated-driver requirement (Constraint 2).** Broader market context for the thesis remains in the contaminated training-data zone; the future-dated-driver rule constrains the thesis structure but not the contextual reasoning around it. *Status:* accepted residual. Review trigger: at 24-month gate, audit subset of held theses for retrieval-vs-analysis attribution on contextual reasoning; if > 30% of theses appear retrieval-driven on key contextual claims, escalate.

4. **2.6 management-execution residual after primary-driver-traceability requirement (Constraint 3).** Theses where management-execution quality is a secondary driver remain partially exposed. *Status:* accepted residual. Review trigger: any position closed at invalidation where the invalidation cause is primarily management-execution failure (rather than the public-information-traceable primary driver) escalates to design-change reassessment.

5. **2.7 regime-maladaptation residual.** Router's SUSTAINED-inversion deactivation addresses recessionary regime mismatch but not other regime types underrepresented in training data. *Status:* accepted residual.

6. **2.8 homogenization residual on individual names.** Theme-concentration cap addresses theme-level crowding but not name-level crowding. *Status:* accepted residual.

7. **2.13 miscalibration on multi-year theses.** No multi-year-specific mitigation. *Status:* accepted residual.

8. **2.14 analytical-recency bias on thesis construction.** 8-quarter input window doesn't override hard-wired 10× recency weight. *Status:* accepted residual.

9. **2.15 base-rate neglect on multi-year thesis realization.** No base-rate gate at entry. *Status:* accepted residual.

10. **2.17 algorithm appreciation on long-horizon narratives.** *Status:* accepted residual.

11. **2.20 textbook-rational penalty on thematic theses.** Entry criterion 6 partial mitigation. *Status:* accepted residual.

12. **2.23 LTCG-compensation conditional on 12-month survival.** Positions exited via invalidation < 12 months negate the compensation; entry-deferral rule can push entries into less-tax-efficient timing. *Status:* accepted residual. Review trigger: at 24-month gate, audit fraction of closed positions that reached 12-month LTCG line; if < 50% of closed positions reached LTCG, the 2.23 compensation claim is materially weakened and the strategy's tax-efficiency rationale should be re-examined.

13. **Concentration-within-theme risk and during-hold correlation drift (rev 2 added, rev 3 reframed per cycle 2 T1-C, rev 4 expanded per cycle 3 T1-α).** The 30% GICS-sector cap doesn't catch cross-sector correlation. Rev 27 strategy mechanism adds correlation-bucket cap (max 3 positions per > 0.6 trailing-252-day daily-return correlation bucket, computed by classical-method delegation). Rev 28 extends to post-entry monitoring with a response action that blocks entry into any post-entry-emergent bucket (pair correlation > 0.7) without forcing exits. **[Rev 35: the correlation-bucket count cap and this bucket-based entry block are REMOVED (owner directive); the correlation risk is now monitored-only/informational per Section 6, neither capped nor entry-blocking.]** *Status (entry-decision channel):* **[corrected Rev 8, 2026-08-10, per AR_orc `premortem-D-2026-a3` cycle 7 orchestrator-found priority finding — this line previously reasserted the pre-Rev-35 entry-block immediately after the Rev 35 bracket above stated it was removed]** no longer entry-blocking — per Rev 35 (bracket above) and Section 6's mirrored annotation, correlation-bucket membership at entry-time and in response to post-entry drift is monitored-only/informational; entry decisions are not constrained by correlation-bucket membership. This channel is therefore an accepted, monitored-only residual, not a mitigated one, and joins the during-hold channel below. *Residual (during-hold channel):* already-held positions can drift into high correlation regimes during stress periods, and the strategy does not force exits on already-held positions that experience post-entry correlation drift (forced exits would conflict with D's exit-only-on-thesis-invalidation discipline and would systematically damage multi-year theses on transient regime changes). Cycle 3 T1-α surfaced that correlation-stationarity is a structural property of the cap that cannot be entry-time-resolved; rev 4 closes the entry-decision channel of the gap and acknowledges the during-hold channel as accepted residual. Review trigger: at 24-month gate, audit during-hold correlation drift on all held positions; if average held-pair correlation exceeded 0.7 for ≥ 6 cumulative months, document as a known book-level concentration episode for monthly-review evaluation (informational; no auto-trigger).

14. **Multi-year horizon vs monthly review cadence mismatch.** Most edge-decay signals can't appear in monthly cadence. Section 6 addresses this with portfolio-monitoring monthly indicators + multi-year edge-decay gates. *Status:* accepted property of multi-year strategy in monthly-review experiment framework.

15. **Subtype B single-metric reduction may be operationally unfaithful to multi-driver theses (rev 5 added per cycle 4 Tier 3 finding).** Genuine multi-driver theses (e.g., Costco-pattern flywheel: membership × retention × per-member spend × adjacent expansion) may not reduce to one quantitative trend metric without losing thesis fidelity. The rev 5 typing rule requires articulating a primary driver mechanism (date-arithmetic-determined) but the underlying thesis structure may be irreducibly multi-driver. *Status:* architectural residual. The "primary driver" framing leaves room for naming the dominant quantifiable metric, and Subtype A remains available for catalyst-anchored versions, but flywheel-style theses without a clear single primary driver are excluded from D's universe by mechanism. Review trigger: at 24-month gate, audit whether closed positions show common pattern of "primary driver continued but thesis failed via secondary drivers" — if so, the single-driver framing is materially mismatched to D's actual thesis space and the strategy's universe restriction needs reconsideration.

16. **Beta-estimate-noise finite false-negative rate on alpha-test (rev 5 added per cycle 4 T1-4 acknowledged residual).** Even with the CI-gate operationalization, β̂ standard error of ±0.2 over 24 monthly data points produces ~±4.5pp SE on the alpha estimate at typical SPY return magnitudes. The 95% CI gate sets the false-positive rate at ≤ 5% but accepts a finite false-negative rate on materially-decayed-but-not-statistically-significantly-so cases. The alpha-test is therefore conservative (favors false-negatives over false-positives) — edge decay must be both substantial in magnitude AND statistically significant before the flag fires. The Section 4 indicator portfolio (joint signal across alpha-test, SGOV gap, invalidation count, theme concentration, EV-per-5-closes) is the framework's response to this finite-false-negative property — single-indicator firings are not actionable in isolation. *Status:* architectural residual; not curable by within-strategy revision (requires longer measurement window or different alpha estimator infrastructure beyond current scope). **Effective firing threshold, quantified (added cycle 6 — arithmetic on the SE already stated above; no change to the -3pp threshold, the CI gate, or the estimator itself):** the ±4.5pp SE means the alpha-test's *effective* firing threshold — the point estimate needed before the 95% upper CI bound can clear 0pp — is approximately **1.96 × 4.5pp ≈ -8.8pp**, materially deeper than the headline -3pp figure. Composite published evidence on LLM-driven equity strategies versus a passive equity benchmark (2004-2024, survivorship- and look-ahead-corrected: Buy-and-Hold Sharpe 0.703 vs. best LLM agent Sharpe 0.241, no significant alpha at p>0.34) describes a large real-world underperformance gap that is nonetheless statistically weak. If D's realized alpha tracked a gap of that character, the *modal* outcome — not a tail case — is a point estimate that fails to reach -8.8pp, i.e., inside the alpha-test's non-firing band. This sharpens, rather than newly discovers, the residual already conceded above: at this SE magnitude, the "finite false-negative rate" is not an edge-case property, it is the expected outcome for a strategy performing in line with this evidence. **What the joint-indicator portfolio does and does not compensate for:** Section 4's five-indicator design means a single ambiguous alpha-test reading does not by itself need its own escalation path — SGOV gap, invalidation count, theme concentration, and EV-per-5-closes can each independently corroborate an edge-decay conclusion the alpha-test alone cannot statistically support. It does NOT make the alpha-test's own threshold well-calibrated to the modal null-alpha case — a strategy that is quietly non-performing but produces no invalidation spike, no theme concentration, and a merely-flat (not negative) EV-per-5-closes could see every Section 4 indicator read ambiguous simultaneously. This joint-ambiguity scenario is not further mitigated by this revision; it is recorded here as part of this Known Limitation's architectural residual rather than resolved by it.

17. **Narrative-synthesis architecture's exposure to 5+ Tier 1 disadvantages is no longer magnitude-bounded (rev 19 propagation).** D's edge remains exposed to 2.4, 2.13, 2.15, 2.17, and 2.19. The retired 2% cap and successor per-name envelope no longer mitigate loss magnitude; only the seven-factor justification and mandatory adversarial size attack remain, both judgment-dependent. Post-hoc Section 4 indicators and kill triggers are detectors, not caps. *Status:* architectural residual. Review trigger: continued underperformance against the Section 4 indicator portfolio over multiple 24-month gates requires strategy-mechanism re-evaluation.

18. **Conviction-tier-vs-outcome calibration is undetected, not merely unmitigated (rev 7 added per cycle 6 TIER 1 fix, AR_orc `premortem-D-2026-a3`, 2026-08-05).** The per-thesis risk budget (Rev 43) introduced an ordinal conviction tier recorded at entry, which in principle makes a size-vs-outcome calibration series constructible. Rev 6 claimed this series was "consumed by the Section 4 indicator portfolio" — false; AR_orc independently re-enumerated Section 4's five indicators (beta-adjusted alpha-test, SGOV-underperformance gap, thesis-invalidation count, theme-concentration check, EV-per-5-closes) and Section 6's monitoring items and found none reads conviction tier, size, or outcome jointly. No conviction-tier-vs-outcome indicator exists anywhere in this document; the claim is withdrawn by rev 7, not softened. *Status:* open architectural gap, not closed by this revision — building the indicator is out of scope for an artifact-consistency revision (it would add machinery to Section 4/6, which this route is not permitted to do). Until built, 2.13/2.26 miscalibration on sizing decisions is monitored only through the five existing Section 4 indicators, none of which is conviction-aware — and this compounds Known Limitation 17's residual given that, per rev 19 (`Experiment_Parameters.md`, owner directive 2026-08-05, both CaR envelopes retired; Rev 40's per-name tranche count was never re-capped), the worst-case single-thesis blast radius is now bounded only by the strategy portfolio itself, not by any per-name ceiling and not merely the 5x figure that describes only Rev 43's now-superseded change — the −50% deployed-TWR drawdown kill is the only quantitative backstop, and it is a detector that fires after a loss is realised, not a bound that caps it. ~~Review trigger: before or at the next SL2 revision cycle for this pre-mortem, or at the Section 6 24-month comprehensive review, whichever comes first, evaluate whether a conviction-tier-vs-outcome indicator should be added to Section 4/6.~~

   **[Rev 9, 2026-08-13, per AR_orc `premortem-D-2026-a3` cycle 8 W1 — struck and replaced. Cycle 8 found this trigger had NO functioning firing path on either stated condition: path 1 (the next SL2 revision cycle) was reached at rev 8 and answered off-topic — rev 8 treated the open item as though it required *inventing a numeric threshold*, declared that out of documentary scope, and left it open, when the trigger actually asks for an evaluation *judgment*; and path 2 (the Section 6 24-month bullet) never itemized the evaluation at all, listing only the beta-adjusted alpha-test and the SGOV gap. A trigger with two conditions, neither of which can fire, fails screen (iii). Rev 9 closes this by taking BOTH remedies the review offered rather than either alone.]**

   **THE EVALUATION, PERFORMED (rev 9 — this is the judgment the trigger asked for, answered on its own terms).**
   1. **Is the series constructible today? Yes, and no new recording obligation is needed.** Each D thesis's ordinal conviction tier and its chosen risk budget are both already written into that thesis's `events.decision_log` entry (per the seven-factor sizing justification, factor 7), and realized outcome is recorded at close. KL18's "constructible in principle" is correct, and the gap is precisely and only a **missing consumer** — not missing data, and not a missing threshold. Rev 8's framing of the task as threshold-invention was therefore the wrong question, which is why it produced no answer.
   2. **Should the indicator be promoted into Section 4 now? No — and this is a substantive judgment, not a deferral of convenience.** Section 4 is an edge-decay indicator *portfolio* whose outputs are read jointly ("Multiple Section 4 indicators agreeing → edge issue"; "alpha-test alone no longer primary"), and a joint-signal determination there can terminate the strategy. A conviction-vs-outcome calibration statistic computed over the handful of closes D has produced cannot separate miscalibration from sampling noise; admitting it into that portfolio would let noise contribute to an edge-decay determination. **That is the same defect class this review chain has repeatedly graded Tier 1 elsewhere — an indicator that cannot detect what it nominally exists to detect (screen (iii)) — so adding it now would trade a disclosed gap for a concealed one.**
   3. **Why the timing constraint is structural for D, not an excuse.** D is a long-horizon strategy whose own dated indicators already sit at 12, 24 and 36 months, and whose thesis-invalidation count runs on a rolling 36-month window at a ≥3 threshold. The section's design already assumes closes accumulate slowly. A calibration statistic is strictly more data-hungry than a count, so it cannot be the one indicator that runs on a faster clock than everything around it.
   4. **Verdict: record now, evaluate at the 24-month comprehensive review — as a Section 6 dated review item, NOT a Section 4 indicator.** Section 6 already carries dated evaluation items that inform rather than fire, which is the correct form for a question whose answer depends on an accumulating population. **Rev 9 has wired that hook**: the Section 6 24-month bullet now itemizes this exact computation and re-evaluation explicitly, with two permitted outcomes (promote into Section 4 with a stated response action, or record a written re-deferral in `events.decision_log` naming the population actually available and the next checkpoint) and silence excluded. **No Section 4 indicator, numeric threshold, or response action is created by this revision.**

   **Review trigger (rev 9, single non-reinterpretable path):** at the **Section 6 24-month comprehensive review**, which now itemizes this task by name, perform the computation and re-evaluation described in item 4 above. **Consequence if that gate passes without either permitted outcome being recorded:** KL18 is re-raised as a **Tier 1** item at the next adversarial cycle rather than carried forward again — the carry-forward is what cycle 8 correctly refused to accept a third time. The former "next SL2 revision cycle" path is deliberately NOT retained as a trigger: an SL2 cycle's occurrence is not schedulable from inside this artifact, and cycle 8 demonstrated that the path is reinterpretable by the very revision meant to satisfy it.

The gap of two is inherited, not new — E's text advanced twice outside the adversarial loop before rev 14 (rev 12's documentary correction and rev 13's owner directive, both after cycle 11's verdict) and each has advanced by exactly one since. **Screen glossary and forcing-question gloss, stated in-section (rev 10, closing the cycle-9 Tier 3 sweep item that asked for exactly this or for the framing to be dropped).** The roman-numeral screens are `Experiment_Parameters.md`'s four-screen test, which the adversarial review applies to every pre-mortem: **(i) internal consistency** — the artifact does not contradict itself; **(ii) completeness** — its enumerations are complete; **(iii) deployment risk** — a control the artifact claims exists actually does, on the executed path, what it is claimed to do; **(iv) self-containment** — load-bearing content is reproduced inside the artifact rather than sourced away to a document the strict-blinded reviewer is forbidden to open. "**Forcing question answered (a)**" is the cycle-5 soft cap's decision point: **(a)** another cycle's deployment-risk reduction is worth its cost, so the review continues; **(b)** it is not, so the soft cap fires acceptance and the artifact is taken as it stands. AR_orc answered (a) at cycle 9. The arabic "Screen 1–4" paraphrase that appears in adjacent corpus text is older wording for the same four screens, both nominally EP rev 15; where the two differ in shading, the roman set reproduced here is the one the reviews actually apply, and it is the one this section's screen citations mean.

**(T1-1 — three status clauses survived unstruck in flat contradiction of rev 9's own RESOLUTION; screen (iv) FAILS.)** Rev 9 resolved the EP-rev-18 coverage question in-section — a compensating control *does* govern E's uncapped CaR budget — but struck only one of the four clauses that said otherwise. Rev 10 strikes the remaining three at Known Limitation 18, each with its own bracketed reason: the *"no size-specific control is currently documented in-section"* half-sentence rev 9's strikethrough stopped short of; the *Status:* sentence naming the seven-factor justification as **the only** section-documented sizing discipline (the RESOLUTION names a second); and the *"Escalated, not resolved"* sentence still posing as live the disjunction rev 9 had already answered, including a now-moot "author a genuine E-specific size-adversarial step" branch. **A single controlling status sentence is then added** — coverage RESOLVED, transmission residual live and triggered — explicitly superseding every struck status clause above it, so KL18 states one status no matter which paragraph a reader lands on.

**(T1-2 — the rev-9 review trigger lived in prose no checklist-executing session reads; screen (iii) FAILS.)** Rev 9 wrote KL18 a real trigger and then left it in Section 7, where a session working the Section 6 monthly checklist never encounters it — a trigger that cannot fire is the same defect class rev 9 had just fixed for *having* no trigger. Rev 10 itemizes it as an actual Section 6 bullet and as a named component of the 15-pair gate item, with the three things the review asked for and rev 9 lacked: a **named owner** (M4 — the only monthly routine whose declared read scope includes `strategy/08_pre_mortems.md`, verified against the slice map rather than assumed; M2 is disqualified both by that map and by its own "exclude pairs where either leg is in the open E book" rule), a **stated consequence for the specific flagged pair** (named in M4's cycle decision-log entry and the omission repaired against the live position before the next review), and a **named escalation path** (`ops.sp_raise_alert('warning','M4','premortem_live_gate_defect', …)`, an already-registered category, warning-not-critical so it cannot enter the halt gate).

**(O1 — entry criterion 2 stated no consequence for a negative size-attack verdict; screen (iv) FAILS.)** Orchestrator-found. The annotation closes by disclaiming what it does not do: it adds no gate, threshold or computation, it does not bound how large a budget the attack may clear, and **it is not code-enforced** — nothing reads a size-attack verdict, which is precisely why the KL18 transmission trigger now itemized in Section 6 is the mechanism that makes it auditable at all.

**Tier 2 / Tier 3 sweep items — what rev 10 did and did not take.** **Taken:** the screen/forcing-question gloss (Tier 3, above). **Taken, on new evidence:** the self-containment statement's claim that "full definitions are in the attacker-prompt inline block" is corrected — this run verified it directly against the AR_att routine spec, which grants the attacker only the queue entry plus `artifact_path` and *"EXPLICITLY DO NOT READ … any other repo file"*, and inlines no disadvantage definitions anywhere; the nearest real text (`Experiment_Parameters.md`) states the prompt *should* carry such definitions, which is a requirement on drafting, not a block that exists. **Not taken, with reasons:** "attacked the size" remaining a rubric-free self-graded judgment (Tier 2) — closing it means originating a grading rubric `Experiment_Parameters.md` itself does not supply, which is machinery and outside this documentary route; it remains open and is now at least *auditable* through the Section 6 item. **Note a live grading conflict, recorded rather than resolved:** AR_orc graded the attacker-prompt boilerplate INVALID at `premortem-D-2026-a3` cycle 7 ("deliberate boilerplate split with the attacker-routine prompt, verbatim and correct across all five strategies") and Tier 2 CONFIRMED FICTIONAL at `premortem-C-2026-a3` cycle 13. The clause is repo-wide boilerplate; rev 10 corrects it in E (and this run's sibling rev corrects it in C), and the identical clause survives in the A, B and D sections, which no queue item this run is scoped to touch — referred forward, not swept silently.

**This revision alters no entry criterion, exit rule, threshold, indicator, router rule, hedge-ratio rule, short stop or kill criterion.** Entry criterion 2's own wording is untouched — rev 10 states a consequence that already follows from it and reproduces the governing EP text inline; it does not amend the criterion, and E's live `## Strategy E` Entry criteria in `Strategy.md` are byte-unchanged. No numeric threshold is created or moved; the new Section 6 item defines no indicator and gates no entry. A cumulative audit at the existing 15-pair gate is added alongside the monthly read. The trigger is checkable from records that already exist; it defines no indicator, moves no threshold, and gates no entry. Amending criterion 2's text remains machinery and stays escalated — but it is no longer the precondition for this limitation having any monitoring at all, which is what made "none" untenable.

Rev 4 spot-edit fixes:

- (T1.A) **Pair-prioritization rule added.** When multiple candidate pairs simultaneously meet entry criterion 3's quantitative-divergence-anchor threshold (≥ 95th percentile of trailing-252-day distribution), Claude evaluates them for thesis construction in deterministic rank order: descending by spread-percentile-magnitude (highest percentile evaluated first). Ranking computed by classical-method delegation across the candidate pool. No skip-and-prioritize step permitted — Claude must evaluate the top-ranked candidate before moving to the next; if it fails any criterion 1-6 gate, Claude proceeds to the next-ranked candidate. The rank-order rule eliminates Claude-judgment pair-prioritization, which would otherwise be a 2.4 / 2.24-exposed selection step upstream of all downstream gates. Cycle 3 T1.A surfaced that rev 3's tightened 95th-percentile gate created a higher-stakes admissibility test without addressing the prioritization layer above it; rev 4 closes the gap by rule-binding the prioritization decision.

- (T2.E) **KL #15 review trigger extended to deferral-rate audit.** At 15-pair gate, audit fraction of theses deferred due to Anchor 2 reference-set < 10 qualifying pairs (the deterministic rule's restrictive minimum). If material fraction (e.g., > 30% of evaluated pairs deferred), calibration-refine the minimum-pair threshold or 5-year-joint-history requirement. The audit closes the cycle 3 T2.E gap on uncalibrated declared-frequency-vs-realizable-rate mismatch.

- (T2.F) **Constraint 3 component (b) reframed as post-hoc verification surface.** The quantitative-reconvergence-threshold requirement (entry criterion 1) is principled but its mitigation effect on 2.24 was overstated in rev 3. The threshold-choice itself (5% vs 10% vs 3%) remains session-level Claude reasoning subject to weight-level bias. Rev 4 explicit framing: component (a) (criterion 3 quantitative-divergence-anchor) is the genuine 2.24 mitigation at divergence-existence layer; component (b) (criterion 1 quantitative reconvergence) is a falsifiability surface that aids post-hoc verification (the threshold either fires or doesn't, externally checkable) but does NOT address weight-level bias on threshold choice. This is honest framing — the threshold-choice layer remains a 2.24 residual.

- (T2.J) **Section 1 cross-reference to Constraint 3 universe restriction.** Section 1 instrument eligibility now notes that Constraint 3 component (b) excludes theses without quantitative reconvergence thresholds — a thesis-mechanism qualitativeness exclusion. The strategy's universe is "any 6-digit GICS industry group's L/S pairs WITH falsifiable quantitative reconvergence predictions"; pairs whose reconvergence mechanism is genuinely qualitative (e.g., "S has cultural momentum that will erode as fashion cycles") are excluded. Cross-walk between Section 1 declared universe and Constraint 3 operating universe restored.

T2.G (3x share-price-ratio mechanism-enforcement ceremonial at boundary), T2.H (logging spec cross-strategy schema asymmetry), T2.I (2.18 × 2.13/2.26 interaction not surfaced as compounding failure mode) accepted as documentation residuals per attacker recommendation.
