Anchor 2 is removed (entry criterion 5), so it has no non-stationarity or deferral-rate property left to be a residual of. KL #15 is repurposed below to record Anchor 2's history and removal, per this document's practice of recording a finding rather than erasing it.]**

    **Anchor 2: what it was, and why it is gone (rev 11, owner directive 2026-08-18).** Anchor 2 was a dual-anchor gate requiring financing cost to also clear 15% of a trailing-252-day historical-median pair-mean-reversion magnitude, computed across a reference set of ≥ 10 qualifying same-6-digit-GICS-industry-group pairs (each leg ≥ $10B market cap and ≥ $20M ADV, ≥ 0.5 trailing-252-day correlation, ≥ 5 years joint trading history); fewer than 10 qualifying pairs deferred the thesis entirely with no override. It was introduced at rev 3 (2026-04-25, cycle 2 T2.A) via SL2's documentary-only route (B), which is explicitly forbidden from altering entry criteria. It was never in `Strategy.md`'s Entry criteria, and `strategy_math/strategy_e.py`'s `financing_cost_ok()` has never implemented it — the code has only ever computed the single 15%-of-expected-return test. Despite that, Anchor 2 was enforced as a hard NO-GO gate from rev 3 through rev 10 (2026-04-25 through 2026-08-17). On 2026-08-18 it was one of three independent grounds for declining the TLN/VST thesis, having been found unsatisfiable for the entire GICS 551050 industry group: 9 names in the group, 4 clear the ≥ $10B market-cap / ≥ $20M ADV filter, and the resulting pairing ceiling C(4,2) = 6 falls short of the required 10 — a property of the group's membership, not of TLN or VST's price action. Owner directive, same day, dropped Anchor 2 (`Strategy.md` Rev 45 companion): removing an accretion that was never legitimately adopted into frozen machinery, not weakening a ratified control. **The GICS 551050 arithmetic itself remains true and is worth keeping as a recorded observation** — the group genuinely cannot support 10 qualifying pairs under the stated filters — even though the gate that made this fact binding on entry is gone. *Status:* closed; not a live residual. No review trigger.

16. **2.24 weight-level bias on narrative-threshold-choice layer remains exposed (rev 4 added per cycle 3 T2.F).** Constraint 3 component (b) (entry criterion 1's quantitative-reconvergence-threshold requirement) reduces the narrative's predictive content to a falsifiable quantitative claim, providing post-hoc verification value, but does NOT mitigate 2.24 at the threshold-choice layer where weight-level bias actually operates. Different sessions evaluating the same thesis could converge on similarly-biased threshold choices (e.g., uniformly choosing 5% rather than 3% as the revenue-beat threshold). **[rev 6, 2026-08-03, cycle 5 W8 — dual-cause reframing: a second, distinct cause produces the identical observable signature — memory-mediated cross-session contamination (2.28): a prior session's persisted `reconvergence_thresholds` value in `Decision_Log.md` steers a later session's threshold choice, rather than the later session converging independently on a similarly-biased value. 2.28 is distinct from 2.24 (2.24 is run-to-run weight-level *variance*; 2.28 is a prior session's persisted artifact steering a later session's behaviour). Clustering alone does not distinguish the two.]** *Status:* accepted architectural residual; ~~component (a) of Constraint 3 (criterion 3 quantitative-divergence-anchor) is the genuine 2.24 mitigation at divergence-existence layer,~~ **[rev 15, 2026-08-30, per AR_orc `premortem-E-2026-a3` cycle 12 Tier 1 (v) — STRUCK. This is verbatim the sentence rev 13 struck at Constraint 3 component (a) itself, surviving here in the Known Limitations register, one level removed from the correction. Rev 13 (owner directive) dropped criterion 3's ≥ 95th-percentile requirement as an entry condition, so component (a) is **no longer a 2.24 mitigation at any layer** — the percentile is still computed and recorded, and different sessions still reach the same value for the same spread (the cross-session objectivity property survives, per component (a)'s own rev-13 bracket), but it excludes no candidate pair, so it filters no weight-level-convergent false positive. **Corrected statement:** 2.24 is now unmitigated at the divergence-existence layer as well as at the threshold-choice layer this limitation was written about — which makes this limitation's residual broader than its own framing, not narrower. The threshold-choice exposure described below is unchanged and remains load-bearing-but-not-mitigatable.]** threshold-choice 2.24 exposure is acknowledged as load-bearing-but-not-mitigatable within the strategy's structural constraints. Review trigger: at 15-pair gate, audit whether closed pairs' threshold values (per Decision_Log.md `reconvergence_thresholds` field) cluster at specific values across multiple pairs in similar contexts — if so, document as evidence of threshold-choice convergence (a 2.24 manifestation). **[rev 6, cycle 5 W8 — the audit conclusion is reframed: clustering is evidence of threshold-choice convergence from EITHER 2.24 (weight-level bias) OR 2.28 (memory-mediated anchoring on a prior session's persisted threshold value) — the 15-pair-gate review must weigh both candidate causes rather than assign the finding to 2.24 by construction; the observable signature alone cannot discriminate between them.]**

17. **2.18 × 2.13/2.26 interaction surfaces compounding failure mode (rev 19 propagation).** When 2.13/2.26 inflates expected return, the financing gate clears more permissively; if convergence then takes longer, the six-month exit can crystallize loss. With both envelopes retired, that loss has no numeric per-pair or strategy-level sizing bound. *Status:* accepted residual. At the 15-pair gate, audit the fraction of pairs exited by the time limit; if >30%, document convergence-speed overconfidence and reassess the framing or time cap. **[rev 14, 2026-08-25, per AR_orc `premortem-E-2026-a3` cycle 11 T3-1 — recorded because the mismatch is optical, not real, and a future reader should not have to re-derive that. The >30% bar here and Section 4's "< 50% over rolling 10 closes" indicator bound DIFFERENT quantities: this one is the fraction of pairs closed by the 6-month time cap; Section 4's is the pair convergence rate, whose 50% floor is the lower bound of the 50-70% convergence range the pairs-trading literature reports. They are not two calibrations of one phenomenon and are not in conflict — but they are near-complementary readings of the same book, so a session working one should read the other. Neither figure is changed and no new threshold is derived; had they been genuinely inconsistent, reconciling them would have required originating a calibration, which is machinery and outside this documentary route.]**

18. **No section-level size-specific adversarial control for E's uncapped CaR budget (rev 8 added per cycle 7 AR_orc W3).** The eligibility rule and the Cross-constraint flowing limitation previously named entry criterion 2 as an "entry-criterion-2 adversarial size attack" compensating control for E's now-uncapped per-thesis CaR budget; entry criterion 2's full definition (Section 1) is a narrative adversarial-counter-argument step that never mentions size, CaR, or sizing, and no distinct size-specific adversarial mechanism is defined anywhere else in this section — the only two occurrences of the phrase were these two mislabeled ones, both struck at rev 8. `Experiment_Parameters.md` rev 18 §Position size does define a general "mandatory adversarial attack on the size" as an extension of "every strategy's existing adversarial counter-argument entry criterion," which by that text would appear to include E's entry criterion 2 — but E's own entry-criterion-2 text, in both this pre-mortem and Strategy.md's live Entry criteria, has never been amended to state that extension, so ~~per this pre-mortem's self-containment convention no size-specific control is currently documented in-section, and whether E's live sessions actually apply the EP-level extension in practice is not resolved here.~~ **[rev 9 — struck per AR_orc `premortem-D-2026-a3`-parallel review `premortem-E-2026-a3` cycle 8 W1: this concession is the screen-(iv) failure itself.    **RESOLUTION OF THE EP-REV-18 COVERAGE QUESTION (remedy 1 — resolved in-section, as self-containment requires).** Rev 8 struck the "entry-criterion-2 adversarial size attack" label correctly: entry criterion 2's own text never mentions size, CaR or sizing, so that label misdescribed it. But rev 8 then ran two distinct propositions together — **(a)** E's criterion-2 text does not itself state the size extension, and **(b)** the size extension therefore may not cover E. **(a) is true; (b) does not follow, and is contradicted by the governing text.** `Experiment_Parameters.md` rev 18 §Position size, reproduced here verbatim for self-containment: *"**Mandatory adversarial attack on the size (compensating control).** Every strategy's existing adversarial counter-argument entry criterion is extended: the counter-argument must attack the size as well as the direction — it must state what the position would cost if the thesis is wrong, and argue whether that loss is acceptable."* That sentence is universally quantified and self-executing — "**every** strategy's existing … criterion **is** extended" — not an instruction for each strategy section to opt in by restating it. E has an existing adversarial counter-argument entry criterion (criterion 2, Section 1). **Therefore the extension covers E's entry criterion 2, and E's uncapped per-thesis CaR budget is NOT without a compensating control.** Two independent cross-checks support this reading rather than leaving it as one artifact's assertion: EP's own summary of what survives rev 19 names the mandatory size attack as one of the three surviving disciplines applying across the board ("*What survives, and is now the whole of the sizing discipline*"), and **Strategy C's pre-mortem already reads the same sentence the same way**, stating that "per `Experiment_Parameters.md`'s sizing policy, the existing adversarial counter-argument requirement (entry criterion 3) is extended so the counter-argument must also attack the size." E is not a special case; rev 8 simply never asked whether the extension self-executes. **This resolves the coverage question inside this section. It authors no new control** — it correctly reports the scope of one that already governs.

   **THE RESIDUAL THAT ACTUALLY SURVIVES, stated precisely.** What remains is narrower than rev 8 recorded and is a *transmission* gap, not a coverage gap: because E's own entry-criterion-2 text has never been amended to restate the extension, a live session reading only the `strategy/` E slice — which the blinding architecture actively encourages — may never encounter it. The control governs; whether it reaches the desk on any given entry is the open question. *Status:* accepted, presently-live residual, now with a firing trigger rather than none. Amending E's entry-criterion-2 text to state the extension inline remains a **machinery** change outside SL2's documentary route, and stays escalated — but it is no longer the precondition for this limitation having any monitoring at all, which is what made rev 8's "Review trigger: none" untenable.

   **Review trigger (rev 9 — remedy 2, an explicit interim safeguard wired to an EXISTING checkpoint rather than a new one):** at each monthly review, for every E pair entered since the previous review, confirm from that thesis's `events.decision_log` entry that the counter-argument on record **attacked the size** — i.e. that it stated what the pair would cost if the thesis is wrong and argued whether that loss is acceptable, per the EP rev 18 text reproduced above. Document the number of entries checked and the number carrying a size attack. **Escalate on any miss:** a pair entered without a recorded size attack is direct evidence that the EP-level extension is not reaching E's live sessions in practice — the exact residual named above — and escalates the in-section amendment of entry criterion 2 from a deferred machinery question to an active one. A review at which no new pairs were entered is recorded as such and is not a miss. **Also audit at the 15-pair gate** across all pairs entered to date, so the check has a cumulative reading and not only a monthly one. **[rev 14 DISCLOSURE, 2026-08-25, per AR_orc `premortem-E-2026-a3` cycle 11 T1-3 — the SCOPE of this trigger, stated here as well as in the Section 6 bullet so a reader of either surface alone sees it. The trigger tests **presence, not correctness**: it confirms the record contains a stated cost and an acceptability argument, and cannot distinguish a correctly-graded "not acceptable" from a self-servingly-graded "acceptable." Since entry criterion 2's bar is now unconditional ("there is no advisory reading under which the pair is entered unchanged") and both CaR envelopes are retired, this self-audited grade is the substantive control on E's sizing, and the failure mode it cannot see is precisely the one 2.13/2.26/2.24 predict. Strengthening it into a correctness test requires authoring a grading standard — machinery, outside this documentary route — so the gap is DISCLOSED and stays escalated alongside the criterion-2 transmission residual above. Recorded because rev 10's revision note already conceded this ("a rubric-free self-graded judgment") in prose no executing session reads, which is the same reachability defect KL18 itself exists to fix.]** This trigger is independently checkable from records that already exist; it defines no new indicator, moves no threshold, and gates no entry.

   *(Accuracy note, rev 9: the cycle-8 finding described KL18 as "alone among eighteen Known Limitations in carrying no review trigger." On the literal string "Review trigger:", KL 9 and KL 17 also lack the label — KL 9 states a self-resolving accepted cost, KL 17 states its trigger in the bare "At the 15-pair gate, audit…" form. On substantive content, which is the intended reading, the finding holds exactly: KL18 was the only limitation that affirmatively declared it had **no** review mechanism. That is what rev 9 fixes; KL 9 and KL 17 are left alone as adequately triggered in substance.)*

## Regime scoring (strategy-blind, monthly)

The **M1a** routine — strategy-blind monthly regime scoring. It produces a scored assessment across five regime-condition axes from public macro/policy/earnings inputs, with **no reference to any strategy** (no strategy letter, activation rule, or mechanism). The per-strategy mapping that consumes this scoring — the **M1b** routine, its reconciliation rules that name specific strategies, the output format, and the divergence-review path — lives in `## Regime router` above; M1a is blinded from all of it. This is a **distinct top-level section** so the generated `strategy/` slice for M1a carries the scoring inputs WITHOUT the strategy-naming mapping, making M1a's blinding a hard file boundary rather than a discipline-only rule (see `Claude_Task_Plan.md` "Strategy reading" + `ops/RUNBOOK.md` §9).

*M1a routine (strategy-blind regime scoring).* Fresh routine context. Given the 6 input categories below, produces a scored assessment across five regime condition axes without any reference to strategies A–E, their activation rules, or their mechanisms. No mention of the word "strategy" or any of the five strategy letters in this routine's prompt or input material:
- Growth momentum (accelerating / stable / decelerating)
- Inflation trend (disinflating / stable / reaccelerating)
- Policy stance (dovish / neutral / hawkish)
- Risk sentiment (risk-on / neutral / stressed)
- Shock / overlay (none / latent / acute)

Four of the five axes are scored on judgment against the inputs below. The Shock / overlay axis additionally carries a written grading rubric — see `### Shock / overlay grading rubric` at the end of this section.

M1a's output is the structured regime scoring — the 5 axis assignments with brief rationale for each, citing the inputs that drove the call. M1a writes ONLY this output to the regime-scoring file (`Monthly_Fundamental_RegimeScore.md` per `Claude_Task_Plan.md`). Underlying inputs and M1a's full reasoning chain are not persisted.

**Inputs to M1a (all must be gathered each month, in this order):**

1. **Macro data releases from the prior month:**
   - CPI headline and core (BLS, initial prints — not revisions): latest, 3-month trend, 12-month trend, prior-cycle analogue for current level if one exists
   - PPI headline (BLS, initial): latest, 3-month, 12-month
   - Non-farm payrolls (BLS establishment survey, initial): latest, 3-month, 12-month
   - Unemployment rate (BLS household survey): latest, 3-month, 12-month, prior-cycle trough-to-peak
   - Retail sales (Census Bureau advance): latest, 3-month, 12-month
   - GDP growth rate (BEA — specify which estimate: advance, second, or third): most recent quarterly print if released during the prior month, 4-quarter trend

2. **Fed/FOMC developments:**
   - Any FOMC meeting in prior month: decision, statement changes, dot-plot updates
   - Fed speeches with market-moving content (cited by reference to originating Fed publication or transcript, not news-media summary)
   - Current fed funds futures implied path (CME FedWatch) vs latest dot plot
   - Dot-plot drift vs realized path over past 12 months

3. **Earnings aggregate status:**
   - If in earnings season: percentage of S&P 500 reported, aggregate EPS beat rate, aggregate sales beat rate, aggregate EPS surprise magnitude (source: FactSet Earnings Insight or equivalent named source)
   - Forward S&P 500 EPS consensus: change over prior month and over prior 12 months

4. **Geopolitical events (structured inclusion criteria):**
   - Include only events affecting ≥1 of: global trade flows; oil price move > $5/bbl; sovereign credit spreads; major currency > 2% move against USD.
   - Other events are excluded regardless of narrative salience (per 2.4).

5. **Policy environment:**
   - Regulatory changes affecting broad sectors (with citation to primary-source regulatory release)
   - Tariff and trade developments (with citation)
   - Major legislation passed or imminent (with citation)

6. **Cross-cycle comparison input:**
   - For current values of unemployment, inflation, fed funds, yield curve, breadth, VIX: identify the closest prior-cycle historical analogue (if any) and summarize what happened in the subsequent 6–18 months in that analogue. If no close analogue exists, state so.
   - Honest scope note: this input is contextual information only. It is produced by the same model it is meant to partially de-bias and therefore does NOT constitute a mitigation against 2.14 (recency bias). Treated as a contextual prompt to surface comparison data, not as a de-biasing mechanism.

Each input is gathered through explicit tool calls (web search, data fetch) to the specific named public sources above. No input is recalled from memory — per 2.3 (hallucination) and 2.5 (training cutoff), every input is verified by live retrieval.

**Fallback protocol for unavailable inputs.** If a primary data source is unavailable at routine run time, M1a documents the specific miss in its output and does NOT substitute a secondary source silently. The regime assessment proceeds with the remaining inputs and explicitly flags which inputs were absent. If ≥2 of the 5 primary input categories (macro / Fed / earnings / geopolitical / policy) are absent — input 6 is compensatory and does not count toward this threshold — the fundamental call for the affected month is DO-NOT-ACTIVATE across all strategies regardless of the partial assessment, and M1b is not run that month (M1a writes a fallback-suppression flag to the regime-scoring file; M1b reads that flag and exits without producing strategy mappings).

### Shock / overlay grading rubric

*Added 2026-09-05 (Rev 48, owner directive).* Until this revision the Shock / overlay axis had a token set and no stated criterion for entering or leaving any of its three values — a scoring could raise the axis on judgment, and nothing in this document said when to lower it again. This rubric supplies the missing anchors. It **constrains judgment; it does not replace it**: a scoring that reads the evidence differently may still deviate from the grade the rubric indicates, but must argue against these anchors explicitly in that axis's rationale rather than around them.

**Qualifying shock event.** A dated development that (i) meets input category 4's structured inclusion criteria above — an effect on global trade flows, an oil price move > $5/bbl attributable to the shock channel, a widening in sovereign credit spreads, or a major currency move > 2% against USD — and (ii) is attributable to a named geopolitical or trade channel. Narrative escalation with no measurable leg, undated commentary, and developments failing category 4's criteria are not qualifying events regardless of salience (per 2.4).

**Pre-shock baseline.** The 60-trading-day median of Brent front-month closes ending the session BEFORE the first qualifying event of the current shock structure — or, when fewer than 60 sessions precede that event, the median of the full available history (the series begins 2026-06-01, so a shock structure older than roughly three months has a shorter window by construction rather than by fault), with the actual observation count N recorded in the rationale alongside the triplet so a short baseline is visible rather than implied. Brent closes are read from `state.signal_marks_curated` (`ticker = 'BZUSD'`), one row per trading day off the same daily signal-marks ingest that captures the rest of the mark set — the CURATED view and never the raw `events.signal_marks` table, per the SOURCE TRAP `bigquery/216` pins for every price series (the raw table carries same-day duplicates from two ingest paths, which shift a computed margin), and the surface `state.rerisking_limb_status` computes the same triplet from; a baseline asserted from memory is not a baseline.

**Shock elevation.** The peak Brent close since that first qualifying event, minus the pre-shock baseline. If the peak never exceeded the baseline the elevation is zero and the price leg is treated as satisfied — a price leg that never lifted has nothing to retrace, and the grade then turns on the event-recency and transmission legs alone.

**Grades.**
- `acute` — EITHER a qualifying event dated within the trailing 15 trading days OR (the shock elevation is positive AND Brent is still at or above baseline + 50% of it); AND transmission is ACTIVE, meaning at least one measurable market leg (Brent, sovereign spreads, FX) is still pricing the named channel.
- `latent` — an unresolved shock STRUCTURE remains (a standing blockade, a tariff regime, an unsettled sanctions posture), but there has been no qualifying event in the trailing 15 trading days AND Brent has retraced below baseline + 50% of shock elevation.
- `none` — no unresolved shock structure and no qualifying event in the trailing 60 trading days.

**De-escalation from `acute` to `latent` is REQUIRED, not optional, once both legs hold.** Downgrade when BOTH are true, each dated and measured in the row rationale: (i) zero new qualifying events for 15 consecutive trading days; and (ii) Brent has retraced at least 50% of the shock elevation, i.e. it now sits at or below the baseline-to-peak midpoint. Either leg failing keeps the grade at `acute`. This is the operative exit the axis previously lacked — an `acute` reading that satisfies both legs and is scored `acute` anyway is a deviation, and is subject to the same obligation to argue against the anchors as any other deviation.

**Escalation is immediate, and the asymmetry is deliberate.** Any qualifying event with active transmission moves the axis to `acute` at the next scoring with no dwell requirement, while the downgrade waits on a 15-trading-day quiet window and a measured retracement. Fast to raise and slow to lower is a choice, not an oversight, and it is stated here so that it stays a choice on the record rather than an artifact of an unwritten exit.

**Rationale-recording duty.** Whatever the grade, that axis's rationale MUST record: (1) the date of the most recent qualifying event; (2) the baseline / peak / current Brent triplet; and (3) which legs held and which failed. This is what makes a grade auditable after the fact, and it is what leaves the next scoring — and any out-of-cycle re-scoring — a dated, measured anchor to move off rather than a re-reading of the same narrative.

**Calibration.** The rubric was checked against this axis's own recorded history before being written down, and reproduces it: 2026-07-01 → `latent` (the June spike's driver had reversed within the month, so the transmission leg fails); 2026-08-01 → `acute`; 2026-09-01 → `acute`. A rubric that re-graded its own history differently would be a new rule dressed as a written-down one, and would need to be argued as such.

## Document completion checklist

Before first trade:

- [ ] Adversarial review of each pre-mortem (one per strategy plus the one-time router pre-mortem) via the Attacker + Orchestrator routine pair per `Claude_Task_Plan.md`
- [ ] Revisions to any pre-mortem that fails adversarial review (authored by SL2 for a newcomer)
- [ ] Binding graduation verdict from AR_orc — the former "third-session adjudication if the first two disagree" step is superseded (rev 2026-07-10, Strategy Arsenal autonomy conversion, owner directive) by additional Attacker + Orchestrator cycles to the soft cap plus AR_orc's binding verdict and the bigquery/11 theater-judge
- [ ] Each strategy's pre-mortem completes its full lifecycle (original + adversarial + revision + binding verdict) before THAT strategy's first PROBE trade — scoped per-strategy at its own entry, not to a founding N+1 cohort before one shared first trade (N = current `state.strategy_roster` size)
- [ ] External ledger set up to track strategy portfolios independently
- [ ] Classical-method code delegation scaffolding operational for options math (C), correlation and beta computation (E), and sector/concentration checks (A, B, D)
- [ ] First monthly fundamental analysis completed; initial router states established

Once a strategy's spec locks at SHADOW entry (`spec_locked_since`), every threshold and template of THAT strategy is immutable for its life per `Experiment_Parameters.md` (two-tier immutability — see the Immutability clause near the top of this document; rev 2026-07-10, Strategy Arsenal autonomy conversion, owner directive). Roster membership and N remain versioned policy, revised only through the Strategy Arsenal Lifecycle.
